9366 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, the speakers of the Anṣār stood to address the people.
Some of them said, “Emigrants, whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ appointed a man from among you, he would place a man from among us beside him. We therefore believe this authority should be held by two men: one from you and one from us.”
The speakers of the Anṣār continued repeating this proposal.
Zayd ibn Thābit stood and said, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was from the Emigrants, so the leader should be from the Emigrants. We shall be his supporters just as we were the supporters of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Abū Bakr stood and said, “May Allah reward you with goodness as a tribe, people of the Anṣār, and may He make your speaker steadfast.”
He then said, “By Allah, had you done otherwise, we would not have reconciled with you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (21617), al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (5/122), and al-Ḥākim (3/76), through ʿAffān ibn Muslim, from Wuhayb ibn Khālid, from Dāwūd ibn Abī Hind, from Abū Naḍrah, from Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī.

9367 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, the Anṣār said, “There should be one ruler from us and one ruler from you.”
ʿUmar came to them and said, “People of the Anṣār, do you not know that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ commanded Abū Bakr to lead the people in prayer? Which of you would willingly place himself ahead of Abū Bakr?”
The Anṣār replied, “We seek refuge with Allah from placing ourselves ahead of Abū Bakr.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (133), through Muʿāwiyah ibn ʿAmr, from Zāʾidah, from ʿĀṣim; and through Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī, from Zāʾidah, from ʿĀṣim, from Zirr, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd.

9368 — Sālim ibn ʿUbayd reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ became ill and lost consciousness. When he regained consciousness, he asked:
“Has the time for prayer arrived?”
They replied, “Yes.”
He said: “Tell Bilāl to call the prayer, and tell Abū Bakr to lead the people.”
He then lost consciousness again. When he regained consciousness, he asked:
“Has the time for prayer arrived?”
They replied, “Yes.”
He said: “Tell Bilāl to call the prayer, and tell Abū Bakr to lead the people.”
He then lost consciousness again.
ʿĀʾishah said, “My father is a deeply tenderhearted man. If only you would appoint someone else.”
When the Prophet ﷺ regained consciousness, he asked:
“Has the prayer commenced?”
They replied, “No.”
He said:
“Tell Bilāl to announce its commencement, and tell Abū Bakr to lead the people.”
ʿĀʾishah said, “My father is a tenderhearted—or deeply sorrowful—man. If only you would appoint someone else.”
He replied:
“You women are indeed like the women associated with Joseph! Tell Bilāl to call the prayer, and tell Abū Bakr to lead the people.”
Bilāl announced the prayer’s commencement, and Abū Bakr stepped forward.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ later regained consciousness and said:
“Find people upon whom I can lean.”
He came out leaning upon Barīrah and another person until he sat beside Abū Bakr.
Abū Bakr intended to step backward, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prevented him.
Abū Bakr continued leading the people in prayer.
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, ʿUmar said, “If I hear anyone say that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has died, I shall strike him with my sword.”
Sālim ibn ʿUbayd continued: They sent me, saying, “Go to the companion of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and summon him.”
I went to Abū Bakr while he was in the mosque, and I was overwhelmed with shock.
Abū Bakr asked me, “Has the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died?”
I replied, “ʿUmar says, ‘If I hear anyone say that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has died, I shall strike him with my sword.’”
Abū Bakr stood and took hold of my forearm.
He and I arrived together, and he said, “Make room for me.”
They made room for him.
He bent over the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, touched him, placed his hands—or one hand—upon him, and recited:
“Indeed, you will die, and they too will die.”
They asked, “Companion of the Messenger of Allah, has the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died?”
He replied, “Yes.”
They then knew that the matter was as he had said.
They were an unlettered people, and no prophet had previously lived among them.
They asked, “Companion of the Messenger of Allah, should we perform the funeral prayer over him?”
He replied, “Yes.”
They asked, “How should we pray over him?”
He replied, “Groups should enter one after another, pronounce the takbīr, pray, and supplicate. They should then leave, and another group should enter, until everyone has finished.”
They asked, “Companion of the Messenger of Allah, should he be buried?”
He replied, “Yes.”
They asked, “Where should he be buried?”
He replied, “In the place where Allah took his soul, for Allah takes a prophet’s soul only in a pure place.”
They knew that the matter was as he had said.
Abū Bakr then went out and instructed that the members of the Prophet’s ﷺ paternal family wash his body.
The Emigrants assembled and consulted one another.
They said, “The Anṣār have a rightful role in this matter.”
They went to them, and one of the Anṣār said, “There should be one ruler from us and one ruler from the Emigrants.”
ʿUmar stood and asked them, “Who possesses three distinctions like those of Abū Bakr?
“‘The second of two when they were in the cave’—who were those two?
“‘When he said to his companion, “Do not grieve; indeed, Allah is with us”’—who were those two?
“With whom was Allah Almighty?”
He then took Abū Bakr’s hand and pledged allegiance to him. The people also pledged allegiance, and it was a good and honorable pledge.
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd (365), whose wording this is, al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (379), Ibn Mājah (1234), al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (7081), and Ibn Khuzaymah (1541), through ʿAbdullāh ibn Dāwūd, from Salamah ibn Nubayṭ, from Nuʿaym ibn Abī Hind, from Nubayṭ ibn Sharīṭ, from Sālim ibn ʿUbayd, who was a Companion.

9369 — ʿĀʾishah said: When they intended to wash the Prophet ﷺ, they said, “By Allah, we do not know whether we should remove the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ clothes as we do with our deceased, or wash him while his clothes remain on him.” When they differed over this, Allah caused sleep to overcome them until there was not a man among us except that his chin rested upon his chest. Then someone spoke to them from a corner of the house, though they did not know who he was, saying, “Wash the Prophet ﷺ while his clothes remain on him.” They then went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and washed him while his shirt remained on him, pouring water over the shirt and rubbing him with it rather than with their bare hands. ʿĀʾishah used to say, “Had I known beforehand what I came to know afterward, none but his wives would have washed him.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3141), Ibn Mājah (1464), Aḥmad (26306), Ibn Ḥibbān (6627), and al-Ḥākim (3/59–60), all through Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, from Yaḥyā ibn ʿAbbād, from his father ʿAbbād ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr, who said: I heard ʿĀʾishah relate it. This wording is Abū Dāwūd’s. Ibn Mājah limited his version to ʿĀʾishah’s statement: “Had I known beforehand what I came to know afterward…”

9370 — Ibn ʿAbbās said: When the people resolved to wash the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, no one was in the house except members of his family: his uncle al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, al-Faḍl ibn al-ʿAbbās, Qutham ibn al-ʿAbbās, Usāmah ibn Zayd ibn Ḥārithah, and his freedman Ṣāliḥ. When they assembled to wash him, Aws ibn Khawli al-Anṣārī—one of Banū ʿAwf ibn al-Khazraj who had participated at Badr—called to ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib from behind the door, saying, “O ʿAlī, I adjure you by Allah, and remind you of our share in the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” ʿAlī said to him, “Enter.” He entered and attended the washing of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, although he took no part in washing him.
ʿAlī supported him against his chest while his shirt remained on him. Al-ʿAbbās, al-Faḍl, and Qutham helped ʿAlī turn him, while Usāmah ibn Zayd and his freedman Ṣāliḥ poured the water. ʿAlī washed him and saw none of the things ordinarily seen in a deceased person. He kept saying, “May my father and mother be sacrificed for you! How pure and pleasant you are, both alive and dead!”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2357) through Yaʿqūb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, from Ḥusayn ibn ʿAbdullāh, from ʿIkrimah, from Ibn ʿAbbās.

9371 — ʿĀʾishah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was shrouded in three white garments from Saḥūl, containing neither a shirt nor a turban.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (5), al-Bukhārī (1271, 1273), and Muslim (941:46).

9372 — ʿĀʾishah said: I entered upon Abū Bakr, and he asked, “In how many garments did you shroud the Prophet ﷺ?” She replied, “In three white garments from Saḥūl, containing neither a shirt nor a turban.” He then asked her, “On what day did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ die?” She replied, “Monday.” He asked, “What day is today?” She replied, “Monday.” He said, “I hope that I will die sometime between now and tonight.”
He looked at a garment he was wearing during his illness, which bore a stain of saffron, and said, “Wash this garment of mine, add two other garments to it, and shroud me in them.” I said, “But this is worn out.” He replied, “The living have a greater right to new clothes than the dead. It is only destined for the bodily discharge.” He did not die until Tuesday night had begun, and he was buried before morning.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1387).
Regarding the expression “for the bodily discharge,” ʿIyāḍ said that the first letter of the Arabic word was variously narrated with ḍammah, fatḥah, and kasrah. Ibn Ḥabīb said: With kasrah, it means pus or bodily discharge; with fatḥah, it means delay; and with ḍammah, it means the sediment of oil. What is intended here is bodily discharge.

9373 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was shrouded in three white garments from Saḥūl.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (1470).

9374 — Abū ʿAsīb—or Abū ʿAsīm—reported, according to Bahz, that he witnessed the funeral prayer over the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. They asked, “How should we pray over him?” He replied, “Enter in separate groups, one group after another.” They would enter through one door, perform the funeral prayer over him, and then leave through the other door.
When he ﷺ was placed in his grave niche, al-Mughīrah said, “Something near his feet has been left unfinished.” They said, “Then enter and put it right.” He entered, reached in with his hand, and touched the Prophet’s feet. He then said, “Pour the earth over me.” They poured earth until it reached halfway up his shins, after which he came out. He thereafter used to say, “I am the last of you to have had contact with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (20766) through Bahz and Abū Kāmil, both from Ḥammād ibn Salamah, from Abū ʿImrān al-Jawnī, from Abū ʿAsīb or Abū ʿAsīm.

9375 — Sālim ibn ʿUbayd, who was one of the People of the Ṣuffah, said: Abū Bakr entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ after he had died and then came out. He was asked, “Has the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died?” He replied, “Yes,” and they knew that the matter was as he said.
He was then asked, “Should the funeral prayer be performed over him, and how should it be performed?” He replied, “They should come in groups, one group after another, and pray over him.” They knew that the matter was as he said. They asked, “Should he be buried, and where?” He replied, “In the place where Allah took his soul, for Allah takes a prophet’s soul only in a pure place.” They knew that the matter was as he said.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī (4/30) through Yūnus ibn Bukayr, from Salamah ibn Nubayṭ, from his father Nubayṭ ibn Sharīṭ al-Ashjaʿī, from Sālim ibn ʿUbayd.

9376 — Anas said: When the Prophet ﷺ died, there were two gravediggers in Madinah: one made niche graves, while the other made trench graves. They said, “We shall seek our Lord’s choice and send for both of them; whichever arrives first, we shall employ him.” They sent for them, and the man who made niche graves arrived first, so they made a niche grave for the Prophet ﷺ.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (1557) through Maḥmūd ibn Ghaylān, from Hāshim ibn al-Qāsim, from Mubārak ibn Faḍālah, from Ḥumayd al-Ṭawīl, from Anas ibn Mālik.

9377 — ʿĀmir ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ reported that Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ said during the illness in which he died: “Make a niche grave for me and set unbaked bricks over me in rows, just as was done for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Funerals (966) through Yaḥyā ibn Yaḥyā, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Jaʿfar al-Miswarī, from Ismāʿīl ibn Muḥammad ibn Saʿd, from ʿĀmir ibn Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ.

9378 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib said: I washed the Prophet ﷺ and looked for what is ordinarily seen in a deceased person, but I saw nothing. He ﷺ was pure and pleasant both alive and dead. Four persons, to the exclusion of the rest of the people, undertook his burial and concealment in the grave: ʿAlī, al-ʿAbbās, al-Faḍl, and Ṣāliḥ, the freedman of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. A niche was made for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and unbaked bricks were set over him in rows.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ḥākim (1/362), and through him al-Bayhaqī (3/388), by way of Musaddad, from ʿAbd al-Wāḥid ibn Ziyād, from Maʿmar, from al-Zuhrī, from Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib, from ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

9379 — ʿĀʾishah said: There were two gravediggers in Madinah: one made niche graves and the other made trench graves. They waited to see which of the two would come, and the one who made niche graves arrived. He therefore made a niche grave for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Saʿd (2/295) through Yazīd ibn Hārūn and Hishām Abū al-Walīd al-Ṭayālisī, from Ḥammād ibn Salamah, from Hishām ibn ʿUrwah, from his father, from ʿĀʾishah.

9380 — Ibn ʿAbbās said: A red velvet cloth was placed in the Prophet’s ﷺ grave.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Funerals (967) through several routes from Shuʿbah, from Abū Jamrah, from Ibn ʿAbbās.

9381 — ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, said: We did not know that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had been buried until we heard the sound of the spades in the middle of the night preceding Wednesday.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (26394) through Yaʿqūb, from his father, from Ibn Isḥāq, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm, from his wife Fāṭimah bint Muḥammad ibn ʿUmārah, from ʿAmrah bint ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Saʿd ibn Zurārah, from ʿĀʾishah.
The Arabic word masāḥī is the plural of misḥāh, an iron spade used to level the earth.
This is the correct view: the Prophet ﷺ died on Monday before midday and was buried during the night preceding Wednesday. This is the position of the majority of the scholars of prophetic biography and history; contrary reports are anomalous.

9382 — ʿĀʾishah said: During his illness, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would repeatedly ask, “Where am I today? Where will I be tomorrow?” eagerly awaiting ʿĀʾishah’s day. When my day came, Allah took his soul while he was resting between my upper chest and neck, and he was buried in my house.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1389) and Muslim (2443:84); this is al-Bukhārī’s wording. Muslim’s wording says, “He would keep asking.”
Her expression “between my saḥr and my neck” refers to the lungs and what is connected to them. She meant that he died while leaning against her chest, between her torso and neck.

9383 — ʿUrwah reported that ʿĀʾishah said to ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr: “Bury me alongside my fellow wives, and do not bury me in the house alongside the Prophet ﷺ, for I dislike being praised and declared especially virtuous on that account.”
ʿUrwah also said: ʿUmar sent a request to ʿĀʾishah, saying, “Permit me to be buried alongside my two companions.” She replied, “Yes, by Allah.” Whenever another man from among the Companions had previously sent her such a request, she would say, “No, by Allah! I will never prefer anyone over them.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7327).

9384 — ʿĀʾishah said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, they differed concerning where he should be buried. Abū Bakr said, “I heard something from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that I have never forgotten. He said: ‘Allah never takes the soul of a prophet except at the place where He wishes him to be buried.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (1018) and al-Shamāʾil (372), al-Bazzār in his Musnad (60), and Abū Yaʿlā (1/46).
The Prophet ﷺ will not emerge from his grave before the Day of Resurrection, nor does he make contact with anyone among the people or attend gatherings celebrating his birth. Rather, he remains in his grave until the Day of Resurrection, while his soul is in the highest heights with his Lord in the Abode of Honor. Allah, Glorified and Exalted, says:
“Then, on the Day of Resurrection, you will surely be raised.”
 [Al-Muʾminūn: 16]
The Prophet ﷺ also said:
“I will be the leader of the children of Adam on the Day of Resurrection, the first whose grave will be opened, the first intercessor, and the first whose intercession will be accepted.”
Narrated by Muslim (2278).
This verse, this noble hadith, and the other verses and hadiths conveying the same meaning all indicate that the Prophet ﷺ and the rest of the dead will emerge from their graves only on the Day of Resurrection.
Religious Innovations and Their Harmful Effects, by His Eminence Shaykh Ibn Bāz (pp. 15–16).

9385 — ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith—the brother of Juwayriyyah bint al-Ḥārith and thus the Prophet’s ﷺ brother-in-law—said: At his death, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ left neither a dirham nor a dinar, neither a male nor a female slave, nor anything else, except his white mule, his weapons, and some land that he had designated as charity.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2739).

9386 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “My heirs shall not divide a single dinar among themselves. Whatever I leave after the maintenance of my wives and the expenses of my administrator is charity.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in The Book of Speech, Backbiting, and Godfearingness (28), al-Bukhārī (6729), and Muslim (1760:55), both through Mālik. Their wording contains “a single dinar.”
That is, whatever remains after the maintenance of his wives and the expenses of his administrator is charity for the poor and needy. In light of this should be understood his statement ﷺ in the hadith of ʿĀʾishah and others: “We are not inherited from; whatever we leave is charity.” This means after providing for his family and the expenses of his administrator, not before, as indicated by the preceding hadith of Abū Hurayrah, because obligatory charity is not lawful for the family of Muḥammad ﷺ.

9387 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ left neither a dinar nor a dirham, neither a sheep nor a camel, and he made no personal bequest.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1635).

9388 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported that the Prophet ﷺ turned toward Mount Uḥud and said: “By Him in whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, I would not be pleased if Uḥud were transformed into gold for the family of Muḥammad and I spent it in the cause of Allah, yet on the day I died I left behind even two dinars of it—except two dinars that I had set aside to repay a debt, if there were one.”
He died leaving neither a dinar nor a dirham, neither a male nor a female slave. He left his armor pledged to a Jewish man in return for thirty sāʿs of barley.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (2724), Abū Yaʿlā (2684), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (3682), and ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd (598).

9389 — Abū Dharr reported that the Prophet ﷺ said: “I would not be pleased to possess the equivalent of Mount Uḥud in gold and then, on the day I died, have even a dinar or half a dinar of it remaining with me, unless I had reserved it for a creditor.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (21322, 21329, 21425, 21570), al-Ṭayālisī (467), and al-Dārimī (2809).

9390 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, said: The Prophet’s ﷺ wives sent ʿUthmān to Abū Bakr to ask him for their one-eighth share of what Allah had granted His Messenger ﷺ as spoils. I was the one who dissuaded them, saying, “Will you not fear Allah? Do you not know that the Prophet ﷺ used to say—referring to himself—‘We are not inherited from; whatever we leave is charity. The family of Muḥammad ﷺ may only receive their maintenance from this property’?” The Prophet’s ﷺ wives then desisted when I informed them of this.
This charity later came under ʿAlī’s administration. ʿAlī denied al-ʿAbbās control of it and prevailed over him concerning it. It then came under the administration of al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī, followed by al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī, and then ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn and al-Ḥasan ibn al-Ḥasan, who administered it in turn. It later came under Zayd ibn al-Ḥasan’s administration. It was truly the charitable endowment of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4034) and Muslim (1758:51); the wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

9391 — ʿĀʾishah reported that Fāṭimah and al-ʿAbbās came to Abū Bakr seeking their inheritance: the Prophet’s land at Fadak and his share of Khaybar. Abū Bakr said: “I heard the Prophet ﷺ say: ‘We are not inherited from; whatever we leave is charity. The family of Muḥammad may only receive their maintenance from this property.’ By Allah, maintaining ties with the relatives of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ is dearer to me than maintaining ties with my own relatives.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4035, 4036) and Muslim (1759:53); the wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

9392 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered upon me while I was ill and unconscious. He performed ablution, and some of the water he had used for ablution was poured over me, whereupon I regained consciousness.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (194) and Muslim (1616:8).

9393 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Prophet ﷺ used to enter the house of Umm Sulaym and sleep upon her bed while she was away. One day he came and slept on her bed. Someone went to her and said, “The Prophet ﷺ is sleeping in your house upon your bed.” She came and found that he had perspired and that his perspiration had collected upon a piece of leather on the bed. She opened her small container and began absorbing the perspiration and squeezing it into her bottles. The Prophet ﷺ awoke with a start and asked, “What are you doing, Umm Sulaym?” She replied, “O Messenger of Allah, we hope for its blessing for our children.” He said, “You have done well.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2331:84).

9394 — Thumāmah reported from Anas that Umm Sulaym used to spread a leather mat for the Prophet ﷺ, upon which he would take his midday rest in her home. When the Prophet ﷺ slept, she would collect some of his perspiration and hair in a bottle and then mix it into sukk. When Anas ibn Mālik was near death, he instructed that some of that sukk be placed in the perfume used for his burial preparation, and it was placed in it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6281).
The word “niṭʿ” means a mat made of leather.
The expression “he would take his midday rest” means that he would sleep around noon.
“Sukk” is a type of perfume.
“His burial perfume” means the blended perfume specifically applied to a deceased person.

9395 — Al-Sāʾib ibn Yazīd said: My maternal aunt took me to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “O Messenger of Allah, my sister’s son is ill.” He stroked my head and prayed that I be blessed. He then performed ablution, and I drank from the water he had used. I stood behind him and looked at the Seal of Prophethood between his shoulder blades; it resembled the button of a bridal canopy.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3541) and Muslim (2345).

9396 — Abū Ayyūb reported that the Prophet ﷺ stayed in his home. He then related the report, saying that he used to prepare food for the Prophet ﷺ. When the dish was returned to him, he would ask where the Prophet’s fingers had touched it and would eat from those places.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2053:171).

9397 — Abū Mūsā said: I was with the Prophet ﷺ while he was staying at al-Jiʿrānah between Makkah and Madinah, and Bilāl was with him. A Bedouin came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “Muḥammad, will you not fulfill what you promised me?” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him, “Receive glad tidings.” The Bedouin replied, “You have said ‘Receive glad tidings’ to me too many times.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then turned to Abū Mūsā and Bilāl with the appearance of someone displeased and said, “This man has rejected the glad tidings, so you two accept them.” They replied, “We accept them, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then called for a vessel containing water. He washed his hands and face in it and rinsed his mouth into it. He then said, “Drink from it, pour it over your faces and chests, and receive glad tidings.” They took the vessel and did as the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had instructed them. Umm Salamah called to them from behind the curtain, “Leave some of what is in your vessel for your mother.” They therefore left a portion of it for her.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4328) and Muslim (2497).

9398 — ʿUrwah ibn Masʿūd al-Thaqafī said: By Allah, whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ expectorated, it would fall into the palm of one of them, who would then rub it over his face and skin. Whenever he commanded them, they hastened to carry out his command; and whenever he performed ablution, they would nearly fight one another over the water he had used.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2731–2732).

9399 — ʿAwn ibn Abī Juḥayfah reported that his father saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in a red leather tent. He said: I saw Bilāl bring out the water the Prophet ﷺ had used for ablution, and I saw the people rushing to obtain it. Whoever obtained some of it rubbed himself with it, and whoever obtained none took moisture from the hand of his companion.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (376) and Muslim (503:250).

9400 — Abū Burdah said: I came to Madinah and met ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām. He said to me, “Come with me to my home, and I will give you a drink in a cup from which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ drank, and you may pray in a place of prayer in which the Prophet ﷺ prayed.” I went with him, and he gave me a drink of sawīq, fed me dates, and I prayed in his place of prayer.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7342).

9401 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: After the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had thrown pebbles at the Jamrah, slaughtered his sacrificial animal, and was ready to shave his head, he presented his right side to the barber, who shaved it. He then summoned Abū Ṭalḥah al-Anṣārī and gave the hair to him. Next, he presented his left side to the barber and said, “Shave it.” After it was shaved, he gave the hair to Abū Ṭalḥah and said, “Distribute it among the people.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1305:326).

9402 — ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn Mawhab said: My family sent me to Umm Salamah with a cup of water in which some of the Prophet’s ﷺ hair had been placed. Whenever a person was afflicted by the evil eye or some other ailment, a vessel would be sent to her. I looked into a small container and saw some reddish hairs.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5896).

9403 — Sahl ibn Saʿd reported: The Prophet ﷺ came and sat with his Companions in the shaded meeting place of Banū Sāʿidah. He then said, “Give us something to drink, Sahl.” So I brought out this cup and served them from it.
Sahl later brought that same cup out for us, and we drank from it. Afterward, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz asked Sahl to give it to him, and Sahl presented it to him as a gift.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5637) and Muslim (2007).

9404 — Sahl ibn Saʿd reported: A woman came to the Prophet ﷺ carrying a cloak. Sahl asked the people, “Do you know what a burdah is?” They replied, “It is a mantle.” Sahl explained, “It is a woven mantle with a border.”
The woman said, “O Messenger of Allah, I have brought this for you to wear.” The Prophet ﷺ accepted it because he needed it and put it on. A man among the Companions saw him wearing it and said, “O Messenger of Allah, how beautiful this is! Give it to me to wear.” He replied, “Yes.”
After the Prophet ﷺ had left, the man’s companions reproached him, saying, “You did not do well. You saw that the Prophet ﷺ accepted it because he needed it, yet you asked him for it, knowing that he never refuses anyone who asks him for something.” The man replied, “I hoped to obtain its blessing after the Prophet ﷺ had worn it, so that perhaps I might be shrouded in it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6036).

9405 — Asmāʾ bint Abī Bakr said: This is the robe of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. It remained with ʿĀʾishah until she died, and when she died, I took possession of it. The Prophet ﷺ used to wear it, and we wash it in water for the sick, seeking healing through it.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2069).

9406 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: The Prophet ﷺ would visit all his women within a single period of the night or day, and they numbered eleven. Qatādah said: I asked Anas, “Was he capable of doing that?” He replied, “We used to say that he had been given the strength of thirty men.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Ghusl (268) through Muḥammad ibn Bashshār, from Muʿādh ibn Hishām, from his father, from Qatādah, from Anas ibn Mālik.

9407 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ would visit all his wives in a single night, and at that time he had nine wives.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Ghusl (284) through ʿAbd al-Aʿlā ibn Ḥammād, from Yazīd ibn Zurayʿ, from Saʿīd, from Qatādah, from Anas ibn Mālik.

9408 — ʿĀʾishah said: The Prophet ﷺ did not marry another woman while Khadījah was alive, until she died.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2436).

9409 — ʿĀʾishah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “You were shown to me in a dream for three nights. The angel brought you to me wrapped in a piece of silk and said, ‘This is your wife.’ I uncovered your face and saw that it was you. I then said, ‘If this is from Allah, He will bring it to pass.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5078) and Muslim (2438); the wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

9410 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, reported that the Prophet ﷺ contracted marriage with her when she was seven years old, and she was brought to live with him as his bride when she was nine. Her dolls remained with her, and he died when she was eighteen years old.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1422:71).

9411 — ʿĀʾishah said: I never saw a woman in whose place I would have preferred to be more than Sawdah bint Zamʿah, despite the sharpness in her temperament. When she grew old, she gave her allotted day with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to ʿĀʾishah. She said, “O Messenger of Allah, I have given my day with you to ʿĀʾishah.” The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would therefore allot two days to ʿĀʾishah: her own day and Sawdah’s day.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (1463:47) and al-Bukhārī (5212).

9412 — Sālim ibn ʿAbdullāh reported that he heard ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar, may Allah be pleased with them both, relate that when Ḥafṣah bint ʿUmar became a widow after the death of Khunays ibn Ḥudhāfah al-Sahmī—one of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ who died in Madinah—ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said:
“I went to ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān and offered Ḥafṣah to him in marriage. He replied, ‘I will consider the matter.’ Several nights passed, and then he met me and said, ‘I have decided not to marry at this time.’
“I then met Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq and said, ‘If you wish, I will marry Ḥafṣah bint ʿUmar to you.’ Abū Bakr remained silent and gave me no reply. I was more upset with him than I had been with ʿUthmān.
“Several nights passed, and then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ proposed marriage to her, so I gave her to him in marriage. Later, Abū Bakr met me and said, ‘Perhaps you were upset with me when you offered Ḥafṣah to me and I gave you no reply?’
“I said, ‘Yes.’
“Abū Bakr said, ‘Nothing prevented me from responding to your offer except that I knew the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had mentioned her, and I would never disclose the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ secret. Had the Messenger of Allah ﷺ decided not to marry her, I would have accepted her.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5122).
Khunays ibn Ḥudhāfah al-Sahmī was wounded at Uḥud and later died, leaving Ḥafṣah a widow.
Ḥafṣah died in the year 41 AH, though it has also been said that she died in 45 AH. See: Al-Iṣābah (11180).

9413 — Umm Salamah reported that she heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“No Muslim is afflicted by a calamity and then says what Allah has commanded: ‘Indeed, we belong to Allah, and to Him we shall return. O Allah, reward me for my affliction and replace it for me with something better,’ except that Allah replaces it for him with something better.”
She said: When Abū Salamah died, I thought, “Which Muslim could be better than Abū Salamah, the head of the first household to migrate to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?” Nevertheless, I recited the supplication, and Allah replaced him for me with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
She continued: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah to propose marriage to me on his behalf. I said, “I have a daughter, and I am a woman who experiences jealousy.” He ﷺ replied, “As for her daughter, we shall pray to Allah to make her independent of her; and I shall pray to Allah to remove her jealousy.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (918:3).

9414 — ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ distributed the captives of Banū al-Muṣṭaliq, Juwayriyyah bint al-Ḥārith fell to the share of Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās—or one of his cousins—and she entered into a contract to purchase her freedom from him. She was a beautiful and attractive woman whom no one could see without being captivated by her. She came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ seeking his assistance in paying her emancipation contract.
ʿĀʾishah said: By Allah, as soon as I saw her at the door of my chamber, I disliked her presence, because I knew that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would see in her what I had seen.
Juwayriyyah entered and said, “O Messenger of Allah, I am Juwayriyyah, daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Abī Ḍirār, the chief of his people. You are well aware of the affliction that has befallen me. I fell to the share of Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās—or one of his cousins—and entered into a contract to purchase my freedom. I have come to ask your assistance in paying it.”
He ﷺ said, “Would you like something better than that?”
She asked, “What is it, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied, “I will pay your emancipation contract and marry you.”
She said, “Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He ﷺ said, “I have done so.”
The news then spread among the people that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had married Juwayriyyah bint al-Ḥārith. The people said, “They are now the in-laws of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ!” They consequently released the captives in their possession.
ʿĀʾishah said: One hundred households of Banū al-Muṣṭaliq were set free as a result of his marriage to her. I do not know of any woman who brought greater blessing to her people than she did.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3931) and Aḥmad (26265), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4054) and al-Ḥākim (4/26), all through Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq, from Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar ibn al-Zubayr, from ʿUrwah ibn al-Zubayr, from ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers. This wording is Aḥmad’s.
Juwayriyyah’s original name was Barrah, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ renamed her Juwayriyyah. According to the correct view, she died in the year 50 AH.

9415 — Anas reported: Zayd ibn Ḥārithah came complaining about his marriage, and the Prophet ﷺ repeatedly said to him, “Fear Allah and keep your wife.”
Anas said: Had the Messenger of Allah ﷺ been one to conceal any part of the revelation, he would have concealed this verse.
Zaynab used to express her distinction over the other wives of the Prophet ﷺ by saying, “Your families arranged your marriages, but Allah Most High gave me in marriage from above the seven heavens.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7420).
Zaynab bint Jaḥsh was the daughter of the Prophet’s ﷺ paternal aunt; her mother was Umaymah bint ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. She married Zayd ibn Ḥārithah, the beloved of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and remained with him for approximately a year or slightly longer. Their marital relationship then deteriorated, and in the fifth year after the Hijrah, Allah gave her in marriage to His Prophet ﷺ from above the seven heavens. This abolished the pre-Islamic custom of adoption under which a man was forbidden from marrying the former wife of his adopted son.
She, may Allah be pleased with her, died in the year 20 AH and was the first of the Prophet’s ﷺ wives to join him after his death.

9416 — Umm Ḥabībah had been married to ʿUbaydullāh ibn Jaḥsh, who died in Abyssinia. The Negus then married her to the Prophet ﷺ and, on his behalf, gave her a dowry of four thousand dirhams. He sent her to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the company of Shuraḥbīl ibn Ḥasanah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not send her anything toward the dowry. The dowries of his other wives were four hundred dirhams.
Abū Dāwūd said: Ḥasanah was his mother.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2107), al-Nasāʾī (3350), Aḥmad (27408), al-Ḥākim (2/181), and al-Bayhaqī (7/232), all through Maʿmar, from al-Zuhrī, from ʿUrwah, from Umm Ḥabībah. Al-Ḥākim said: “Authentic according to the conditions of the two Shaykhs.”
She, may Allah be pleased with her, died during the caliphate of Muʿāwiyah in the year 44 AH, although it has also been said that she died in 42 AH.

9417 — Anas reported: I was riding behind Abū Ṭalḥah on the day of Khaybar, and my foot was touching the foot of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. We reached the people of Khaybar just as the sun rose. They had brought out their livestock and had gone out carrying their axes, baskets, and spades. When they saw the army, they exclaimed, “Muḥammad and the army!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Khaybar is ruined! When we descend into the territory of a people who have been warned, terrible is the morning that befalls them.”
Allah Almighty defeated them. A beautiful female captive fell to the share of Diḥyah, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ acquired her from him in exchange for seven captives. He then entrusted her to Umm Sulaym so that she might prepare and adorn her for him. The narrator said: I think he also said that she was to observe her waiting period in Umm Sulaym’s house. This woman was Ṣafiyyah bint Ḥuyayy.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ made dates, dried yogurt, and clarified butter the food for her wedding feast. Shallow pits were dug in the ground, leather mats were spread within them, and dried yogurt and clarified butter were brought. The people ate until they were satisfied.
The people said, “We do not know whether he has married her or taken her as a bondwoman who may bear him a child. If he screens her, she is his wife; but if he does not screen her, she is a bondwoman.” When he was ready to depart, he screened her, and she sat upon the rear part of the camel. They then knew that he had married her.
As they drew near Madinah, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ urged his mount forward, and we did likewise. His she-camel al-ʿAḍbāʾ stumbled, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and Ṣafiyyah both fell. He immediately stood and screened her from view. Some women were looking on and said, “May Allah remove the Jewish woman far away!”
Anas was asked, “Abū Ḥamzah, did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ really fall?” He replied, “Yes, by Allah, he certainly fell.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Military Expeditions (4200) and Muslim in the Book of Marriage (1365:87), both through Thābit, from Anas.
She, may Allah be pleased with her, died in the year 52 AH during the caliphate of Muʿāwiyah.

9418 — Yazīd ibn al-Aṣamm said: Maymūnah bint al-Ḥārith informed me that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ married her while he was not in the state of iḥrām. She was both my maternal aunt and the maternal aunt of Ibn ʿAbbās.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Marriage (1411) through Abū Bakr ibn Abī Shaybah, from Yaḥyā ibn Ādam, from Jarīr ibn Ḥāzim, from Abū Fazārah, from Yazīd ibn al-Aṣamm.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ married her in Dhū al-Qaʿdah of the seventh year after the Hijrah, when he performed the compensatory ʿumrah.
Ibn Saʿd said: She was the last woman he married—meaning the last wife with whom he consummated marriage.
According to the correct view, Maymūnah died at Sarif in the year 51 AH. She was buried at the location of the tent in which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had consummated his marriage with her. See: Al-Iṣābah (11919).

9419 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb reported: The ruler of the Copts presented the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with two Coptic bondwomen who were sisters, together with a mule. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to ride the mule. He took one of the two women as a concubine, and she gave birth to his son Ibrāhīm. He gave the other woman to Ḥassān ibn Thābit al-Anṣārī.
Good: Narrated by al-Ḥārith ibn Abī Usāmah (452, Bughyat al-Bāḥith) and al-Ṭaḥāwī in Sharḥ al-Mushkil (2569).
These were the thirteen women with whom the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had intimate marital relations. Eleven of them were his wives. Regarding the twelfth, Rayḥānah, scholars differed as to whether she was a wife or a concubine. The thirteenth was Māriyah the Copt, who was indisputably a concubine.
As for the women whom he married and then divorced, proposed to without marrying, or who offered themselves to him but whom he did not marry, they numbered approximately four or five, as Ibn al-Qayyim stated in Zād al-Maʿād (1/113). Among them was the daughter of al-Jawn, as reported in an authentic narration.

9420 — ʿĀʾishah reported: When the daughter of al-Jawn was brought to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and he approached her, she said, “I seek refuge in Allah from you.” He replied, “You have sought refuge in the Almighty. Return to your family.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5254).
Her statement, “I seek refuge in Allah from you,” indicates the woman’s lack of sound judgment.
As for the reports claiming that she said this at the instruction of some of the Prophet’s ﷺ wives, all such narrations are weak and objectionable.

9421 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “A son was born to me tonight, and I named him after my father Ibrāhīm.”
He then entrusted him to Umm Sayf, the wife of a blacksmith known as Abū Sayf. The Prophet ﷺ went to visit the child, and I followed him. We reached Abū Sayf while he was blowing his bellows, and the house was filled with smoke. I hurried ahead of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “Abū Sayf, stop; the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has arrived.” He stopped, and the Prophet ﷺ called for the child, embraced him, and said whatever Allah willed him to say.
Anas continued: I saw Ibrāhīm struggling for breath in the presence of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Tears began flowing from the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ eyes, and he said:
“The eyes shed tears, the heart grieves, and we say only what pleases our Lord. By Allah, Ibrāhīm, we are deeply saddened by your departure.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2315) and al-Bukhārī (1303).

9422 — Anas ibn Mālik said: I never saw anyone more compassionate toward children and dependents than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. His son Ibrāhīm was being nursed in the upper districts of Madinah. The Prophet ﷺ would go there, and we would accompany him. He would enter the smoke-filled house—for the child’s foster father was a blacksmith—take Ibrāhīm into his arms, kiss him, and then return.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2316).

9423 — Anas ibn Mālik said: We attended the burial of one of the daughters of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was sitting beside the grave, and I saw tears flowing from his eyes. He asked, “Is there any man among you who did not have marital relations last night?”
Abū Ṭalḥah replied, “I did not.”
The Prophet ﷺ said, “Then descend into the grave.”
Abū Ṭalḥah accordingly descended into her grave.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1285).

9424 — Buraydah reported: Abū Bakr and ʿUmar, may Allah be pleased with them, each proposed marriage to Fāṭimah, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “She is young.” Then ʿAlī proposed to her, and the Prophet ﷺ married her to him.
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3221).

9425 — ʿAlī said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ furnished Fāṭimah for her marriage with a velvet covering, a waterskin, and a pillow stuffed with fibers of idhkhir grass.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (3384) and Aḥmad (643), both through Abū Usāmah, from Zāʾidah ibn Qudāmah, from ʿAṭāʾ ibn al-Sāʾib, from his father, from ʿAlī, who related it.

9426 — ʿĀʾishah reported that Fāṭimah, the daughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, sent a request to Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq asking for her inheritance from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. She then related a lengthy account in which it is stated:
Fāṭimah lived for six months after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. When she died, her husband ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib buried her at night without notifying Abū Bakr of her death, and ʿAlī performed the funeral prayer over her.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Military Campaigns (4240–4241) and Muslim in the Book of Jihād and Expeditions (1759:52), both through al-Layth, from ʿUqayl, from Ibn Shihāb, from ʿUrwah, from ʿĀʾishah, who related it as part of a lengthy account.

9427 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was neither excessively tall nor short. He was neither extremely pale nor dark-complexioned. His hair was neither tightly curled nor completely straight. Allah Most High commissioned him as a prophet at the age of forty. He remained in Makkah for ten years and in Madinah for ten years, and Allah took his soul when he was sixty years old. There were not even twenty white hairs on his head and in his beard.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (1), al-Bukhārī (3548, 5900), and Muslim (2347:113).
The expression “neither excessively tall” means that he was not extraordinarily tall. The word amhaq refers to an unpleasant, extreme paleness. Curly hair is the opposite of straight hair, while sabt refers to hair that hangs straight and smooth.
Regarding the statement that he remained in Makkah for ten years, the correct view is that he remained there for thirteen years and died at the age of sixty-three, as established by numerous reports. This narration is therefore understood to mean that the narrator omitted the fraction exceeding ten and sixty.

9428 — Anas described the Prophet ﷺ, saying: He was of medium height among the people, neither tall nor short. His complexion was radiant—neither extremely pale nor dark. His hair was neither tightly curled nor completely straight, but moderately wavy. Revelation came to him when he was forty years old. He remained in Makkah receiving revelation for ten years and in Madinah for ten years. When he died, there were not even twenty white hairs on his head and in his beard.
Rabīʿah said: I saw some of his hair, and it appeared red. I asked about it and was told that the perfume had made it red.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3547) and Muslim (2347:113).

9429 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had the most beautiful face and the finest physical form of all people. He was neither excessively tall nor short.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3549) and Muslim (2337:91).

9430 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib said: The Prophet ﷺ was neither tall nor short. His hands and feet were strong and substantial, his head was large, his joints were prominent, and the line of hair extending from his chest to his navel was long. When he walked, he leaned forward with strength, as though descending a slope. I never saw anyone like him, either before or after him ﷺ.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3637), who authenticated it, and Aḥmad (746, 1053, 1122); authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/606).

9431 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had a large head, large eyes, and long eyelashes. There was a reddish tinge in the whites of his eyes. His beard was thick, and his complexion was radiant. When he walked, he leaned forward as though ascending an incline. When he turned, he turned his entire body. His hands and feet were strong and substantial.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (684, 796), Ibn Saʿd (1/410), al-Bukhārī in Al-Adab al-Mufrad (1315), and al-Bazzār (660).

9432 — Wahb Abū Juḥayfah al-Suwāʾī said: I saw the Prophet ﷺ and noticed some whiteness beneath his lower lip, in the ʿanfaqah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3545) and Muslim (2342:106).
The ʿanfaqah is the area between the chin and the lower lip, whether or not hair grows upon it.

9433 — Abū Juḥayfah said: I saw the Prophet ﷺ, and al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī resembled him.
Ismāʿīl ibn Abī Khālid asked Abū Juḥayfah, “Describe him to me.”
He replied, “He was fair-complexioned, and some whiteness had appeared in his hair. The Prophet ﷺ ordered that thirteen young she-camels be given to us, but he died before we received them.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3544) and Muslim (2343:107).
Muslim’s wording reads, “He was fair-complexioned and had grown some white hair,” instead of, “some whiteness had appeared in his hair.”
The Arabic word shamiṭa means that white hairs had become mixed with the dark hairs. These white hairs were located principally in the ʿanfaqah, the area between the chin and lower lip.

9434 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Busr, a Companion of the Prophet ﷺ, was asked, “Did you see the Prophet ﷺ when he had become elderly?” He replied, “There were a few white hairs in the area beneath his lower lip.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3546).

9435 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib said: The Prophet ﷺ was of medium height and broad between the shoulders. His hair reached the lobes of his ears. I saw him wearing a red outfit, and I never saw anything more beautiful than him.
Another wording states: I never saw anyone with hair reaching the shoulders look more beautiful in a red outfit than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. His hair touched his shoulders, he was broad between them, and he was neither tall nor short.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3551) and Muslim (2337:91). The alternate wording was narrated by Muslim (2337:92).

9436 — Anas ibn Mālik was asked, “What was the hair of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ like?” He replied, “His hair was moderately wavy, neither curly nor completely straight, and its length was between his ears and shoulders.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5905) and Muslim (2338:94).

9437 — Anas reported that the hair of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ touched his shoulders.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5903, 5904) and Muslim (2338:95).

9438 — Anas said: The hair of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ reached the middle of his ears.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2338:96).

9439 — ʿĀʾishah said: The hair of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was longer than a wafrah but shorter than a jummah.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4187), al-Tirmidhī (1755), Ibn Mājah (3635), and Aḥmad (24871).
Al-ʿIrāqī said: Three descriptions have been reported concerning the Prophet’s ﷺ hair: jummah, wafrah, and limmah. A wafrah is hair reaching the earlobes; a limmah extends below the earlobes; and a jummah extends farther, reaching the shoulders.

9440 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ initially let his hair hang down without parting it. The polytheists used to part their hair, while the People of the Scripture let theirs hang down. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ liked to agree with the People of the Scripture in matters concerning which he had received no specific command. Later, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ parted his hair.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3558, 3944) and Muslim (2336:90).

9441 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Prophet ﷺ had large, substantial hands, and I never saw anyone like him afterward. His hair was moderately wavy, neither curly nor completely straight.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5906) and Muslim (2338:94).

9442 — Anas said: The Prophet ﷺ had large, substantial hands and feet and a beautiful face. I never saw anyone like him, either before or after him. His palms were broad.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5907).

9443 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib was asked, “Was the Prophet’s ﷺ face like a sword?” He replied, “No, rather it was like the moon.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3552).

9444 — Jābir ibn Samurah said: Some white hairs had appeared at the front of the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ head and in his beard. When he applied oil, they were not noticeable, but when his hair was disheveled, they became visible. His beard was thick.
A man asked, “Was his face like a sword?”
He replied, “No, rather it was like the sun and the moon, and it was somewhat rounded. I saw the Seal of Prophethood near his shoulder; it was like a pigeon’s egg and resembled the color of his body.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2344:109).

9445 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik said, during the lengthy account of his remaining behind from the expedition of Tabūk: When I greeted the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, his face was shining with happiness. Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was pleased, his face would become radiant as though it were a piece of the moon, and we could recognize his happiness from that.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3556) and Muslim (2769:53).

9446 — Abū al-Ṭufayl said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and no man who saw him remains upon the face of the earth besides me.
He was asked, “What did he look like?”
He replied, “He was fair-complexioned, handsome, and well-proportioned.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2340:99).
The word muqaṣṣadan means that he was neither heavily built nor thin, and neither tall nor short.
This hadith indicates that Abū al-Ṭufayl was the last Companion to die. Muslim explicitly stated: “Abū al-Ṭufayl died in the year 100 AH and was the last of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to die.”
I say: The additional ten years were omitted, for Abū al-Ṭufayl actually died in 110 AH.

9447 — Anas said: I never touched silk or brocade softer than the palm of the Prophet ﷺ, and I never smelled any fragrance more pleasant than the fragrance of the Prophet ﷺ.
Another wording states: I never smelled ambergris, musk, or anything else more fragrant than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and I never touched brocade or silk softer to the touch than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3561) and Muslim (2330:81). The alternate wording is Muslim’s.

9448 — Anas said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had a radiant complexion, and his perspiration appeared like pearls. When he walked, he leaned forward with strength. I never touched brocade or silk softer than the palm of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and I never smelled musk or ambergris more pleasant than the fragrance of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Another wording states: I never touched silk fabric or pure silk softer than the palm of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and I never smelled musk or any perfume more fragrant than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1973) and Muslim (2330:82). The alternate wording is al-Bukhārī’s.

9449 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: The Prophet ﷺ used to enter the house of Umm Sulaym and sleep upon her bed while she was away. One day he came and slept upon her bed. Someone went to her and said, “The Prophet ﷺ is sleeping in your house upon your bed.”
She came and found that he had perspired and that his perspiration had collected upon a piece of leather on the bed. She opened her small container and began absorbing the perspiration and squeezing it into her bottles. The Prophet ﷺ awoke with a start and asked, “What are you doing, Umm Sulaym?”
She replied, “O Messenger of Allah, we hope for its blessing for our children.”
He said, “You have done well.”
Another narration states: Umm Sulaym used to spread a leather mat for the Prophet ﷺ, upon which he would take his midday rest in her home. When the Prophet ﷺ slept, she would collect some of his perspiration and hair in a bottle and mix it into sukk perfume while he slept. When Anas ibn Mālik was near death, he instructed that some of that sukk be placed in the perfume used to prepare his body for burial, and it was placed in it.
Another narration states: Anas said: The Prophet ﷺ entered our home and took his midday rest with us. He perspired, and my mother brought a bottle and began collecting the perspiration in it. The Prophet ﷺ awoke and asked, “Umm Sulaym, what are you doing?”
She replied, “This is your perspiration. We mix it into our perfume, and it is among the finest of fragrances.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2331:84). The second wording was narrated by al-Bukhārī (6281), and the third by Muslim (2331:83).

9450 — Umm Sulaym reported that the Prophet ﷺ used to visit her and take his midday rest in her home. She would spread a leather mat for him, and he would sleep upon it. He perspired profusely, so she would collect his perspiration and place it in her perfume and bottles.
The Prophet ﷺ asked, “Umm Sulaym, what is this?”
She replied, “It is your perspiration. I mix it with my perfume.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2332:85).
The word “adūfu” means “I mix.”

9451 — Jābir ibn Samurah said: I performed the noon prayer with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He then left to go to his family, and I accompanied him. Some children met him, and he began stroking their cheeks one by one. He also stroked my cheek, and I felt a coolness and fragrance from his hand, as though he had just taken it from a perfumer’s box.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2329:80).

9452 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Mālik ibn Buḥaynah reported that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ prayed, he kept his arms away from his sides until the whiteness of his underarms became visible.
Another wording states: When he prostrated, he kept his arms away from his sides.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3564) and Muslim (495:235).

9453 — Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not ordinarily raise his hands so high during supplication, except when praying for rain. On that occasion, he would raise them until the whiteness of his underarms could be seen.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3565) and Muslim (895:7).

9454 — Abū Juḥayfah reported:
I came to the Prophet ﷺ in Makkah while he was at al-Abṭaḥ in a red leather tent. Bilāl came out carrying the water the Prophet ﷺ had used for ablution; some people obtained some of it, while others received drops sprinkled upon them.
Then the Prophet ﷺ came out wearing a red garment, and it is as though I can still see the whiteness of his legs… the remainder of the hadith.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3566) and Muslim (503/249). This is Muslim’s wording. In al-Bukhārī’s wording: “the gleam of his legs.”

9455 — Al-Barāʾ said: I heard the Prophet ﷺ reciting during the night prayer, “By the fig and the olive” [Al-Tīn: 1], and I never heard anyone with a more beautiful voice or recitation than his.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7546) and Muslim (464).

9456 — Jābir ibn Samurah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had a broad mouth, long openings to his eyes, and lean heels.
The narrator said: I asked Simāk, “What does ‘broad-mouthed’ mean?” He replied, “His mouth was large.”
I asked, “What does the description of his eyes mean?” He replied, “The openings of his eyes were long.”
I asked, “What does ‘lean-heeled’ mean?” He replied, “There was little flesh upon his heels.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2339:79).

9457 — Anas was asked, “Did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ dye his hair?” He replied, “Only a small amount of white hair was visible on him.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5894) and Muslim (2341:102).

9458 — Anas said: It is disliked for a man to pluck a white hair from his head or beard. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not dye his hair. The whiteness was confined to the area beneath his lower lip, his temples, and a few scattered hairs on his head.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3550) and Muslim (2341:104).

9459 — Anas was asked about the Prophet ﷺ dyeing his hair. He replied: His white hairs never became numerous enough to require dyeing. If I wished, I could count the white hairs in his beard.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5895) and Muslim (2341:103).

9460 — Anas was asked about the Prophet’s ﷺ white hairs and replied: Allah did not mar his beauty with extensive whiteness.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2341:105).

9461 — Anas reported:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was not excessively tall… the remainder of the hadith.
It also states:
Allah took his soul when there were not even twenty white hairs on his head and in his beard.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3548) and Muslim (2347/113).

9462 — Jābir ibn Samurah was asked about the Prophet’s ﷺ white hairs. He replied: When he applied oil to his hair, no whiteness could be seen, but when he did not apply oil, some of it was visible.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2344:108).

9463 — Umm Salamah brought out for us some of the Prophet’s ﷺ hair that had been dyed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5897).

9464 — Anas said: I saw some of the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ hair dyed.
Ḥammād said: ʿAbdullāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAqīl also informed us: I saw some of the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ dyed hair in the possession of Anas ibn Mālik.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (47).
There is no contradiction between this report and the narrations denying that he dyed his hair. Anas may later have seen some dyed hair in the possession of Umm Salamah and consequently reported it.
Al-Nawawī said: The preferred view is that the Prophet ﷺ dyed his hair on one occasion, as indicated by Ibn ʿUmar’s hadith in the two Ṣaḥīḥ collections, but refrained from doing so most of the time. Each narrator reported what he personally witnessed, and each spoke truthfully.

9465 — Abū Rimthah said: I went with my father to see the Prophet ﷺ. His hair reached his earlobes and bore traces of henna, and he was wearing two green garments.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4206), whose wording this is, al-Tirmidhī (2812), al-Nasāʾī (1572), and Aḥmad (7109).

9466 — Al-Sāʾib ibn Yazīd said: My maternal aunt took me to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “O Messenger of Allah, my sister’s son is ill.” He stroked my head and prayed that I be blessed. He then performed ablution, and I drank from the water he had used. I stood behind him and looked at the Seal of Prophethood between his shoulders; it resembled the button of a bridal canopy.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3541) and Muslim (2345:111).
For the expression “the button of a bridal canopy,” with both Arabic consonants bearing fatḥah, see its explanation in the Book of Faith.

9467 — Jābir ibn Samurah said: I saw the Seal upon the back of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and it resembled a pigeon’s egg.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2344:110).

9468 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Sarjis said: I saw the Prophet ﷺ and ate bread and meat with him—or he said: tharīd.
The narrator asked him, “Did the Prophet ﷺ seek forgiveness for you?”
He replied, “Yes, and for you as well.” He then recited the verse:
“Seek forgiveness for your sin and for the believing men and believing women.”
 [Muḥammad: 19]
He continued: I then moved around behind him and looked at the Seal of Prophethood between his shoulders, near the upper part of his left shoulder. It formed a gathered cluster, with moles resembling small warts upon it.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2346:112).
The expression “a gathered cluster” means that its shape resembled a closed palm with the fingers brought together.
The Arabic word khīlān is the plural of khāl, meaning a mole upon the body.

9469 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ possessed the finest character of all people.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Adab (6203) and Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2310:55), both through ʿAbd al-Wārith, from Abū al-Tayyāḥ, from Anas, who related it.

9470 — Anas reported: I served the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for ten years. By Allah, he never once said “Uff!” to me. Nor did he ever say concerning something I had done, “Why did you do that?” or concerning something I had not done, “Why did you not do that?”
Another narration states: I served the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for nine years, and to the best of my knowledge, he never once said to me, “Why did you do such and such?” Nor did he ever criticize anything I did.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Adab (6038) and Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2309:51), both through Thābit al-Bunānī, from Anas ibn Mālik, who related it. The alternate narration is also recorded by Muslim (2309:53).

9471 — Anas reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ arrived in Madinah, Abū Ṭalḥah took me by the hand and brought me to him. He said, “O Messenger of Allah, Anas is an intelligent boy, so let him serve you.”
I therefore served him both while traveling and while at home. By Allah, he never said concerning something I had done, “Why did you do it in this manner?” Nor did he ever say concerning something I had not done, “Why did you not do it in this manner?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Waṣāyā (2768) and al-Diyāt (6911), and Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2309:52), through Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm ibn ʿUlayyah, from ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, from Anas, who related it.

9472 — Anas reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was among the finest of people in character. One day he sent me to attend to an errand. I said, “By Allah, I will not go,” although inwardly I intended to carry out what the Prophet of Allah ﷺ had commanded me.
I went out and passed some children playing in the marketplace. Suddenly, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came up behind me and took hold of the back of my neck. I looked at him and saw that he was laughing.
He said, “Little Anas, did you go where I instructed you?”
I replied, “Yes, Messenger of Allah, I am going now.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2310:54), from Abū Maʿn al-Raqāshī Zayd ibn Yazīd, from ʿUmar ibn Yūnus, from ʿIkrimah—namely Ibn ʿAmmār—who reported that Isḥāq said: Anas related it.

9473 — Saʿd ibn Hishām said: I asked, “Mother of the Believers, tell me about the character of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
She replied, “Do you not read the Qur’an?”
I said, “Yes.”
She said, “The character of the Prophet of Allah ﷺ was the Qur’an”—the remainder of the hadith.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in Ṣalāt al-Musāfirīn (746:139), from Muḥammad ibn al-Muthannā al-ʿAnazī, from Muḥammad ibn Abī ʿAdī, from Saʿīd, from Qatādah, from Zurārah, that Saʿd ibn Hishām ibn ʿĀmir came to ʿĀʾishah and asked her; he then related the hadith in a lengthy account.

9474 — Yazīd ibn Bābnūs said: We asked ʿĀʾishah, “Mother of the Believers, what was the character of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ like?”
She replied, “The character of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was the Qur’an.” She then recited from “Successful indeed are the believers” until she reached, “And those who carefully maintain their prayers.” [Al-Muʾminūn: 1–9]
She then said, “Such was the character of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11287) and al-Bukhārī in al-Adab al-Mufrad (308), both through Jaʿfar—namely Ibn Sulaymān al-Ḍubaʿī—from Abū ʿImrān, from Yazīd ibn Bābnūs, who related it.

9475 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ was asked, “Tell me how the Messenger of Allah ﷺ is described in the Torah.”
He replied: Yes, by Allah, he is described in the Torah with some of the same qualities by which he is described in the Qur’an:
“O Prophet, We have sent you as a witness, a bearer of glad tidings, and a warner” [Al-Aḥzāb: 45], and as a refuge for the unlettered people.
“You are My servant and Messenger. I have named you al-Mutawakkil—the one who places his trust in Allah. He is neither harsh nor hard-hearted, nor is he loud and clamorous in the marketplaces. He does not repay evil with evil; rather, he pardons and forgives. Allah will not take his soul until He straightens through him the crooked religion by causing people to say, ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ thereby opening blind eyes, deaf ears, and closed hearts.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Buyūʿ (2125), from Muḥammad ibn Sinān, from Fulayḥ, from Hilāl, from ʿAṭāʾ ibn Yasār, who said: I met ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ and asked him to describe the Messenger of Allah ﷺ; he then related it.

9476 — ʿĀʾishah said: It is written in the Gospel concerning the Messenger of Allah ﷺ: “He is neither harsh nor hard-hearted, nor clamorous in the marketplaces. He does not repay an evil deed with one like it; rather, he pardons and overlooks.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Saʿd (1/363), Isḥāq ibn Rāhawayh (1610, 1611), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/614), all through routes from al-ʿAyzār ibn Ḥurayth, from ʿĀʾishah, who related it.

9477 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr said: The Prophet ﷺ was neither obscene in his speech nor deliberately indecent. He used to say, “Among the best of you are those who possess the finest character.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Manāqib (3559) and Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2321:68), both through al-Aʿmash, from Abū Wāʾil Shaqīq ibn Salamah, from Masrūq, from ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr, who related it.

9478 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Prophet ﷺ was neither abusive, obscene, nor given to cursing. When reproaching one of us, he would merely say, “What is the matter with him? May dust touch his forehead!”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Adab (6031, 6046), through Aṣbagh from Ibn Wahb and through Muḥammad ibn Sinān, both from Fulayḥ ibn Sulaymān Abū Yaḥyā, from Hilāl ibn ʿAlī ibn Usāmah, from Anas ibn Mālik, who related it.

9479 — ʿĀʾishah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was neither obscene nor deliberately indecent, nor was he loud and quarrelsome in the marketplaces. He did not repay an evil deed with another evil deed; rather, he pardoned and overlooked.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (2016) and al-Shamāʾil (347), Aḥmad (25417), and Ibn Ḥibbān (6443), through routes from Abū Isḥāq al-Sabīʿī, from Abū ʿAbdullāh al-Jadalī—whose name was ʿAbd ibn ʿAbd—from ʿĀʾishah, who related it. Al-Tirmidhī said: good authentic hadith.

9480 — Abū Hurayrah described the Prophet ﷺ, saying: His forearms were long—or broad—and his eyelashes were long. He was broad between the shoulders. When he turned toward someone or away from them, he turned his entire body. May my father and mother be sacrificed for him! He was neither obscene nor deliberately indecent, nor was he loud and quarrelsome in the marketplaces.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (8352), whose wording this is, Ibn Saʿd (1/414), al-Ṭayālisī (2432), and al-Bayhaqī in al-Dalāʾil (1/316, 244), all through Ibn Abī Dhiʾb—Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān—from Ṣāliḥ, the freedman of al-Tawʾamah, from Abū Hurayrah, who related it.
The expression “his forearms were shabḥ” means that they were long, though it has also been interpreted as broad. “Long eyelashes” means that the hairs upon his eyelids were long.

9481 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Do not exaggerate in praising me as the Christians exaggerated in praising the son of Mary. I am only Allah’s servant, so say: ‘The servant of Allah and His Messenger.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Anbiyāʾ (3445), from al-Ḥumaydī, from Sufyān, from al-Zuhrī, from ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh, from Ibn ʿAbbās, from ʿUmar, who related it.

9482 — Anas ibn Mālik said: A bondwoman from among the bondwomen of Madinah could take the Messenger of Allah ﷺ by the hand and lead him wherever she wished.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Adab (6072) in suspended form: Muḥammad ibn ʿĪsā, from Hushaym, from Ḥumayd al-Ṭawīl, from Anas ibn Mālik, who related it. Aḥmad narrated it with a connected chain (11941), with the wording: “She would take him with her so that he could attend to her need.”
This demonstrates the Prophet’s ﷺ humility and his complete freedom from every form of arrogance. Taking him by the hand here refers to its necessary meaning: that he willingly accompanied and followed her.

9483 — Abū Hurayrah reported: Jibrīl was sitting with the Prophet ﷺ when he looked toward the sky and saw an angel descending. Jibrīl said, “This angel has never descended since the day he was created until this moment.”
When the angel descended, he said, “Muḥammad, your Lord has sent me to ask you: Should He make you a prophet-king or a servant-messenger?”
Jibrīl said, “Humble yourself before your Lord, Muḥammad.”
The Prophet ﷺ replied, “Rather, a servant-messenger.”
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (7160), al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (2462), and Abū Yaʿlā (6105), all from Muḥammad ibn Fuḍayl, from ʿUmārah ibn Abī Zurʿah, who said, “I know it only from Abū Hurayrah”; he then related the hadith. Its chain is authentic.
It has been said that this unnamed angel was Isrāfīl.

9484 — ʿĀʾishah narrated a similar report, with the additional wording: After that, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would not eat while reclining. He would say, “I eat as a servant eats, and I sit as a servant sits.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Yaʿlā (4920), from Muḥammad ibn Bakkār, from Abū Maʿshar, from Saʿīd al-Maqburī, from ʿĀʾishah, who related it.

9485 — Abū Hurayrah reported: A Muslim man and a Jewish man exchanged insults. During an oath, the Muslim said, “By Him who chose Muḥammad above all worlds.”
The Jewish man replied, “By Him who chose Moses above all worlds.”
The Muslim raised his hand and struck the Jewish man across the face. The Jewish man went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and informed him of what had occurred between him and the Muslim.
The Prophet ﷺ said: “Do not declare me superior to Moses in this manner. The people will fall unconscious on the Day of Resurrection, and I will be the first to regain consciousness. I will then find Moses grasping the side of the Throne. I will not know whether he was among those who fell unconscious and regained consciousness before me, or whether he was among those whom Allah exempted.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Tawḥīd (7472) and Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2373:160), both through Ibrāhīm, from Ibn Shihāb, from Abū Salamah ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Aʿraj, from Abū Hurayrah. Their wording is the same.

9486 — Ibn ʿAbbās said: The Prophet ﷺ was the most generous of all people, and he was at his most generous during Ramadan when Jibrīl met him. Jibrīl, peace be upon him would meet him every night of Ramadan and review the Qur’an with him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was more generous in dispensing goodness than a freely blowing wind.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Manāqib (3554) and Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2308:50), both through Ibn Shihāb al-Zuhrī, from ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUtbah ibn Masʿūd, from Ibn ʿAbbās, who related it.

9487 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me, “When the wealth of Bahrain arrives, I shall give you this much, this much, and this much,” gesturing three times with his hands.
The wealth of Bahrain did not arrive until after the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had died. When it reached Abū Bakr, he ordered a herald to announce, “Whoever is owed a debt or promise by the Prophet ﷺ should come to me.”
Jābir said: I went to Abū Bakr and informed him that the Prophet ﷺ had said, “When the wealth of Bahrain arrives, I shall give you this much, this much, and this much,” three times. Abū Bakr then gave me a portion.
Jābir continued: I later met Abū Bakr and asked him, but he did not give me anything. I went to him again, but he did not give me anything. I then went to him a third time, but he still did not give me anything. I said, “I came to you and you did not give me; then I came again and you did not give me; and now I have come once more and you have not given me. Either give me, or you are withholding from me out of stinginess.”
He replied, “Did you say that I am withholding from you out of stinginess? What disease could be worse than stinginess?” He repeated this three times. He then said, “Whenever I withheld from you, I intended all along to give you.”
In another chain, ʿAmr narrated from Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī, who said: I heard Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh say: I went to Abū Bakr, and he said, “Count it.” I counted the amount and found it to be five hundred. He said, “Take twice that amount in addition.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in al-Maghāzī (4383) and Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2314:60), both through Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah, from Ibn al-Munkadir, who heard Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh relate it.

9488 — Anas reported:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was never asked for anything for the sake of Islam except that he gave it.
A man came to him, and he gave him a flock of sheep filling the space between two mountains. The man returned to his people and said:
“My people, embrace Islam, for Muḥammad gives with the generosity of one who does not fear poverty.”
In another narration:
A man asked the Prophet ﷺ for a flock of sheep filling the space between two mountains, and he gave it to him… the remainder of the hadith.
Anas said therein:
“A man might initially embrace Islam desiring nothing but worldly gain, yet he would not remain long before Islam became more beloved to him than the world and everything in it.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in al-Faḍāʾil (2312:57), from ʿĀṣim ibn al-Naḍr al-Tamīmī, from Khālid—namely Ibn al-Ḥārith—from Ḥumayd, from Mūsā ibn Anas, from his father, who related it. The other narration is also recorded by Muslim (2312:58).

9489 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported: When the Kaʿbah was being rebuilt, the Prophet ﷺ and al-ʿAbbās carried stones. Al-ʿAbbās said to the Prophet ﷺ, “Place your waist-wrapper over your neck so that it will protect you from the stones.”
The Prophet ﷺ immediately fell to the ground, and his eyes turned upward toward the sky. When he recovered, he said, “My waist-wrapper! My waist-wrapper!” He then fastened it securely around himself.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3829) and Muslim (340).

9490 — Abū al-Ṭufayl reported: When the Sacred House was being rebuilt, the people carried stones, and the Prophet ﷺ carried stones with them. He took his garment and placed it over his shoulder, whereupon a voice called out to him, “Do not uncover your private parts.” He therefore put down the stone and put his garment back on.
Good: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (1105), and through him by Aḥmad (23794), and by al-Ḥākim (4/179).

9491 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, said: Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was given a choice between two matters, he chose the easier of the two, provided that it was not sinful. If it involved sin, he was the person farthest removed from it. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ never took revenge for a personal wrong unless one of Allah’s sacred limits had been violated.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (2), al-Bukhārī (3560), and Muslim (2327:77).

9492 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was more modest than a virgin secluded in her private chamber. Whenever he disliked something, we could recognize it from his face.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3562, 6102) and Muslim (2320:76).

9493 — Abū Hurayrah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ never criticized any food. If he desired it, he ate it; and if he disliked it, he left it.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3563) and Muslim (2064:187).

9494 — Anas said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was the most handsome of people, the most generous of people, and the most courageous of people.
One night, the people of Madinah were alarmed by a sound. Some people set out in its direction, but the Messenger of Allah ﷺ met them as he was returning, having already preceded them to investigate it. He was riding Abū Ṭalḥah’s unsaddled horse with a sword hanging around his neck, and he was saying, “Do not be frightened! Do not be frightened!”
