He then said regarding the horse, “We found it as swift and smooth as the sea,” or, “It is indeed like the sea.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6033) and Muslim (2307:48). Al-Bukhārī adds: “He was riding Abū Ṭalḥah’s bare horse, which had no saddle upon it.”

9495 — Anas reported: There was an alarm in Madinah, so the Prophet ﷺ borrowed a horse belonging to Abū Ṭalḥah called al-Mandūb and rode it. When he returned, he said, “We found nothing to fear, and we found the horse to be as swift and smooth as the sea.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2627, 6212) and Muslim (2307:49).

9496 — Al-Barāʾ said: By Allah, whenever the battle became fierce, we would seek protection behind him. The courageous among us was the one who could remain alongside him—meaning the Prophet ﷺ.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1776:79).

9497 — ʿAlī said: I saw us on the day of Badr seeking shelter behind the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He was the nearest of us to the enemy and was among the fiercest of people in battle that day.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (654).

9498 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was never asked for anything and replied, “No.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6034) and Muslim (2311:56).

9499 — Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim reported: While he was accompanying the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and the people on their return from Ḥunayn, some Bedouins crowded around the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asking him for things. They pressed him against a thorny tree, which caught and pulled away his cloak.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stopped and said, “Give me back my cloak. If I possessed livestock equal in number to these thorny trees, I would distribute it all among you. You would then find me neither stingy, untruthful, nor cowardly.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3148).

9500 — Anas ibn Mālik passed by some children and greeted them with salām. He then said: The Prophet ﷺ used to do this.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6247) and Muslim (2168:15).

9501 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “A son was born to me tonight, and I named him after my father Ibrāhīm.”
He then entrusted him to Umm Sayf, the wife of a blacksmith known as Abū Sayf. The Prophet ﷺ went to visit the child, and I followed him. We reached Abū Sayf while he was blowing his bellows, and the house was filled with smoke. I hurried ahead of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “Abū Sayf, stop; the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has arrived.”
He stopped, and the Prophet ﷺ called for the child, embraced him, and said whatever Allah willed him to say.
Anas continued: I saw Ibrāhīm struggling for breath in the presence of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Tears began flowing from the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ eyes, and he said:
“The eyes shed tears, the heart grieves, and we say only what pleases our Lord. By Allah, Ibrāhīm, we are deeply saddened by your departure.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1303) and Muslim (2315:62).
Al-Bukhārī adds that when ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf, may Allah be pleased with him, saw the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ tears, he asked, “Even you, Messenger of Allah?”
He replied, “Ibn ʿAwf, this is compassion.”

9502 — ʿĀʾishah reported: Some Bedouins came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked, “Do you kiss your children?”
They replied, “Yes.”
The Bedouins said, “But by Allah, we do not kiss ours.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “What power do I have to help you if Allah has removed compassion from your hearts?”
Al-Bukhārī’s narration reads: “What power do I have?”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5998) and Muslim (2317:64).

9503 — Anas ibn Mālik said: I never saw anyone more compassionate toward children and dependents than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. His son Ibrāhīm was being nursed in the upper districts of Madinah. The Prophet ﷺ would go there, and we would accompany him. He would enter the smoke-filled house—for the child’s foster father was a blacksmith—take Ibrāhīm into his arms, kiss him, and then return.
ʿAmr said: When Ibrāhīm died, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Ibrāhīm is my son. He died while still of nursing age, and he has two wet nurses who will complete his nursing in Paradise.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2316:63).

9504 — Abū Hurayrah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ kissed al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī while al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis al-Tamīmī was sitting with him.
Al-Aqraʿ said, “I have ten children, yet I have never kissed any of them.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at him and said, “Whoever does not show mercy will not be shown mercy.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5997) and Muslim (2318:65).

9505 — Jarīr ibn ʿAbdullāh reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Allah does not show mercy to one who does not show mercy to people.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6013, 7376) and Muslim (2319:66).

9506 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Awfā said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ remembered Allah frequently, engaged very little in useless speech, lengthened the prayer, and kept the sermon brief. He did not consider it beneath him to walk with a widow or a poor person and fulfill their needs.
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (1414).

9507 — Anas reported: The Prophet ﷺ had a camel driver named Anjashah who possessed a beautiful voice. The Prophet ﷺ said to him, “Gently, Anjashah! Do not break the glass vessels.”
Qatādah said: He meant the physically delicate women.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6211) and Muslim (2323:73).
The name “Anjashah” begins with a fatḥah on the hamzah, followed by a still nūn, then jīm and shīn. He was a black bondsman who drove the camels carrying the Prophet’s ﷺ wives during the Farewell Pilgrimage and other journeys. He had a beautiful voice when chanting to the camels, causing them to move more vigorously. The Prophet ﷺ therefore said, “Gently, Anjashah! Be gentle with the glass vessels,” because the forceful movement could harm the women or cause them to fall.

9508 — Anas reported: After the Messenger of Allah ﷺ performed the dawn prayer, the servants of Madinah would come to him carrying vessels containing water. No vessel was brought to him without his dipping his hand into it. They would sometimes come to him on a bitterly cold morning, yet he would still place his hand in the water.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2324:74).
The statement “he would place his hand in it” means that they sought blessing through that water.

9509 — Anas reported: A woman who had some mental impairment said, “Messenger of Allah, I have a need that I wish you to fulfill.”
He replied, “Mother of so-and-so, choose whichever street you wish, and I will attend to your need.”
He stood apart with her along one of the roads until she had finished explaining her need.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2326:76).
The expression “until she had finished with her need” means until she had finished explaining it.

9510 — Anas said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while the barber was shaving his head. His Companions surrounded him, not wanting a single hair to fall except into the hand of one of them.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2325:75).

9511 — Anas reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was ready for the barber to shave his head, Abū Ṭalḥah took the hair from one side of his head in his hand. He then brought the hair to Umm Sulaym, who used to mix it into her perfume.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (12483).
The expression “she mixed it into her perfume” means that she blended it with the perfume. In Arabic, one says that a substance was mixed with water when it was moistened and blended with it.

9512 — ʿĀʾishah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ never struck anything with his hand—neither a woman nor a servant—except when fighting in the cause of Allah. Whenever he was personally wronged, he never took revenge upon the offender, unless one of Allah’s sacred prohibitions was violated; then he acted for the sake of Allah Almighty.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2328:79).

9513 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported that he joined the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on an expedition toward Najd. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned, Jābir returned with him. The midday heat overtook them in a valley filled with thorny trees. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ dismounted, and the people dispersed among the trees to seek shade. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ rested beneath an acacia tree and hung his sword upon it.
Jābir said: We slept for a while, and suddenly the Messenger of Allah ﷺ called us. We went to him and found a Bedouin sitting beside him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “This man drew my sword while I was asleep. I awoke to find him holding it unsheathed. He asked me, ‘Who will protect you from me?’ I replied, ‘Allah.’ Here he is now, sitting before you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not punish him.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4135) and Muslim (843:13).
The word ʿiḍāh refers to every tree possessing thorns.
The Bedouin mentioned in the statement “we found a Bedouin sitting beside him” was named Ghawrath ibn al-Ḥārith, as stated in a suspended report recorded by al-Bukhārī (4136).
In Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim, the Bedouin asked, “Who will protect you from me?” twice, and the Prophet ﷺ answered, “Allah.”

9514 — ʿĀʾishah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did something, thereby demonstrating its permissibility. News of this reached some of his Companions, but they seemed to dislike it and considered themselves too pious to do it.
When this reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he stood and addressed the people, saying:
“What is the matter with men who were informed that I did something permissible, yet they disliked it and considered themselves too pious to do it? By Allah, I possess greater knowledge of Allah than they do and fear Him more intensely.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6101, 7301) and Muslim (2356:127).

9515 — Anas ibn Mālik reported:
The Prophet ﷺ had the finest character of all people. I had a younger brother called Abū ʿUmayr. Anas said: I believe he said that the boy had recently been weaned.
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came and saw him, he would say:
“O Abū ʿUmayr, what happened to the little nughayr?”
The boy used to play with that bird.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6203) and Muslim (2150/30). 
Al-Tirmidhī said in al-Shamāʾil (236):
Among the lessons of this hadith is that the Prophet ﷺ used to joke. He said to him, “O Abū ʿUmayr, what happened to the little nughayr?” because the boy had a small bird with which he used to play. It died, and the boy became sad, so the Prophet ﷺ joked gently with him and said, “O Abū ʿUmayr…” the remainder of the hadith.
The word nughayr, with a ḍammah on the nūn, is the diminutive of nughar, a small bird. Its plural is nughrān.

9516 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that a man asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to provide him with a riding animal. The Prophet ﷺ said, “I shall mount you upon the offspring of a she-camel.”
The man asked, “Messenger of Allah, what could I do with the offspring of a she-camel?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied, “Do camels give birth to anything other than the offspring of she-camels?”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4998), al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (1991), who authenticated it, in Al-Shamāʾil (238), and Aḥmad (13817).

9517 — Abū Hurayrah reported: The Companions said, “Messenger of Allah, you joke with us.”
He replied, “I say nothing except the truth.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (1990), who graded it good, in Al-Shamāʾil (237), and Aḥmad (8723).

9518 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “I certainly joke, but I say nothing except the truth.”
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (999) and Al-Muʿjam al-Ṣaghīr (779).

9519 — Anas reported: A Bedouin man named Zāhir used to bring the Prophet ﷺ gifts from the desert. Whenever Zāhir was preparing to leave Madinah, the Prophet ﷺ would equip him with provisions. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said, “Zāhir is our man of the desert, and we are his people of the city.”
The Prophet ﷺ loved him, though Zāhir was not physically handsome. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came to him one day while he was selling his merchandise and embraced him from behind, so Zāhir could not see who it was.
Zāhir said, “Let me go! Who is this?” He turned and recognized the Prophet ﷺ. Once he recognized him, he did everything he could to keep his back pressed against the Prophet’s ﷺ chest.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ began saying, “Who will buy this slave?”
Zāhir said, “Messenger of Allah, by Allah, you would then find me to be unsellable merchandise.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied, “But you are not worthless in the sight of Allah,” or he said, “Rather, you are precious in the sight of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by ʿAbd al-Razzāq (19688), through him by Aḥmad (12648), and by al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (239).

9520 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Prophet ﷺ said to me, “O possessor of two ears!”
One of the narrators explained: He said this jokingly.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in Al-Āḥād wa-al-Mathānī (2227) and al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (1/211).

9521 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ laugh until his back teeth became visible.
The complete hadith contains the lengthy account of the last person to emerge from the Fire, and it will appear in its appropriate place.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6571) and Muslim (186:308).

9522 — Jarīr ibn ʿAbdullāh said: From the time I embraced Islam, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ never refused me permission to enter upon him, and whenever he saw me, he smiled.
Another wording states: Whenever he saw me, he smiled in my face.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3822) and Muslim (2475:134).

9523 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, reported:
I never saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ laugh so fully and heartily that I could see his uvula; rather, he would only smile… the remainder of the hadith.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4828) and Muslim (899/16). 
The word “mustajmiʿan” means fully giving himself to the act of laughing and deliberately doing so.
The word lahawāt is the plural of lahāh, the fleshy structure projecting over the throat at the back of the mouth.

9524 — Abū Dharr said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ laugh until his back teeth became visible.
The complete hadith contains the lengthy account of the last person to emerge from the Fire, and it will appear in its appropriate place.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (190:314).
The Arabic word nawājidh is the plural of nājidh, the tooth situated between the canine and the molar.

9525 — Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ reported that the Prophet ﷺ mentioned both of his parents together for him on the day of Uḥud, saying, “Shoot! May my father and mother be sacrificed for you.”
Saʿd said: One of the polytheists had inflicted many casualties upon the Muslims. The Prophet ﷺ said to me, “Shoot! May my father and mother be sacrificed for you.”
I drew an arrow that had no arrowhead and struck the man in his side. He fell, and his private parts became exposed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed until I could see his back teeth.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2412:42).
The expression “he had burned the Muslims” means that he had inflicted numerous injuries upon them.

9526 — Jābir ibn Samurah was asked, “Did you use to sit with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ?”
He replied, “Yes, frequently. He would remain in the place where he had performed the dawn prayer until the sun rose. Once it had risen, he would stand. The Companions would converse and recount matters from the pre-Islamic period. They would laugh, and he ﷺ would smile.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2322:69).

9527 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Ḥārith said: The laughter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was generally no more than a smile.
Sahih Gharib: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (3642) and Al-Shamāʾil (229). He said: authentic, strange.

9528 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Jazʾ said: I never saw anyone smile more frequently than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (3641) and Al-Shamāʾil (228), and by Ibn al-Mubārak in Al-Zuhd (145).

9529 — Abū Hurayrah said: I never saw anything more beautiful than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. It was as though the sun flowed across his face. Nor did I see anyone who walked more swiftly than the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. It was as though the earth were folded up for him. We would exert ourselves to keep pace, while he walked without apparent effort.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6309), whose wording this is, al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (3648), and Aḥmad (8604, 8943).

9530 — ʿĀʾishah reported:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended to set out on a journey, he would draw lots among his wives. Whichever wife’s lot was drawn, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would take her with him… the remainder of the hadith.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4750) and Muslim (2770/56).

9531 — ʿĀʾishah reported:
Whenever the Messenger of Allah ﷺ set out on a journey, he would draw lots among his wives. On one occasion, the lot fell to ʿĀʾishah and Ḥafṣah, so they both went out with him… the remainder of the hadith.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5211) and Muslim (2445).

9532 — ʿĀʾishah reported that whenever something concerned the Prophet ﷺ, he would take hold of his beard in this manner.
Ibn Mushir then demonstrated by grasping his own beard.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6439).

9533 — Anas ibn Mālik said: I was walking with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was wearing a Najrānī cloak with a thick, coarse border. A Bedouin caught up with him and violently pulled him by the cloak.
I looked at the side of the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ neck and saw that the cloak’s border had left a mark upon it because of the force with which the man had pulled him.
The Bedouin then said, “Muḥammad, order that I be given some of Allah’s wealth that is in your possession.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ turned toward him and laughed, then ordered that he be given a gift.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3149) and Muslim (1057:128).

9534 — ʿĀʾishah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ died after we had eaten our fill of the two black things: dates and water.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Foods (5442) and Muslim in the Book of Asceticism and Heart-Softening Reports (2975:31), through Sufyān, from Manṣūr ibn Ṣafiyyah, from his mother, from ʿĀʾishah.

9535 — Al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr said: Do you not now possess whatever food and drink you desire? I saw your Prophet ﷺ when he could not even find enough inferior dates to fill his stomach.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Asceticism and Heart-Softening Reports (2977:34), through Abū al-Aḥwaṣ, from Simāk, who said: I heard al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr relate it.
Daqal refers to inferior-quality dates.

9536 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ spend an entire day writhing from hunger, unable to find even enough inferior dates to fill his stomach.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Asceticism (2978:36), through Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar, from Shuʿbah, from Simāk ibn Ḥarb, who said: I heard al-Nuʿmān delivering a sermon; he mentioned what ʿUmar said concerning what people had obtained of worldly prosperity, then related it.

9537 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿĀbis reported from his father, who said: I asked ʿĀʾishah, “Did the Prophet ﷺ prohibit eating the meat of sacrificial animals after three days?”
She replied, “He did so only during a year in which the people suffered hunger. He wanted the wealthy to feed the poor. We would sometimes preserve a sheep’s leg and eat it after fifteen days.”
She was asked, “What compelled you to preserve it for so long?”
She laughed and said, “The family of Muḥammad ﷺ never ate their fill of wheat bread accompanied by another food for three consecutive days before he returned to Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Foods (5423) and Muslim in the Book of Asceticism (1055:23), both through Sufyān al-Thawrī, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿĀbis. The wording is al-Bukhārī’s; Muslim transmitted only ʿĀʾishah’s statement, “The family of Muḥammad never ate their fill…”

9538 — Ibn ʿAbbās said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would spend several consecutive nights hungry, while his family could find nothing for their evening meal. Most of the bread they ate was barley bread.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2360), Ibn Mājah (3347), and Imām Aḥmad (2303).

9539 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: A tailor invited the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to a meal he had prepared. I accompanied the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to that meal. The tailor served him barley bread and a stew containing gourd.
Anas said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ searching for the pieces of gourd around the dish, and I have continued to love gourd ever since that day.
Another narration adds that Anas said: Whenever a meal was prepared for me after that, and it was possible to include gourd in it, gourd was added.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (51), al-Bukhārī (5439), and Muslim (2041:144). The additional wording was also narrated by Muslim (145).

9540 — Anas said: I saw the Prophet ﷺ being served a stew containing gourd and dried meat. I saw him searching for the pieces of gourd and eating them.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5437) and Muslim (2041:144); the wording is al-Bukhārī’s.
Qadīd is meat cut lengthwise, salted, and dried in the air and sunlight.

9541 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The Prophet ﷺ loved gourd. A meal was brought to him—or he was invited to one—and I began searching for the pieces of gourd and placing them before him because I knew that he loved it.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (13894) and al-Nasāʾī in Al-Sunan al-Kubrā (6630).

9542 — Ḥakīm ibn Jābir reported from his father, who said: I entered the Prophet’s ﷺ house and saw some gourd in his possession. I asked, “What is this?”
He replied, “This is pumpkin—it is gourd. We use it to increase the quantity of our food.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (3304), Aḥmad (19101), and al-Nasāʾī in Al-Sunan al-Kubrā (6631).

9543 — Anas reported: Umm Sulaym sent me to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with a basket of fresh dates. I did not find him at home, as he had recently gone to visit one of his freedmen who had invited him and prepared a meal for him.
I reached him while he was eating, and he invited me to eat with him. The man had prepared tharīd with meat and gourd. I noticed that the Prophet ﷺ liked the gourd, so I began gathering the pieces and moving them nearer to him.
After we finished eating, the Prophet ﷺ returned home. I placed the basket of dates before him, and he began eating from them and distributing them until none remained.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (3303) and Imām Aḥmad (12052).

9544 — ʿĀʾishah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ loved sweet foods and honey.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5431, 5682) and Muslim (1474:21).
The Arabic word ḥalwāʾ may be pronounced in an extended form or in the shortened form ḥalwā. It may refer to anything sweet that is eaten. Al-Khaṭṭābī said that the term ḥalwā properly applies only to a sweet food that has been prepared or processed.

9545 — Abū Hurayrah reported:
We were with the Prophet ﷺ at a meal to which he had been invited. A foreleg was presented to him—and he liked the foreleg—so he took a bite from it… the remainder of the lengthy hadith concerning intercession.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3340) and Muslim (194/327). 

The phrase “he took a bite from it” means that he took it with the edges of his teeth.

9546 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ most loved the foreleg among the meat-bearing bones, particularly the foreleg of a sheep. Poison had been placed in a foreleg, and he believed that the Jews were the ones who had poisoned it.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3780, 3781), al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (161), and Aḥmad (3733, 3777), whose wording this is.
The word ʿirāq is the plural of ʿarq, meaning a bone upon which some meat remains.

9547 — Ḍubāʿah bint al-Zubayr ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib slaughtered a sheep in her home. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent someone to her with the request, “Give us some of your sheep to eat.”
She told the messenger, “By Allah, nothing remains with us except the neck, and I feel embarrassed to send the neck to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
The messenger returned and informed the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He said, “Return to her and say: ‘Send it, for the neck leads the sheep, is the part nearest to goodness, and is farthest removed from harm.’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (27031), al-Nasāʾī in Al-Sunan al-Kubrā (6624), and ʿAlī ibn Ḥujr al-Saʿdī in his Ḥadīth collection (377).
The expression “it leads the sheep” means that it is at the front of the animal.
“It is nearest to goodness” means that its meat is among the most flavorful and readily cooked.
“It is farthest removed from harm” means that it is farthest from the animal’s front and rear passages and their waste.
The fact that the Prophet ﷺ sent this request to her demonstrates their close relationship and affection, for Ḍubāʿah was his paternal cousin. This is therefore not regarded as begging.

9548 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib said: I saw the Prophet ﷺ eating fresh dates with cucumber.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5440) and Muslim (2043).
Qiththāʾ is a type of melon resembling a cucumber, but longer.

9549 — ʿĀʾishah reported that the Prophet ﷺ used to eat melon with fresh dates.
Another narration adds that he would say, “We temper the heat of this with the coolness of that, and the coolness of that with the heat of this.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3836), al-Tirmidhī (1843), and al-Nasāʾī in Al-Sunan al-Kubrā (6687).

9550 — Anas said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ eating fresh dates together with melon.
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (12449), al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (201), and al-Nasāʾī in Al-Sunan al-Kubrā (6692).

9551 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: “Whoever eats garlic or onions should remain away from us—or remain away from our mosque—and sit in his house.”
A cooking pot containing various green vegetables was brought to him. He noticed its odor and asked about it, and he was informed which vegetables it contained. He said, “Bring it near,” directing that it be given to one of his Companions.
When the Companion saw that the Prophet ﷺ disliked eating it, the Prophet ﷺ said to him, “Eat, for I hold intimate conversation with those with whom you do not converse.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (855) and Muslim (564:73); the wording is Muslim’s.

9552 — Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī said: Whenever food was brought to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, he would eat some of it and send the remainder to me. One day he sent me a dish from which he had eaten nothing because it contained garlic.
I asked him, “Is it forbidden?”
He replied, “No, but I dislike it because of its odor.”
I said, “Then I dislike whatever you dislike.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Drinks (2053:170), through Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar, from Shuʿbah, from Simāk ibn Ḥarb, from Jābir ibn Samurah, from Abū Ayyūb al-Anṣārī.

9553 — Jābir ibn Samurah reported: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ stayed in the home of Abū Ayyūb. Whenever he ate a meal, he would send its remainder to Abū Ayyūb.
One day he sent him a dish from which the Prophet ﷺ had not eaten. When Abū Ayyūb came to the Prophet ﷺ, he mentioned this to him.
The Prophet ﷺ explained, “It contained garlic.”
Abū Ayyūb asked, “Messenger of Allah, is it forbidden?”
He replied, “No, but I dislike it because of its odor.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1807), ʿAbdullāh ibn Imām Aḥmad (20897), and Ibn Ḥibbān (5110).

9554 — Umm Ayyūb reported that the Prophet ﷺ stayed in their home. They specially prepared a meal for him containing some of these strong-smelling vegetables, but he disliked eating it.
He said to his Companions, “Eat it, for I am not like any of you. I fear that I may offend my companion.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1810), Ibn Mājah (3364), Imām Aḥmad (27422), and Ibn Khuzaymah (1671).

9555 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported that Khālid ibn al-Walīd, who was known as the Sword of Allah, informed him that he entered the home of Maymūnah with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. She was the maternal aunt of both Khālid and Ibn ʿAbbās.
They found a roasted desert lizard in her home, which her sister Ḥufaydah bint al-Ḥārith had brought from Najd. The desert lizard was presented to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He rarely extended his hand toward food before being informed what it was and hearing its name.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ reached toward the desert lizard, whereupon one of the women present said, “Tell the Messenger of Allah ﷺ what you have presented to him. It is a desert lizard, Messenger of Allah.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then withdrew his hand. Khālid ibn al-Walīd asked, “Is the desert lizard forbidden, Messenger of Allah?”
He replied, “No, but it was not found in the land of my people, so I find myself disinclined to eat it.”
Khālid said: I pulled it toward myself and ate it while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ watched me.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5391) and Muslim (1946:44).

9556 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported that the Prophet ﷺ was asked, “Which drink is most pleasant?”
He replied, “That which is sweet and cold.”
Good through supporting narrations: Narrated by Aḥmad (3129).

9557 — Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ would drink in three breaths.
Another narration adds that he would say, “It is more thirst-quenching, healthier, and more wholesome.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Drinks (5631) and Muslim in the Book of Drinks (2028:122), both through ʿAzrah ibn Thābit, from Thumāmah ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn Anas, from Anas. The additional wording is narrated by Muslim (2028:123) through Abū ʿIṣām from Anas.
Al-Bukhārī also records: “Anas would take two or three breaths while drinking.”
This is understood to mean that he sometimes drank in two breaths due to particular circumstances, while drinking in three breaths was his regular and preferred practice.
Other narrations prohibit breathing into the vessel. This prohibition refers to breathing inside the vessel so that the drink does not become contaminated or unpleasant. The report of Anas, may Allah be pleased with him means that the Prophet ﷺ took three breaths while drinking, removing the vessel from his mouth each time before breathing. This is supported by his statement at the end of the hadith: “It is more thirst-quenching, healthier, and more wholesome.”
“More thirst-quenching” means that it satisfies thirst more fully. “More wholesome” means that it is more pleasant and easier to digest. “Healthier” means that it is more beneficial to the body’s well-being.

9558 — Ibn ʿAbbās said: I gave the Messenger of Allah ﷺ some Zamzam water, and he drank it while standing.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1637, 5617) and Muslim (2027:117–118).
The reports prohibiting drinking while standing are understood as indicating that it is disliked rather than strictly forbidden.

9559 — ʿAlī performed the noon prayer and then sat in the courtyard of Kūfah attending to the people’s needs until the time for the afternoon prayer arrived. Water was brought to him, and he drank some of it. He washed his face and hands, and the narrator also mentioned his head and feet. He then stood and drank the remaining water while standing.
He said, “Some people dislike drinking while standing, but the Prophet ﷺ did exactly as I have done.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Drinks (5615, 5616), through routes from ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Maysarah, who said: I heard al-Nazzāl ibn Sabrah relate it from ʿAlī.

9560 — Kabshah al-Anṣāriyyah said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ entered my home and drank while standing from the mouth of a hanging waterskin. I then went to its mouth and cut it off to preserve it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (1892) and Al-Shamāʾil (214), Aḥmad (27448), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5318).

9561 — Umm Sulaym reported that the Prophet ﷺ drank while standing from the mouth of a waterskin.
Good: Narrated by al-Dārimī (2170), al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (215), and Aḥmad (27115).

9562 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ drink both while standing and while sitting.
Hasan Sahih: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (1883) and Al-Shamāʾil (209), Abū Dāwūd (653), Ibn Mājah (931), and Aḥmad (6660), all through ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb, from his father, from his grandfather ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr.

9563 — ʿĀʾishah said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ drink both while standing and while sitting, pray both while wearing sandals and while barefoot, and turn after completing the prayer sometimes toward his right and sometimes toward his left.
Good: Narrated by al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Muʿjam al-Awsaṭ (1235), from Aḥmad, from Yaḥyā ibn Ḥakīm al-Muqaddam, from Makhlad ibn Yazīd al-Ḥarrānī, from Yaḥyā ibn Saʿīd al-Anṣārī, from ʿAṭāʾ, from ʿĀʾishah, who related the hadith.

9564 — Kaʿb ibn Mālik reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to eat with three fingers and lick them after finishing.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Drinks (2032:132), from Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn Numayr, from his father, from Hishām, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Saʿd, who reported from Kaʿb ibn Mālik through one of his sons.

9565 — Anas reported that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ ate, he would lick his three fingers. He said:
“If a morsel belonging to one of you falls, let him remove any dirt from it and eat it, and not leave it for Satan.”
He also instructed them to wipe the dish clean, saying:
“You do not know in which portion of your food the blessing lies.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2034:136).

9566 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Prophet ﷺ said: “When one of you eats, let him lick his fingers, for he does not know upon which of them the blessing may be found.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2035:137).

9567 — Anas ibn Mālik said: I saw the Prophet ﷺ sitting in a squatting posture while eating dates.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2044:148).
The squatting posture described here means sitting upon the buttocks with the lower legs raised upright.

9568 — Jābir ibn Samurah said: I entered the Prophet’s ﷺ home and saw him reclining upon a cushion.
Some narrators add: He was reclining upon his left side.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4143), al-Tirmidhī (2771), and Aḥmad (20975). The additional wording was narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (2770) and Al-Shamāʾil (123).

9569 — Anas said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ possessed a container or blend of perfume from which he would apply fragrance.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4162), al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (218), and Ibn Saʿd (1/399).

9570 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Shikhkhīr reported that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wore a new garment, he would say:
“O Allah, I ask You for its goodness and the goodness of the purpose for which it was made, and I seek refuge in You from its evil and the evil of the purpose for which it was made.”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in Al-Sunan al-Kubrā (10069) and ʿAmal al-Yawm wa-al-Laylah (310).

9571 — ʿImrān ibn Abī Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ used antimony kohl, applying it three times to his right eye and twice to his left.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah (23953) in disconnected form. Abū al-Shaykh narrated it with a connected chain in Akhlāq al-Nabī ﷺ (p. 147).

9572 — ʿUqbah ibn ʿĀmir said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ prohibited cauterization and disliked drinking extremely hot water. When applying kohl, he applied it an odd number of times, and when cleansing himself with stones, he used an odd number.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17426).
The expression “he applied kohl an odd number of times” may have two meanings. First, that he applied an odd number to each eye. Second, that the combined total applied to both eyes was odd, as stated in the hadith of Anas.

9573 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Zayd ibn ʿĀṣim saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ lying on his back in the mosque with one leg resting upon the other.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (93), al-Bukhārī (475, 5969), and Muslim (2100:75).

9574 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the courtyard of the Kaʿbah, sitting with his knees drawn up and held by his hands in this manner.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Seeking Permission (6272), through Muḥammad ibn Abī Ghālib → Ibrāhīm ibn al-Mundhir → Muḥammad ibn Fulayḥ → his father → Nāfiʿ → Ibn ʿUmar.
Al-Bukhārī said: Drawing the knees up and holding them with the hands is a squatting posture.
Iḥtibāʾ means that a person draws his legs toward his abdomen and binds them together with his back using a cloth, or holds them in this position with his hands.

9575 — Abū Hurayrah said: Whenever I saw al-Ḥasan, my eyes overflowed with tears. This was because the Prophet ﷺ once came out and found me in the mosque. He took me by the hand, and I accompanied him. He did not speak to me until we reached the marketplace of Banū Qaynuqāʿ. He walked around it and looked about, then departed while I accompanied him until we returned to the mosque.
He sat with his knees drawn up and said, “Where is the little one? Call the little one to me.”
Al-Ḥasan came running and fell into the Prophet’s ﷺ lap. Al-Ḥasan placed his hand in the Prophet’s beard, and the Prophet ﷺ opened his mouth and placed al-Ḥasan’s mouth against his own.
He then said, “O Allah, I love him, so love him, and love whoever loves him.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (10891), al-Bukhārī in Al-Adab al-Mufrad (1183), and Abū Nuʿaym in Al-Ḥilyah (2/35).

9576 — Qaylah bint Makhramah reported that she saw the Prophet ﷺ sitting in a squatting posture. She said: When I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sitting with such humility and submissiveness, I began trembling with awe.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4847), al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (120), and al-Bukhārī in Al-Adab al-Mufrad (1178).
The squatting posture described here means that a man sits upon his buttocks, draws his thighs against his abdomen, and places his hands upon his lower legs.
“Submissive in his sitting” means humble and modest.
“I began trembling with awe” means that she was overtaken by trembling due to fear and reverence.

9577 — Abū Qatādah said: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was traveling and stopped to rest during the night, he would lie upon his right side. If he stopped shortly before dawn, he would raise his forearm and place his head upon his palm.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (683:313).
The word ʿarrasa means that he stopped and encamped. Taʿrīs refers to stopping for rest at any time.

9578 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that when the Messenger of Allah ﷺ retired to bed, he would say:
“All praise belongs to Allah, who has fed us, given us drink, sufficed us, and granted us shelter. How many are those who have no one to suffice them and no one to shelter them!”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2715:64).

9579 — ʿĀʾishah reported that every night when the Prophet ﷺ retired to bed, he would bring his palms together and blow lightly into them. He would recite into them:
“Say: He is Allah, the One,”
 “Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of daybreak,” and
 “Say: I seek refuge in the Lord of humanity.”
He would then wipe with his hands as much of his body as he could, beginning with his head, face, and the front of his body. He would do this three times.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Virtues of the Qurʾān (5017), through Qutaybah ibn Saʿīd → al-Mufaḍḍal → ʿUqayl → Ibn Shihāb → ʿUrwah → ʿĀʾishah.

9580 — ʿĀʾishah was asked, “What did the Prophet ﷺ do in his home?”
She replied, “He occupied himself with the work of his family”—meaning that he served them—“and when the time for prayer arrived, he went out to pray.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of the Call to Prayer (676), Expenditures (5363), and Manners (6039), through Shuʿbah → al-Ḥakam ibn ʿUtaybah → Ibrāhīm → al-Aswad ibn Yazīd → ʿĀʾishah. Its fuller detail appears in the following hadith.

9581 — ʿUrwah reported: A man asked ʿĀʾishah, “Did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ perform any work in his home?”
She replied, “Yes. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ mended his sandals, sewed his clothes, and worked in his home just as any of you works in his own home.”
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (25341) through ʿAbd al-Razzāq, and it is recorded in ʿAbd al-Razzāq’s Muṣannaf (20492).

9582 — ʿĀʾishah was asked, “What work did the Messenger of Allah ﷺ perform in his home?”
She replied, “He was only a human being like other human beings. He removed vermin from his clothes, milked his sheep, and served himself.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in Al-Adab al-Mufrad (541), al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (335), and Abū Yaʿlā (4873).

9583 — Anas ibn Mālik was asked, “How was the Prophet’s ﷺ recitation?”
He replied, “He lengthened the appropriate sounds.” He then recited:
“In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful,”
lengthening the recitation of “In the name of Allah,” “the Most Compassionate,” and “the Most Merciful.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Virtues of the Qurʾān (5046), through ʿAmr ibn ʿĀṣim → Hammām → Qatādah → Anas.

9584 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to him, “Recite the Qur’an to me.”
Ibn Masʿūd asked, “Should I recite it to you when it was revealed to you?”
He replied, “I desire to hear it from someone other than myself.”
I recited Sūrat al-Nisāʾ until I reached:
“How will it be when We bring a witness from every nation and bring you as a witness against these people?”
 [Al-Nisāʾ: 41]
I raised my head—or a man beside me nudged me, so I raised my head—and saw tears flowing from the Prophet’s ﷺ eyes.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5055) and Muslim (800:247).

9585 — ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ would converse with Abū Bakr at night concerning an affair of the Muslims, and I would be present with them.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (169) and Aḥmad (175); authenticated by Ibn Khuzaymah (1156).

9586 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Prophet ﷺ said: “If I were invited to a meal consisting only of a foreleg or a lower leg, I would accept the invitation; and if a foreleg or lower leg were presented to me as a gift, I would accept it.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3568, 5178).
The kurāʿ is the thin lower portion of an animal’s leg that contains very little meat.

9587 — ʿĀʾishah reported that when the Prophet ﷺ spoke, his words were so clear and measured that anyone wishing to count them could have done so.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3567) and Muslim (2493:71).

9588 — Anas reported that when the Prophet ﷺ said something, he repeated it three times so that it would be properly understood. When he came to a people and greeted them, he would offer the greeting three times.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Knowledge (95), through ʿAbdah ibn ʿAbdullāh → ʿAbd al-Ṣamad → ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Muthannā → Thumāmah ibn ʿAbdullāh → Anas.
The statement that he greeted them three times refers to occasions when he sought permission to enter.

9589 — ʿĀʾishah said to ʿUrwah: Are you not surprised by Abū Hurayrah? He came and sat beside my chamber, relating hadiths from the Prophet ﷺ loudly enough for me to hear. I was performing voluntary prayer, and he departed before I completed it. Had I reached him, I would have corrected him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ did not relate hadiths rapidly in the manner that you do.
Another narration adds: Rather, he spoke distinctly, with clear pauses between his words, so that anyone sitting with him could memorize what he said.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2493:160).
The additional wording was narrated and authenticated by al-Tirmidhī (3639), and narrated by Aḥmad (25077, 26209).

9590 — Anas said: The garment most beloved to the Prophet ﷺ was the ḥibarah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Clothing (5813) and Muslim in the Book of Clothing and Adornment (2079:33), both through Muʿādh ibn Hishām → his father → Qatādah → Anas.
A ḥibarah is a type of Yemeni mantle made from linen or cotton and decorated with woven stripes or patterns.

9591 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib said: I never saw anyone look more beautiful in a red outfit than the Prophet ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Clothing (5901) and Muslim in the Book of Virtues (2337:92), both through Abū Isḥāq from al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib.

9592 — Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah said: I was traveling with the Prophet ﷺ when he said, “Mughīrah, take the water vessel.”
I took it and accompanied him. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ walked away until he was concealed from my sight and relieved himself. He then returned wearing a Syrian robe with narrow sleeves. He tried to bring his hands out through the sleeves, but they were too narrow, so he brought his hands out from underneath the robe.
I poured water for him, and he performed the same ablution he would perform for prayer. He then wiped over his leather socks and prayed.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Prayer (363) and Muslim in the Book of Purification (274:77), both through Abū Muʿāwiyah → al-Aʿmash → Muslim → Masrūq → al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah.
Al-Tirmidhī’s narration (1768) describes it as “a Byzantine robe.” This does not contradict the description “Syrian robe,” because the Byzantines then governed Syria, and the robe could have been made by either Syrians or Byzantines.

9593 — ʿĀʾishah said: The Prophet ﷺ went out one morning wearing a broad, patterned cloak made from black hair.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Clothing (2081:36), through routes from Zakariyyā ibn Abī Zāʾidah → Muṣʿab ibn Shaybah → Ṣafiyyah bint Shaybah → ʿĀʾishah.
A mirṭ is a long, broad cloak made from silk-blend fabric, wool, hair, or linen and wrapped around the body.
The word muraḥḥal means that it bore patterns resembling camel saddles, or that it contained decorative stripes.

9594 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Prophet ﷺ was ill, so he came out leaning upon Usāmah ibn Zayd. He was wearing a Qaṭarī garment draped over his shoulders, and he led the people in prayer.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (128), Aḥmad (13762), and Abū al-Shaykh in Akhlāq al-Nabī ﷺ (p. 90).
A Qaṭarī garment was either a type of Yemeni cotton mantle containing red patterns and stripes, or a fine garment imported from a settlement in Bahrain called Qaṭar.
“He draped it over himself” means that he placed it over his shoulders.

9595 — Qurrah ibn Iyās said: I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ with a group from Muzaynah, and we pledged allegiance to him. The buttons of his shirt were open. After pledging allegiance to him, I placed my hand inside the opening of his shirt and touched the Seal of Prophethood.
ʿUrwah said: After that, I never saw Muʿāwiyah or his son with the buttons of their shirts fastened, whether during winter or hot weather. They always left their buttons open.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4082), Ibn Mājah (3578), al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (57), Aḥmad (15581), and Ibn Ḥibbān (5452).

9596 — Umm Salamah said: The garment most beloved to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was the shirt.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4025), al-Tirmidhī (1764) and in Al-Shamāʾil (54), and al-Nasāʾī in Al-Sunan al-Kubrā (9668).

9597 — Anas said: The sleeves of the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ shirt reached his wrists.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (13/449) and Abū al-Shaykh (p. 91).

9598 — Asmāʾ bint Yazīd said: The sleeve of the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ shirt reached his wrist.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4027), al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (1765), and in Al-Shamāʾil (56).
The narration of Abū al-Shaykh (p. 91), transmitted from him by al-Baghawī (3073), states: “The sleeve of the Prophet’s ﷺ shirt extended below the wrist.”
This may describe a different shirt, with Asmāʾ bint Yazīd describing two shirts on two separate occasions.
The rusgh is the joint at which the hand ends and the forearm begins. The Arabic forms raṣgh and rusgh are both used.

9599 — Anas reported that each of the Prophet’s ﷺ sandals had two straps.
Another wording states: Anas brought out for us a pair of hairless leather sandals, each possessing two straps. Thābit al-Bunānī later narrated to me from Anas that they were the sandals of the Prophet ﷺ.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5857). The alternate wording was also narrated by al-Bukhārī (3107).
The qibāl is the sandal strap passing between the toes.
The description “hairless” means that no animal hair remained upon the leather.

9600 — Ibn ʿAbbās said: Each sandal of the Prophet ﷺ had two straps, with its thong doubled.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (72) and Ibn Mājah (3614).

9601 — Ibn ʿUmar was asked:
“O Abū ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, I have seen you do four things that I have not seen any of your companions do.”
He said:
“What are they, Ibn Jurayj?”
He mentioned them, including:
“I have seen you wearing sabtī sandals.”
Ibn ʿUmar replied:
“As for the sabtī sandals, I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wearing sandals made from leather from which the hair had been removed, and he would perform ablution while wearing them. Therefore, I love to wear them”… the remainder of the hadith.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik in the Book of Ḥajj (33), al-Bukhārī (5851), and Muslim (1187/25).

9602 — Abū Hurayrah said: Each sandal of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had two straps.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (75) and al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Muʿjam al-Ṣaghīr (254).

9603 — Anas ibn Mālik reported: When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ intended to write to the Byzantines, he was informed, “They do not read a letter unless it bears a seal.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ therefore had a silver signet ring made. It is as though I can still see its whiteness upon the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ hand. Its inscription was:
Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2938, 5875) and Muslim (2092:56).
Other wordings state: “When he intended to write to the non-Arabs,” and: “to Chosroes, Caesar, and the Negus.”

9604 — Ibn ʿUmar said: The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had a silver signet ring made. It remained in his possession, then afterward with Abū Bakr, then with ʿUmar, and then with ʿUthmān, until it fell into the well of Arīs. Its inscription read:
Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5873) and Muslim (2091:54).
A narration recorded by al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (83) and Aḥmad (5366) states: “He used it to seal documents but did not wear it.” Its chain is authentic.
This does not contradict the report in the two Ṣaḥīḥ collections. It may be understood as describing different occasions: he sometimes wore the ring and sometimes left it unworn. The narration is therefore not anomalous.

9605 — Ibn ʿUmar reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had a ring made from gold—or silver—and positioned its stone toward the palm of his hand. He had the words “Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah” engraved upon it.
The people began making similar rings. When he saw that they had done so, he threw his ring away and said, “I shall never wear it again.” He then had a silver ring made, and the people likewise began wearing silver rings.
Ibn ʿUmar said: After the Prophet ﷺ, Abū Bakr wore the ring, followed by ʿUmar and then ʿUthmān, until it fell from ʿUthmān into the well of Arīs.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5866), whose wording this is, and Muslim (2091:54).
Muslim (2091:55) also narrated through another chain from Ibn ʿUmar:
The Prophet ﷺ had a gold ring made and then discarded it. He afterward had a silver ring made and engraved upon it: “Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.” He said, “No one should engrave an inscription identical to the inscription upon my ring.”
When he wore it, he positioned its stone toward the inner part of his palm. This was the ring that fell from Muʿayqīb into the well of Arīs.
Arīs was a well located near the mosque of Qubāʾ. It is now situated within the mosque’s courtyards.
Muʿayqīb embraced Islam at an early stage, participated at Badr, and migrated to Abyssinia. He was entrusted with the Prophet’s ﷺ ring, and Abū Bakr, ʿUmar, and ʿUthmān appointed him to oversee the public treasury.

9606 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that he saw a silver ring upon the hand of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ for one day. The people then had silver rings made and wore them. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ discarded his ring, so the people discarded theirs as well.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5868) and Muslim (2093:59).
The wording “a silver ring,” followed by the statement that the Prophet ﷺ and the people discarded their rings, appears to conflict with Ibn ʿUmar’s hadith stating that the silver ring remained after the Prophet ﷺ with Abū Bakr, then ʿUmar, and then ʿUthmān until it fell into the well. Scholars therefore said that an error occurred in the words “a silver ring,” and that the correct wording is “a gold ring.”

9607 — Anas ibn Mālik said: The signet ring of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was made of silver, and its stone was Abyssinian.
Another narration states: Its stone was made from the same silver as the ring.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2094:61) and al-Bukhārī (5870).
Al-Ḥāfiẓ said: There is no contradiction between the two reports. They may refer to more than one ring, or the stone may have been attributed to Abyssinia because of one of its characteristics, such as its craftsmanship or engraving.

9608 — Anas reported that when Abū Bakr became caliph, he sent him to Bahrain and wrote a document for him, which he sealed with the signet ring.
The ring’s inscription consisted of three lines:
Muḥammad — on one line
Messenger — on one line
Allah — on one line
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of the One-Fifth (3106) and Clothing (5878), through Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Anṣārī → his father → Thumāmah → Anas.

9609 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wore a silver ring on his right hand. It contained an Abyssinian stone, which he positioned toward the palm of his hand.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Clothing (2094:62), through Ṭalḥah ibn Yaḥyā al-Anṣārī al-Zuraqī → Yūnus → Ibn Shihāb → Anas.

9610 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib reported that the Prophet ﷺ used to wear his ring on his right hand.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4226), al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (90), al-Nasāʾī (5203), Abū al-Shaykh in Akhlāq al-Nabī (p. 110), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (5501).

9611 — Ibn ʿAbbās said: I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wearing his ring on his right hand.
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4229), al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (1742), whose wording this is, and in Al-Shamāʾil (94), and Abū al-Shaykh in Akhlāq al-Nabī (p. 108).

9612 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Jaʿfar said: The Prophet ﷺ used to wear his ring on his right hand.
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī in his Sunan (1744) and Al-Shamāʾil (91), al-Nasāʾī (5204), and Aḥmad (1746, 1755).

9613 — Anas said: The Prophet’s ﷺ ring was worn upon this finger, and he pointed to the little finger of his left hand.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Clothing (2095:63), through Abū Bakr ibn Khallād al-Bāhilī → ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mahdī → Ḥammād ibn Salamah → Thābit → Anas.
The reports concerning the right and left hands are understood as referring to different occasions. The Prophet ﷺ sometimes wore his ring on his right hand and sometimes on his left. This reconciliation is preferable to declaring one of the reports weak.

9614 — Anas reported that the Prophet ﷺ had a silver signet ring made and engraved upon it:
Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.
He said to the people: “I have had a silver signet ring made and engraved upon it, ‘Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.’ No one should engrave an inscription identical to its inscription.”
Anas said: It is as though I can still see its gleam upon his little finger.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5874, 5876) and Muslim (2092).

9615 — Abū Umāmah ibn Sahl said: The pommel of the Messenger of Allah’s ﷺ sword was made of silver.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (5373).
The sword’s qabīʿah is the silver or iron fitting located at the end of its hilt.

9616 — Al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām said: The Prophet ﷺ was wearing two coats of armor on the day of Uḥud. He attempted to climb onto a rock but was unable to do so, so Ṭalḥah crouched beneath him. The Prophet ﷺ stepped upon him until he stood firmly upon the rock.
Al-Zubayr said: I heard the Prophet ﷺ say, “Ṭalḥah has secured Paradise.”
Good: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (1692, 3738), Aḥmad (1417), and Ibn Ḥibbān (6979).
The statement “Ṭalḥah has made it obligatory” means that he performed a deed that made Paradise obligatory for him.
Other hadiths on this subject have been included in the Book of Jihad and Military Campaigns.

9617 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported that the Prophet ﷺ entered Makkah wearing a black turban while not in the state of iḥrām.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1358:451).

9618 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came out wearing a cloak wrapped around his shoulders and a dark turban… the remainder of the hadith concerning the virtues of the Anṣār.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Virtues (3800).

The word ʿiṣābah means a turban.
The word dasmāʾ refers to something darkened by the oiliness of his hair from perfume; what is intended here is that the turban was black.

9619 — Abū Juḥayfah reported:
I came to the Prophet ﷺ in Makkah while he was at al-Abṭaḥ in a red leather tent. Bilāl came out carrying the water the Prophet ﷺ had used for ablution; some people obtained some of it, while others received drops sprinkled upon them.
Then the Prophet ﷺ came out wearing a red garment, and it is as though I can still see the whiteness of his legs… the remainder of the hadith.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3566) and Muslim (503:249).

9620 — Abū Burdah said: I entered upon ʿĀʾishah, and she brought out for us a coarse waist-wrapper made in Yemen and a cloak of the type known as mulabbadah. She swore by Allah that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died while wearing these two garments.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5818) and Muslim (2080:34); the wording is Muslim’s.
Mulabbadah means either a patched cloak or a thick garment that had become like felt.

9621 — ʿIkrimah reported that he saw Ibn ʿAbbās wearing his waist-wrapper with its front edge resting upon the tops of his feet while raising it higher at the back.
I asked, “Why do you wear your waist-wrapper in this manner?”
He replied, “I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wear his waist-wrapper in this manner.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4096), Ibn Saʿd (1/459), and Abū al-Shaykh in Akhlāq al-Nabī ﷺ (p. 97).

9622 — ʿĀṣim al-Aḥwal said: I saw the Prophet’s ﷺ cup in the possession of Anas ibn Mālik. It had cracked, so Anas bound it with silver. It was a fine, broad cup made from naḍār wood.
Anas said: I served the Messenger of Allah ﷺ drink from this cup on more occasions than I could count.
Ibn Sīrīn said: The cup had an iron ring upon it. Anas wished to replace it with a ring of gold or silver, but Abū Ṭalḥah said to him, “Do not alter anything made by the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.” Anas therefore left it unchanged.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (5638).
Naḍār refers to pure, select wood. It has also been said to be the finest wood used for making vessels.

9623 — Anas said: I served the Messenger of Allah ﷺ every kind of drink from this cup of mine: honey, nabīdh, water, and milk.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2008:89).
Nabīdh here means water in which dates were placed to sweeten it. Dates would be placed in the water at the beginning of the night, and he would drink it the following morning.

9624 — ʿĀʾishah said: The mattress upon which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ slept was made of leather and stuffed with palm fibers.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6456) and Muslim (2082:38).
Another wording states, “The bedding of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ,” instead of, “The mattress of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”

9625 — ʿĀʾishah said: The cushion upon which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ reclined was made of leather and stuffed with palm fibers.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in The Book of Clothing (2082:37).

9626 — Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī said: I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ while he was cleaning his teeth with a tooth-stick held in his hand. He was making the sound, “Aʿ, aʿ,” while the tooth-stick was in his mouth, as though he were retching.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (244) and Muslim (254); this is al-Bukhārī’s wording.
Muslim’s wording states: “I entered upon the Prophet ﷺ while the end of the tooth-stick was upon his tongue.”
The word “retching” refers to the motion preceding vomiting. In this context, it means drawing phlegm up from the deepest part of the throat and bringing it out so that it may be expelled, resembling the action of someone about to vomit.

9627 — ʿĀʾishah reported that whenever the Prophet ﷺ entered his home, he began by using the tooth-stick.
Another narration states: Shurayḥ said: I asked ʿĀʾishah, “What did the Prophet ﷺ begin with when he entered his home?”
She replied, “The tooth-stick.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (253).

9628 — Ḥudhayfah ibn al-Yamān said: When the Prophet ﷺ rose during the night, he would vigorously clean his mouth with the tooth-stick.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (245) and Muslim (255).
In the narration of Ḥuṣayn ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān from Abū Wāʾil recorded by Muslim: “When he rose to perform the night prayer, he would vigorously clean his mouth with the tooth-stick.”
Shawṣ means rubbing the teeth horizontally with the tooth-stick.

9629 — ʿĀʾishah, the wife of the Prophet ﷺ, reported that during the illness in which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ died, he repeatedly asked, “Where will I be tomorrow? Where will I be tomorrow?” eagerly awaiting ʿĀʾishah’s allotted day.
His wives permitted him to remain wherever he wished, so he stayed in ʿĀʾishah’s house until he died there.
ʿĀʾishah said: He died in my house on the day that had been allotted to me. Allah took his soul while his head rested between my upper chest and neck, and his saliva became mixed with mine.
ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr entered carrying a tooth-stick with which he was cleaning his teeth. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ looked at it, so I said, “ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, give me that tooth-stick.”
He gave it to me. I bit and softened it, then gave it to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He cleaned his teeth with it while leaning against my chest.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4450) and Muslim (2443:84).

9630 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ said: I led the gray mule carrying the Prophet of Allah ﷺ, al-Ḥasan, and al-Ḥusayn until I brought them into the Prophet’s ﷺ chamber. One of the two boys was seated in front of him and the other behind him.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2423:60).

9631 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“I was granted victory by the east wind, while ʿĀd was destroyed by the west wind.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4105) and Muslim (900:17).
The word al-ṣabā—with the letter ṣād pronounced with fatḥah and the bāʾ without gemination—means the east wind. Al-dabūr means the west wind.

9632 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I was sent with concise, comprehensive speech; I was granted victory through fear; and while I was asleep, the keys to the treasures of the earth were brought to me and placed in my hand.”
Abū Hurayrah said: “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has departed, while you are extracting those treasures.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2977) and Muslim (523:6).
Abū Hurayrah’s words, “The Messenger of Allah ﷺ has departed, while you are extracting them,” mean that they were bringing forth the contents of those treasures.

9633 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I have been given what none of the prophets was given.”
We asked, “What is it, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“I was granted victory through fear; I was given the keys of the earth; I was named Aḥmad; the earth was made a means of purification for me; and my nation was made the best of nations.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (763) and Ibn Abī Shaybah (11/434).

9634 — ʿAmr ibn Shuʿayb reported from his father, from his grandfather, that during the campaign of Tabūk, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stood in prayer one night. Some of his Companions gathered behind him to guard him. When he had finished praying and turned toward them, he said:
“Tonight I was granted five things that no one before me was granted. I was sent to all humanity, whereas those before me were sent only to their own people. I was granted victory over the enemy through fear: even if a month’s journey separated me from them, they would be filled with fear of me. The spoils of war were made lawful for me to consume, whereas those before me regarded consuming them as forbidden and would burn them. The earth was made for me a place of prayer and a means of purification; wherever the time for prayer reaches me, I perform dry ablution and pray. Those before me treated this matter differently and prayed only in their churches and synagogues.
“As for the fifth—and what a thing it is!—I was told: ‘Ask, for every prophet has made his request.’ So I deferred my request until the Day of Resurrection. It is for you and for everyone who bears witness that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (7068).

9635 — Abū Umāmah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“My Lord favored me over the prophets—or he said: over the nations—with four things: I was sent to all people; the whole earth was made a place of prayer and a means of purification for me and my nation, so wherever the time of prayer reaches a man from my nation, there he has his place of prayer and his means of purification; I was granted victory through fear cast into the hearts of my enemies from a month’s journey away; and the spoils of war were made lawful for us.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22137, 22209) and al-Tirmidhī (1553). The wording is that of Aḥmad.

9636 — Abū Dharr reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I was granted five things that no one before me was granted: I was sent to the red and the black; the earth was made a means of purification and a place of prayer for me; the spoils of war were made lawful for me, though they had not been lawful for anyone before me; I was granted victory through fear, so that an enemy a month’s journey away from me is struck with fear; and I was told, ‘Ask and you will be granted.’
“I reserved my supplication as intercession for my nation. It will reach—if Allah wills—those among you who do not associate anything with Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (489) and Aḥmad (21299, 21314); authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6462).

9637 — Al-Mughīrah ibn Shuʿbah said: I heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Telling a lie about me is unlike telling a lie about anyone else. Whoever deliberately lies about me, let him take his seat in the Fire.”
I also heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Whoever is lamented over is punished because of the lamentation made over him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1291) and Muslim (933:28).

9638 — ʿAlī reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not lie about me, for whoever lies about me shall enter the Fire.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (106) and Muslim in his Introduction (1).

9639 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said: I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“I am only a human being, and I stipulated with my Lord, Mighty and Majestic, that any Muslim servant whom I rebuke or insult should have that made a purification and reward for him.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2602:94).

9640 — Abū Hurayrah reported that he heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“O Allah, whichever believing servant I may have rebuked, make that a means of nearness to You for him on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6361) and Muslim (2601:92).

9641 — Abū Hurayrah said: I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“O Allah, Muhammad is only a human being who becomes angry as other human beings become angry. I have taken from You a covenant that You will never break: whichever believer I may have harmed, rebuked, or struck, make it an expiation for him and a means by which You bring him near to You on the Day of Resurrection.”
Another wording states:
“Make it a blessing, purification, and means of nearness by which You bring him close to You on the Day of Resurrection.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2601:91).

9642 — ʿĀʾishah said:
Two men entered upon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and spoke to him about something whose nature I do not know. They angered him, so he cursed and rebuked them. After they had left, I said, “O Messenger of Allah, no one has received as much good as these two have!”
He asked, “Why is that?”
I replied, “You cursed and rebuked them.”
He said:
“Do you not know the condition I made with my Lord? I said: ‘O Allah, I am only a human being, so whichever Muslim I curse or rebuke, make it a purification and reward for him.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2600).

9643 — Anas ibn Mālik reported:
There was an orphan girl in the care of Umm Sulaym, the mother of Anas. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ saw the orphan girl and said:
“Is it you? You have grown! May your age not increase!”
The orphan girl returned to Umm Sulaym weeping. Umm Sulaym asked, “What is wrong, my daughter?”
The girl replied, “The Prophet of Allah ﷺ supplicated against me that my age should not increase. Now my age will never increase”—or she said, “my lifetime.”
Umm Sulaym hurried out, hastily wrapping her head-covering, until she met the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He asked her:
“What is the matter, Umm Sulaym?”
She replied, “O Prophet of Allah, did you supplicate against my orphan girl?”
He asked, “What do you mean, Umm Sulaym?”
She said, “She claims that you supplicated that neither her age nor her lifetime should increase.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed and then said:
“O Umm Sulaym, do you not know the condition I made with my Lord? I stipulated with my Lord, saying: ‘I am only a human being. I am pleased as human beings are pleased, and I become angry as human beings become angry. So if I supplicate against anyone from my nation with a supplication he does not deserve, make it a purification, a blessing, and a means by which You bring him near to You on the Day of Resurrection.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2603).

9644 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do you think my attention is directed only toward my prayer direction here? By Allah, neither your humility nor your bowing is hidden from me. Indeed, I see you from behind my back.”
Authentic: Narrated by Mālik (399), al-Bukhārī (418), and Muslim (424:109).

9645 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Perform the bowing and prostration properly. By Allah, I see you behind me”—or he may have said, “from behind my back”—“when you bow and prostrate.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (742) and Muslim (425:110).
Al-Nawawī said in his commentary on Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim:
“The scholars said that this means Allah created for him ﷺ a faculty of perception in the back of his head by which he could see what was behind him. Even greater customary laws were miraculously suspended for him ﷺ. Neither reason nor revelation makes this impossible. Rather, revelation has conveyed it according to its apparent meaning, so it must be affirmed accordingly.”
Al-Qāḍī said:
“Aḥmad ibn Ḥanbal—may Allah Most High have mercy upon him—and the majority of scholars held that this was actual sight with the eyes.”

9646 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said:
When the Prophet ﷺ went out, his Companions would walk ahead of him and leave the space behind him for the angels.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (246) and Aḥmad (14236), whose wording this is.

9647 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever sees me in a dream will see me while awake—or it is as though he has seen me while awake—for Satan cannot assume my form.”
This is Muslim’s wording. Al-Bukhārī adds that Ibn Sīrīn said: “This applies when he sees him in his actual form.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6993) and Muslim (2266:11).

9648 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever sees me in a dream has truly seen me, for Satan cannot appear in my likeness. The dream of a believer is one of forty-six parts of prophethood.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6994), whose wording this is, and Muslim (2264).

9649 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī reported that he heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
“Whoever sees me has truly seen the truth, for Satan cannot take on my form.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6997).
The expression “cannot take on my form” means that Satan cannot assume the Prophet’s appearance.

9650 — Abū Qatādah reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A good dream is from Allah, while a disturbing dream is from Satan. Whoever sees something he dislikes should lightly spit to his left three times and seek refuge from Satan, and it will not harm him. Satan cannot appear in my guise.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6995) and Muslim in the Book of Dreams (2261:2).
The expression lā yatazāyā bī is written with the letter zāy and a yāʾ after the alif, according to the narrations other than that of Abū Dharr. This was stated by al-Ḥāfiẓ in Fatḥ al-Bārī (12/386). It means that Satan cannot appear in the Prophet’s guise.
The narration of Abū Dharr reads lā yatarāʾā bī, with the letter rāʾ, meaning that Satan cannot become visible in the Prophet’s form.

9651 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Whoever sees me in sleep has truly seen me, for Satan is not able to assume my form.”
He also said:
“When one of you has a disturbing dream, he should not tell anyone about Satan’s playing with him in his sleep.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2268:12). Another wording of his (13) states: “Satan is not able to resemble me.”
The words “cannot assume my form” mean that Satan cannot resemble the Prophet in his actual appearance. See Fatḥ al-Bārī (12/386).

9652 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever sees me in a dream has indeed seen me as though awake, for Satan cannot assume my form.”
Authentic: Narrated and authenticated by al-Tirmidhī (2276), and narrated by Ibn Mājah (3900), Imām Aḥmad (3559), and al-Dārimī (2143).

9653 — ʿAwn ibn Abī Juḥayfah reported from his father that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever sees me in a dream, it is as though he has seen me while awake. Satan cannot assume my form.”
Ibn Ḥibbān’s wording states:
“Satan cannot resemble me.”
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (3904) and Abū Yaʿlā in his Musnad (881).
The summary of this chapter is that the claim of someone who says he saw the Prophet ﷺ in a dream is not accepted unless two conditions are fulfilled:
First: The person must know the Prophet’s physical characteristics ﷺ as transmitted in the authentic collections and books of Sunnah. These include that he was of medium height, fair-skinned with a reddish complexion, the most handsome person on earth, and had black hair containing only a few white hairs, along with the other characteristics mentioned in the section describing the physical appearance of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the collection on his characteristics.
If someone sees him contrary to these descriptions—such as with a dark complexion, a white beard, or short stature—then it is Satan and not the Prophet ﷺ. Satan may claim to be the Prophet ﷺ. If the figure’s appearance does not correspond to the Prophet’s description given above, it is not the Prophet; it is Satan seeking to cause confusion in the dreamer’s religion.
Second: The figure must not command anything newly introduced into the religion or prohibit anything legitimately prescribed, because Allah Most High completed this religion during the Prophet’s lifetime ﷺ. He said:
“Today I have perfected your religion for you, completed My favor upon you, and chosen Islam as your religion.” [Al-Māʾidah: 3]
After the death of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, legislation and abrogation came to an end. If either or both of these conditions are absent, then the figure is not the Prophet ﷺ. Rather, it is Satan seeking to corrupt the person’s religion, for Allah Most High has protected His noble Prophet’s form from being assumed by Satan.

9654 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“By Him in Whose hand is the soul of Muhammad, a day will certainly come upon one of you when he will no longer see me; then seeing me would be dearer to him than having his family and wealth together.”
Abū Isḥāq said:
“In my view, its meaning is: ‘Seeing me along with them would be dearer to him than his family and wealth.’ I regard the wording as containing an instance of transposition.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2364:142) and al-Bukhārī (3589).

9655 — Khālid ibn Maʿdān reported from some Companions of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that they said, “O Messenger of Allah, tell us about yourself.”
He replied:
“I am the answer to the supplication of my forefather Abraham and the glad tidings given by Jesus—peace be upon them both. While my mother was carrying me, she saw a light emerge from her by which the palaces of Buṣrā in the land of Syria were illuminated.
“I was then placed in the care of a wet nurse among Banū Saʿd ibn Bakr. While I was with a foster brother of mine among some young sheep belonging to us, two men dressed in white came to me. They had with them a golden basin filled with snow. They laid me down, opened my abdomen, removed my heart and opened it. They took a black clot from it and cast it away. They then washed my heart and abdomen with that snow until they had thoroughly cleansed them, after which they restored my heart as it had been.
“One of them then said to the other, ‘Weigh him against ten members of his nation.’ He weighed me against ten, and I outweighed them. He then said, ‘Weigh him against one hundred members of his nation.’ He weighed me against one hundred, and I outweighed them. He then said, ‘Weigh him against one thousand members of his nation.’ He weighed me against one thousand, and I outweighed them.
“He then said, ‘Leave him, for if you weighed him against his entire nation, he would outweigh them.’”
Good: Narrated by Muḥammad ibn Isḥāq in Al-Sīrah (p. 28), who said: Thawr ibn Yazīd narrated to me from Khālid ibn Maʿdān, who related it.

9656 — ʿUtbah ibn ʿAbd al-Sulamī related that a man asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, “How did your story first begin, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“My nurse belonged to Banū Saʿd ibn Bakr. Her son and I went out among some young sheep belonging to us, but we took no provisions with us. I said, ‘My brother, go and bring us some provisions from our mother.’ My brother departed while I remained with the sheep.
“Two white figures resembling eagles approached. One said to the other, ‘Is this he?’ He replied, ‘Yes.’ They rushed toward me, took hold of me, laid me flat upon my back, and opened my abdomen. They then removed my heart and opened it, extracting two black clots from it.
“One of them said to the other—as Yazīd’s narration states—‘Bring me snow-water.’ They washed my abdomen with it. He then said, ‘Bring me hail-water,’ and they washed my heart with it. He then said, ‘Bring me serenity,’ and they sprinkled it into my heart. One of them then said to the other, ‘Stitch it.’ He stitched it and sealed it with the Seal of Prophethood.”
Ḥaywah’s narration states: “Stitch it.” He stitched it and was told, “Seal it with the Seal of Prophethood.”
The Prophet ﷺ continued:
“One of them then said to the other, ‘Place him in one pan of the scale and place one thousand members of his nation in the other.’ I could see the thousand above me and feared that some of them might fall upon me. He said, ‘If his entire nation were weighed against him, he would outweigh them.’
