“They then departed and left me. I became extremely frightened, so I went to my nurse and told her what had happened to me. She feared that something might have been inflicted upon me and said, ‘I seek Allah’s protection for you.’
“She prepared her camel, placed me upon the saddle—or, according to Yazīd, carried me upon it—and rode behind me until we reached my mother. She said, ‘I have fulfilled my trust and my obligation,’ and told her what had happened to me.
“This did not alarm my mother. She said, ‘When I gave birth to him, I saw a light emerge from me by which the palaces of Syria were illuminated.’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17648) and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (2/616–617).

9657 — Al-ʿIrbāḍ ibn Sāriyah reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Indeed, I was recorded with Allah as the Seal of the Prophets while Adam—peace be upon him—was still lying in his clay. I shall tell you of the beginning of that: the supplication of my forefather Abraham, Jesus’s glad tidings concerning me, and the vision my mother saw. The mothers of the prophets likewise see such visions.”
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (17150), from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Mahdī, from Muʿāwiyah ibn Ṣāliḥ, from Saʿīd ibn Suwayd al-Kalbī, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Hilāl al-Sulamī, from al-ʿIrbāḍ ibn Sāriyah.

9658 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“There was no prophet who was not given signs by which people would believe in him. What I was given was revelation that Allah revealed to me, so I hope to have the greatest number of followers on the Day of Resurrection.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Virtues of the Qurʾān (4981) and Muslim in the Book of Faith (152), both through al-Layth, from Saʿīd ibn Abī Saʿīd, from his father Abū Saʿīd al-Maqburī, from Abū Hurayrah.

9659 — Jābir ibn Samurah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I certainly know a stone in Makkah that used to greet me before I was commissioned as a prophet. I still know it now.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of Virtues (2277:2), from Abū Bakr ibn Abī Shaybah, from Yaḥyā ibn Abī Bukayr, from Ibrāhīm ibn Ṭahmān, from Simāk ibn Ḥarb, from Jābir ibn Samurah.

9660 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
The moon split into two parts during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, whereupon the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Bear witness.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Signs of Prophethood (3636) and Muslim in the Book of the Characteristics of the Hypocrites (2800:43), both through Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah, from Ibn Abī Najīḥ, from Mujāhid, from Abū Maʿmar, from ʿAbdullāh.
Al-Bukhārī (3869) and Muslim (2800:44) also narrated that ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
“While we were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at Minā, the moon split into two portions: one portion was behind the mountain and the other was on this side of it. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said to us, ‘Bear witness.’”

9661 — Ibn Masʿūd said:
The moon split while we were in Makkah. The disbelievers of Quraysh among the people of Makkah said, “This is magic with which the son of Abū Kabshah has bewitched us. Wait for the travelers who come to you. If they report that they saw what you saw, then it is true; otherwise, he has deceived you.”
No traveler came to them from any direction without reporting that they had seen the same thing.
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd al-Ṭayālisī (293), al-Bazzār (1971), al-Shāshī in his Musnad (404), whose wording this is, and al-Bayhaqī in Al-Dalāʾil (2/266), all through al-Mughīrah ibn Miqsam al-Ḍabbī, from Abū al-Ḍuḥā Muslim ibn Ṣubayḥ, from Masrūq, from ʿAbdullāh. Its chain is authentic.

9662 — Anas ibn Mālik related that the people of Makkah asked the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to show them a sign. He therefore showed them the moon split into two parts, until they could see Mount Ḥirāʾ between them.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Signs of Prophethood (3868) and Muslim in the Book of the Characteristics of the Hypocrites (2802:46), both through Qatādah, from Anas ibn Mālik.

9663 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported that the moon split during the time of the Prophet ﷺ.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Signs of Prophethood (3870) and Muslim in the Book of the Characteristics of the Hypocrites (2803:48), through Bakr ibn Jaʿfar, from Jaʿfar ibn Rabīʿah, from ʿIrāk ibn Mālik, from ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd, from Ibn ʿAbbās.

9664 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar said:
The moon split during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, whereupon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Bear witness.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in the Book of the Description of the Resurrection (2801), though he did not give its wording, and by al-Tirmidhī (2182), whose wording this is, both through Shuʿbah, from al-Aʿmash, from Mujāhid, from Ibn ʿUmar.

9665 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported in the course of his lengthy hadith:
We traveled with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ until we stopped in a wide valley. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went to relieve himself, and I followed him carrying a vessel of water. He looked around but saw nothing with which he could conceal himself. There were two trees at the edge of the valley.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went to one of them, took hold of one of its branches, and said:
“Come under my command by Allah’s permission.”
It came along with him like a haltered camel that complies with its handler. He then went to the other tree, took hold of one of its branches, and said:
“Come under my command by Allah’s permission.”
It came along with him likewise.
When he reached the midpoint between them, he brought them together and said:
“Join together over me by Allah’s permission.”
So they joined together.
Jābir said:
I hurried away out of fear that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ might sense that I was nearby and move farther away—Muḥammad ibn ʿAbbād said: “distance himself farther.” I sat speaking to myself, then happened to glance around and saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ approaching. The two trees had separated, and each had returned to stand upon its own trunk.
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ stop briefly and move his head like this—Abū Ismāʿīl gestured by moving his head right and left—then he came toward me.
When he reached me, he said:
“O Jābir, did you see the place where I stood?”
I replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Go to the two trees, cut a branch from each of them, and bring them back. When you reach the place where I stood, put one branch on your right and the other on your left.”
Jābir said:
I got up, took a stone, broke and sharpened it until it became sharp, then went to the two trees and cut a branch from each. I dragged them back until I reached the place where the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had stood, and I placed one branch on my right and the other on my left.
Then I caught up with him and said:
“I have done it, O Messenger of Allah. Why did you tell me to do that?”
He said:
“I passed by two graves whose occupants were being punished, and I wished, through my intercession, that their punishment might be lightened for as long as the two branches remain fresh”… the remainder of the hadith.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3012).

9666 — Yaʿlā ibn Murrah said:
I witnessed three things from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that no one before me had witnessed and no one after me would witness.
I once traveled with him. While we were on part of the road, we passed a seated woman who had a young boy with her. She said, “O Messenger of Allah, this boy has been afflicted by a disorder, and we have suffered greatly because of it. He is seized by it I do not know how many times each day.”
He said:
“Hand him to me.”
She lifted the boy to him, and he placed him between himself and the middle of the saddle. He then opened the boy’s mouth, blew lightly into it three times and said:
“In the name of Allah. I am the servant of Allah. Be driven away, enemy of Allah!”
He then returned the boy to her and said:
“Meet us at this place on our return and tell us what happened to him.”
We continued our journey and later returned. We found the woman at the same place with three sheep. He asked:
“What happened to your boy?”
She replied, “By the One Who sent you with the truth, we have not noticed anything affecting him up to this very moment. Take these sheep.”
He said:
“Dismount, take one of them, and return the others.”
Yaʿlā continued:
One day, I went with him toward the open land. When we had gone out into the open, he said:
“Look around—may Allah rectify you—and see whether there is anything that can conceal me.”
I replied, “I see nothing that could conceal you except a tree, but I do not think it can fully hide you.”
He asked:
“What is near it?”
I replied, “Another tree like it, or close to its size.”
He said:
“Go to the two trees and tell them: ‘The Messenger of Allah ﷺ commands you both to come together by Allah’s permission.’”
The two trees came together, and he went behind them to relieve himself. When he returned, he said:
“Go to them and say: ‘The Messenger of Allah ﷺ commands each of you to return to its place.’”
They both returned to their places.
Yaʿlā continued:
One day, while I was sitting with him, a camel came trotting toward him. It lowered the underside of its neck before him, and then its eyes began to shed tears. He said:
“Go and see to whom this camel belongs, for it has a matter of concern.”
I went out searching for its owner and discovered that it belonged to a man from the Anṣār. I summoned him to the Prophet ﷺ, who asked:
“What is the matter with this camel of yours?”
The man asked, “What is wrong with it?”
He replied, “By Allah, I do not know what its problem is. We used it for work and for drawing water until it became unable to continue providing water. Last night we consulted one another and decided to slaughter it and divide its meat.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Do not do that. Give it to me as a gift or sell it to me.”
The man replied, “Rather, it is yours, O Messenger of Allah.”
The Prophet ﷺ marked it with the mark used for animals given in charity and then sent it away.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (17548), from ʿAbdullāh ibn Numayr, from ʿUthmān ibn Ḥakīm, from ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, from Yaʿlā ibn Murrah.

9667 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
A Bedouin came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked, “How can I know that you are a prophet?”
He replied:
“If I call this cluster of dates from this palm tree, and it testifies that I am the Messenger of Allah, will you believe?”
The man replied, “Yes.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ called it, and it began descending from the palm tree until it fell before the Prophet ﷺ. He then said:
“Return.”
It returned to its place, whereupon the Bedouin embraced Islam.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Tirmidhī (3628), whose wording this is, Aḥmad (1954), Ibn Ḥibbān (6523), who authenticated it, and al-Ḥākim (2/620), through routes from Abū Ẓabyān Ḥuṣayn ibn Jundab al-Bajalī, from Ibn ʿAbbās.

9668 — Anas ibn Mālik said:
Gabriel came to the Prophet ﷺ one day while he was sitting sorrowfully, stained with blood after some of the people of Makkah had struck him.
Gabriel asked him, “What has happened to you?”
He replied:
“These people did such and such to me.”
Gabriel—peace be upon him—asked, “Would you like me to show you a sign?”
He replied:
“Yes.”
Gabriel looked toward a tree on the other side of the valley and said, “Call that tree.”
The Prophet ﷺ called it, and it came walking until it stood before him. Gabriel said, “Command it to return.”
He commanded it, and it returned to its place. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“That is sufficient for me.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (4208), Aḥmad (12112), and al-Dārimī (23), all through Abū Muʿāwiyah, from al-Aʿmash, from Abū Sufyān Ṭalḥah ibn Nāfiʿ, from Anas ibn Mālik.

9669 — ʿImrān reported:
We were traveling with the Prophet ﷺ and continued traveling by night until its final part. We then lay down and fell into a deep sleep—and no sleep is sweeter to a traveler than that. Nothing awakened us except the heat of the sun.
The first to awaken was one man, then another, then another—Abū Rajāʾ used to name them, but ʿAwf forgot their names—and the fourth was ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.
When the Prophet ﷺ slept, he would not be awakened until he awoke by himself, because we did not know what might be occurring to him in his sleep. When ʿUmar awoke and saw what had happened to the people—and he was a strong, resolute man—he proclaimed Allah’s greatness and raised his voice. He continued saying “Allāhu akbar” in a loud voice until the Prophet ﷺ awoke.
When he awoke, they complained to him about what had happened. He said:
“There is no harm—or: it will cause no harm. Resume your journey.”
He traveled a short distance and then stopped. He called for water for ablution and performed ablution. The call to prayer was made, and he led the people in prayer.
When he finished the prayer, he saw a man sitting apart who had not prayed with the people. He asked:
“What prevented you, so-and-so, from praying with the people?”
The man replied, “I was in a state of major ritual impurity, and there was no water.”
He said:
“Use the clean earth, for it is sufficient for you.”
The Prophet ﷺ then resumed the journey. The people complained to him of thirst, so he stopped and summoned a man—Abū Rajāʾ used to name him, but ʿAwf forgot his name—and also summoned ʿAlī. He told them:
“Go and search for water.”
They departed and encountered a woman riding a camel between two large water-skins—or two leather water containers. They asked her, “Where is the water?”
She replied, “The last time I was at the water was at this hour yesterday, and the men of our household are away.”
They said, “Then come with us.”
She asked, “Where?”
They replied, “To the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
She asked, “The one who is called the Ṣābiʾ?”
They replied, “He is the one you mean. Come with us.”
They brought her to the Prophet ﷺ and told him what had happened. They helped her dismount from her camel, and the Prophet ﷺ called for a vessel. He poured some water into it from the mouths of the two water-skins—or containers—then tied their mouths and opened their lower spouts. A call was made among the people:
“Give water to others and draw water for yourselves!”
Everyone who wished was given water, and everyone who wished drew water. Finally, he gave the man who had been in a state of major ritual impurity a vessel of water and said:
“Go and pour it over yourself.”
The woman remained standing, watching what they were doing with her water. By Allah, when they stopped drawing from the water-skins, it appeared to us that they were even fuller than they had been at the beginning.
The Prophet ﷺ then said:
“Collect something for her.”
They collected dates, flour, and roasted grain for her until they had gathered a quantity of food. They placed it in a cloth, lifted her onto her camel, and placed the bundle before her.
He said to her:
“You know that we have not diminished your water at all. Rather, it was Allah Who gave us water.”
She returned to her people after having been delayed. They asked, “What detained you, so-and-so?”
She replied, “Something astonishing! Two men met me and took me to the man called the Ṣābiʾ, and he did such and such. By Allah, either he is the greatest magician between these two”—she gestured with her middle and index fingers, raising them toward the sky, meaning between the heavens and earth—“or he truly is the Messenger of Allah.”
Thereafter, the Muslims would raid the polytheistic tribes surrounding her people but would leave untouched the small community to which she belonged. One day she said to her people, “I think these people are deliberately sparing you. Would you consider accepting Islam?”
They obeyed her and entered Islam.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Tayammum (344) and Muslim in the Book of Mosques (682:312), both through ʿAwf ibn Abī Jamīlah al-Aʿrābī, from Abū Rajāʾ al-ʿAṭāridī, from ʿImrān ibn al-Ḥuṣayn. The wording is al-Bukhārī’s; Muslim’s is similar.

9670 — Jābir reported:
The people became thirsty on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyah. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had a small leather vessel before him and performed ablution from it. The people then approached him, so he asked:
“What is the matter with you?”
They replied, “O Messenger of Allah, we have no water with which to perform ablution or drink except what is in your vessel.”
The Prophet ﷺ placed his hand in the vessel, and water began gushing from between his fingers like springs. We drank and performed ablution.
I asked Jābir, “How many were you that day?”
He replied, “Had we been one hundred thousand, it would have been sufficient for us. We were fifteen hundred.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Military Campaigns (4152) and Muslim in the Book of Leadership (1856:73), both through Ḥuṣayn ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, from Sālim ibn Abī al-Jaʿd, from Jābir.
His statement, “We were fifteen hundred,” may be reconciled with ʿAmr ibn Dīnār’s narration from Jābir, “We were fourteen hundred,” by understanding that they numbered more than fourteen hundred. Whoever reported fifteen hundred rounded the number upward, while whoever reported fourteen hundred omitted the remaining fraction.
As for ʿAbdullāh ibn Abī Awfā’s report that they were thirteen hundred, it may refer to the number known to him, while others knew of additional participants. Alternatively, the number he mentioned may have been the total when they first departed Madinah, with others joining them later. It may also refer specifically to the fighting men, while the additional number consisted of attendants, women, and children who had not yet reached puberty.

9671 — Al-Barāʾ said:
You consider the conquest to be the conquest of Makkah—and the conquest of Makkah was indeed a conquest—but we regarded the true conquest as the Pledge of al-Riḍwān on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyah.
We were fourteen hundred with the Prophet ﷺ. Al-Ḥudaybiyah had a well, and we drew all its water until we did not leave a single drop in it. News of this reached the Prophet ﷺ, so he came to the well and sat at its edge. He then called for a vessel of water, performed ablution, rinsed his mouth, supplicated, and poured the water into the well.
We left it for a short while, after which it supplied all the water that we and our riding animals desired.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Military Campaigns (4150), from ʿUbaydullāh ibn Mūsā, from Isrāʾīl, from Abū Isḥāq, from al-Barāʾ.

9672 — Muʿādh ibn Jabal said:
We went out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the campaign of Tabūk. He would combine the prayers, performing Ẓuhr and ʿAṣr together and Maghrib and ʿIshāʾ together.
One day he delayed the prayer, then came out and performed Ẓuhr and ʿAṣr together. He went inside, then later emerged and performed Maghrib and ʿIshāʾ together.
He then said:
“Tomorrow, if Allah wills, you will reach the spring of Tabūk. You will not reach it until the morning has advanced. Whoever among you reaches it must not touch any of its water until I arrive.”
When we reached it, two men had preceded us there. The spring was like a sandal strap, trickling only a small amount of water.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked them:
“Did you touch any of its water?”
They replied, “Yes.”
The Prophet ﷺ rebuked them and said to them whatever Allah willed that he should say.
The people then scooped small amounts of water from the spring with their hands until some had collected in a vessel. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ washed his hands and face in it and then poured it back into the spring. The spring began flowing with abundant, gushing water—Abū ʿAlī was uncertain which of the two expressions was used—until all the people drew water.
He then said:
“O Muʿādh, if you live a long life, you will soon see this entire place filled with gardens.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (706:10).
The word tabiḍḍ, written with the letter ḍād, means “to trickle or flow.” The word munhamir means water pouring forth forcefully and abundantly.

9673 — Anas ibn Mālik said:
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when the time for the ʿAṣr prayer had arrived. The people searched for water with which to perform ablution but could not find any. A vessel containing a small amount of water was brought to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He placed his hand in the vessel and then instructed the people to perform ablution from it.
Anas said:
I saw the water springing from beneath his fingers. The people continued performing ablution until the very last of them had done so.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (32), al-Bukhārī (169), and Muslim (2279).

9674 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
We used to regard miraculous signs as blessings, whereas you regard them as warnings that inspire fear.
We were traveling with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when the water became scarce. He said:
“Search for any remaining water.”
They brought a vessel containing a small quantity of water. He placed his hand in the vessel and said:
“Come to the blessed, purifying water. The blessing is from Allah.”
I certainly saw water springing from between the fingers of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. We also used to hear the food glorifying Allah while it was being eaten.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Signs of Prophethood (3579), from Muḥammad ibn al-Muthannā, from Abū Aḥmad al-Zubayrī, from Isrāʾīl, from Manṣūr, from Ibrāhīm, from ʿAlqamah, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd.
The words “during a journey” apparently refer to al-Ḥudaybiyah. A similar event also occurred during the campaign of Khaybar, as narrated by Abū Nuʿaym in Al-Dalāʾil through Yaḥyā ibn Salamah ibn Kuhayl, from his father, from Ibrāhīm, through his chain of transmission.
Al-Ḥāfiẓ said in Fatḥ al-Bārī (6/591):
“This is more likely and indicates that this miracle occurred repeatedly, whether at home or during journeys.”

9675 — Jābir reported in the course of his lengthy hadith:
We reached the encampment, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O Jābir, call out for water for ablution.”
So I called:
“Is there any water for ablution? Is there any water for ablution? Is there any water for ablution?”
I then said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I have not found even a single drop among the people.”
There was a man from the Anṣār who used to keep water cool for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in some leather water-skins placed upon a wooden frame.
He said to me:
“Go to so-and-so, the Anṣārī, and see whether there is anything in his water-skins.”
I went to him and looked inside them, but found only a drop remaining in the spout of one of the water-skins; if I were to pour it out, the dry leather itself would absorb it.
I came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I found only a drop in the spout of one of the water-skins. If I were to pour it out, the dry leather itself would absorb it.”
He said:
“Go and bring it to me.”
So I brought it to him. He took it in his hand and began saying something that I could not make out while pressing it with his hands.
Then he handed it to me and said:
“O Jābir, call for a large basin.”
I called out:
“Bring the basin of the travelers!”
It was brought, carried by people, and I placed it before him.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ placed his hand in the basin like this, spreading his fingers apart, then put his hand at the bottom of the basin and said:
“Take it, O Jābir. Pour upon my hand and say: ‘In the name of Allah.’”
So I poured upon his hand and said:
“In the name of Allah.”
I saw water gush forth from between the fingers of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. Then the basin filled and overflowed until it was completely full.
He said:
“O Jābir, call everyone who has need of water.”
The people came and drew water until they had all drunk their fill.
I asked:
“Is there anyone left who still needs water?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then lifted his hand from the basin while it was still full… the remainder of the hadith.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (3012).

9676 — Al-Barāʾ said:
We numbered fourteen hundred on the day of al-Ḥudaybiyah. Al-Ḥudaybiyah had a well, and we drew from it until we did not leave a single drop.
The Prophet ﷺ sat at the edge of the well, called for some water, rinsed his mouth, and spat the water into the well. We waited only a short while and then drew water until we had drunk our fill and our riding animals had also drunk their fill—or departed fully refreshed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3577).

9677 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ said:
We arrived at al-Ḥudaybiyah with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, numbering fourteen hundred. Its well could not provide sufficient water even for fifty sheep.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sat upon the raised stonework surrounding the well. He either supplicated or spat into it, whereupon the water surged forth. We gave water to our animals and drew water for ourselves.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1807:132).

9678 — Anas ibn Mālik reported:
Abū Ṭalḥah said to Umm Sulaym, “I have heard the voice of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sounding weak, and I recognize hunger in it. Do you have any food?”
She replied, “Yes.” She brought out some small loaves of barley bread, took a head-covering of hers and wrapped part of it around the bread. She then concealed the bread under my arm, wrapped the remaining part of the cloth around me, and sent me to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
I took it and found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sitting in the mosque with other people. I stood before them, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Did Abū Ṭalḥah send you?”
I replied, “Yes.”
He asked:
“Because of food?”
I replied, “Yes.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said to those with him:
“Get up.”
He set out, and I went ahead of them until I reached Abū Ṭalḥah and informed him. Abū Ṭalḥah said, “Umm Sulaym, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ has brought the people, but we do not have enough food to feed them.”
She replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.”
Abū Ṭalḥah went out to meet the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came accompanied by Abū Ṭalḥah, and they entered the house. He said:
“Bring whatever you have, Umm Sulaym.”
She brought the bread. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ ordered that it be crumbled, and Umm Sulaym squeezed clarified butter from a leather container over it to season it. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said whatever Allah willed him to say and instructed:
“Admit ten people.”
Ten were admitted, and they ate until they were satisfied and then left. He again said, “Admit ten people.” They entered, ate until they were satisfied, and departed. He continued admitting them in groups of ten until all the people had eaten their fill. They numbered seventy or eighty men.
Agreed upon: Narrated by Mālik (19), al-Bukhārī (3578), and Muslim (2040).
Muslim also narrated through another chain (2040:143) that Anas ibn Mālik said:
Abū Ṭalḥah sent me to invite the Messenger of Allah ﷺ after he had prepared some food. I approached while the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was among the people. He looked at me, and I felt shy, so I said, “Accept Abū Ṭalḥah’s invitation.”
He said to the people:
“Get up.”
Abū Ṭalḥah said, “O Messenger of Allah, I prepared only a small amount specifically for you.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ touched the food and prayed that it be blessed. He then said:
“Admit ten of my Companions.”
He told them, “Eat,” and brought food out for them from between his fingers. They ate until they were satisfied and then departed. He said, “Admit ten more.” They ate until they were satisfied and departed.
He continued admitting ten and dismissing ten until every one of them had entered and eaten his fill. The food was then set in order, and it appeared just as it had been before they ate from it.

9679 — Anas ibn Mālik said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ married and consummated his marriage. My mother, Umm Sulaym, prepared ḥays—a mixture of dates, clarified butter, and dried curds—and placed it in a stone vessel.
She said, “Anas, take this to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and say: ‘My mother has sent this to you. She conveys her greetings of peace and says: This is only a small offering from us, O Messenger of Allah.’”
I took it to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said, “My mother conveys her greetings of peace and says: ‘This is only a small offering from us, O Messenger of Allah.’”
He said:
“Set it down.”
He then said:
“Go and invite so-and-so, so-and-so, and so-and-so, as well as anyone else you meet.”
He named several men. I invited those whom he named and everyone else I encountered.
The narrator asked Anas, “Approximately how many were there?”
He replied, “Around three hundred.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me:
“O Anas, bring the vessel.”
The people entered until the shaded platform and the room were filled. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Let them form circles of ten, and let each person eat from what is directly before him.”
They ate until they were satisfied. One group departed and another entered until everyone had eaten. He then said to me:
“O Anas, remove it.”
I removed the vessel, but I could not tell whether it had contained more food when I first set it down or when I lifted it afterward.
Some groups remained sitting and conversing in the house of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ was seated, while his wife had turned her face toward the wall. Their prolonged stay became burdensome for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, so he went out, greeted his other wives, and then returned.
When they saw that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had returned, they realized that their presence had become burdensome to him. They hurried toward the door, and all of them departed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ came, lowered the curtain and entered, while I remained seated in the room. A short while later, he came out to me after this verse had been revealed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went out and recited it to the people:
“O you who believe, do not enter the houses of the Prophet unless permission is given to you for a meal, without waiting for its preparation. But when you are invited, then enter; and when you have eaten, disperse without remaining for conversation. Indeed, that was troubling the Prophet.” [Al-Aḥzāb: 53]
The remainder of the verse follows.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1428:94).
Al-Bukhārī cited it in suspended form in the Book of Marriage (5163), from Anas ibn Mālik, who said:
He passed by us in the mosque of Banū Rifāʿah, and I heard him say:
“When the Prophet ﷺ passed near the home of Umm Sulaym, he would enter and greet her.”
He then said:
“The Prophet ﷺ had newly married Zaynab. Umm Sulaym said to me, ‘We should give the Messenger of Allah ﷺ a gift.’
“I replied, ‘Do so.’
“She took dates, clarified butter, and dried curds and prepared ḥays in a cooking pot. She then sent it with me to him.”
The remainder of the narration is similar.

9680 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr reported:
The Companions of al-Ṣuffah were poor people. On one occasion, the Prophet ﷺ said:
“Whoever has food sufficient for two should take a third person with him, and whoever has food sufficient for four should take a fifth or a sixth.”
Or he said words to that effect.
The Prophet ﷺ took ten people with him, while Abū Bakr brought three. Those at our home were myself, my father, and my mother—and I do not know whether he also mentioned my wife—as well as a servant shared between our household and that of Abū Bakr.
Abū Bakr ate dinner with the Prophet ﷺ and remained until the ʿIshāʾ prayer had been performed. He then stayed until the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had eaten dinner. He came home after as much of the night as Allah willed had passed.
His wife asked him, “What detained you from your guests—or your guest?”
He asked, “Have you not served them dinner?”
She replied, “They refused to eat until you came. Food was presented to them, but they insisted on waiting for you.”
I withdrew and hid. Abū Bakr exclaimed, “You foolish fellow!” He reproached and rebuked me, then told the guests, “Eat.” He also declared, “By Allah, I will never eat it.”
By Allah, whenever we took a morsel from the food, more than it rose from beneath it. They ate until they were satisfied, yet the food became more abundant than it had been before.
Abū Bakr looked at it and found that it was as plentiful as before—or even more so. He said to his wife, “Sister of Banū Firās!”
She replied, “By the delight of my eyes, it is now three times more plentiful than it was before.”
Abū Bakr ate from it and said that it had only been Satan—meaning that his oath not to eat had been prompted by Satan. He then ate a morsel from it and carried the food to the Prophet ﷺ, where it remained until morning.
There was a treaty between us and another people, and its term came to an end. We divided ourselves into twelve groups, each under the leadership of a man, and Allah knows how many men accompanied each leader. The Prophet ﷺ sent them out, and all of them ate from that food—or he related words to that effect.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3581) and Muslim (2057).

9681 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr said:
We were with the Prophet ﷺ, numbering one hundred and thirty. The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Does anyone among you have any food?”
One man had approximately one ṣāʿ of food, so it was kneaded.
A tall polytheist with disheveled hair then came driving some sheep. The Prophet ﷺ asked him:
“Are they for sale or a gift?”
—or he said, “A donation?”
The man replied, “No, they are for sale.”
The Prophet ﷺ bought a sheep from him, and it was prepared. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ ordered that its liver be roasted.
By Allah, there was not one of the one hundred and thirty people for whom he did not cut a portion of its liver. If the person was present, he gave it to him; and if he was absent, he reserved it for him.
He then placed the prepared food in two large dishes. All of us ate and were satisfied, yet food remained in both dishes, so I loaded them onto the camel—or he related words to that effect.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2618) and Muslim (2056:175).
The word mishʿān means that his hair was disheveled and scattered. The expression “the dark organ of the abdomen” refers to the liver.

9682 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said:
While the trench was being dug, I noticed that the Prophet ﷺ was suffering from severe hunger. I returned to my wife and asked, “Do you have any food? I have observed severe hunger in the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
She brought out a leather bag containing one ṣāʿ of barley. We also had a young domesticated animal, so I slaughtered it while she ground the barley. She finished grinding when I finished slaughtering, and I cut the meat and placed it in the cooking pot.
I then went to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. My wife said, “Do not embarrass me before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and those who are with him.”
I approached him privately and said, “O Messenger of Allah, we have slaughtered a young animal of ours and ground one ṣāʿ of barley that we had. Please come with a small group of people.”
The Prophet ﷺ called out loudly:
“People of the trench! Jābir has prepared a meal, so come along!”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then instructed me:
“Do not remove your pot from the fire, and do not bake your dough until I arrive.”
I returned home, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ came leading the people. When I reached my wife, she exclaimed, “May such-and-such happen to you!” blaming me for what I had done.
I said, “I did exactly what you told me.”
She brought out our dough. The Prophet ﷺ spat lightly into it and invoked blessing upon it. He then went to our cooking pot, spat lightly into it, and invoked blessing upon it. He said:
“Call another woman to bake with you. Keep ladling from your pot, but do not remove it from the fire.”
They numbered one thousand. I swear by Allah that they all ate until they left the food and withdrew fully satisfied. Our pot continued bubbling just as it had been, and our dough continued being baked just as it had been before.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4102) and Muslim (2039:141).

9683 — Salamah ibn al-Akwaʿ said:
We went out with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on a military expedition and suffered severe hardship, until we considered slaughtering some of our riding animals.
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ instructed us to gather our provision bags. We spread out a leather mat for him, and the people’s remaining provisions were collected upon it.
I stretched upward to estimate how much there was and judged it to be approximately the size of a resting goat, although we numbered fourteen hundred.
We all ate until we were satisfied and then filled our provision bags. The Prophet of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Is there any water for ablution?”
A man brought a small leather vessel containing only a little water and poured it into a cup. All fourteen hundred of us performed ablution from it, pouring from it freely.
Eight people then arrived afterward and asked, “Is there any water for purification?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“The water for ablution has been exhausted.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by Muslim (1729:19) and al-Bukhārī (2484).

9684 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said:
ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām died while owing a debt. I sought the assistance of the Prophet ﷺ in asking his creditors to reduce some of the debt. The Prophet ﷺ appealed to them, but they refused.
The Prophet ﷺ then said to me:
“Go and separate your dates according to their varieties: place the ʿajwah dates separately and the dates of ʿAdhq ibn Zayd separately. Then send for me.”
I did so and sent for the Prophet ﷺ. He sat upon the upper part of the dates—or in their midst—and said:
“Measure them out for the creditors.”
I measured the dates for them until I had fully paid everything they were owed, yet my dates remained as though nothing had been taken from them.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2127, 2405–2406).
Concerning the expression “ʿAdhq ibn Zayd”: ʿadhaq, with the letter ʿayn bearing fatḥah, means a date palm; with kasrah, ʿidhq means a date cluster. Ibn Zayd was a man after whom this particular variety of dates was named.
The varieties of dates in Madinah are extremely numerous. Shaykh Abū Muḥammad al-Juwaynī mentioned in Al-Furūq that while he was in Madinah, he learned that more than sixty varieties of black dates alone had been counted in the presence of its governor. He added that the varieties of red dates there were even more numerous than the black ones. See Fatḥ al-Bārī (4/345).

9685 — Abū Hurayrah said:
I entered with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and he found some milk in a cup. He said:
“O Abū Hurayrah, go to the people of al-Ṣuffah and invite them to come to me.”
I went to them and invited them. They came, sought permission to enter, and were granted permission. They then entered and drank.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6246).
Al-Bukhārī also narrated (6452) that Abū Hurayrah used to say:
By Allah, besides Whom there is no deity worthy of worship, I would press my stomach against the ground because of hunger, and I would tie a stone against my abdomen because of hunger.
One day, I sat along the road by which the Companions usually passed. Abū Bakr passed by, so I asked him about a verse from the Book of Allah. I asked him only so that he might notice my hunger and feed me, but he continued on without doing so.
ʿUmar then passed by me, and I asked him about a verse from the Book of Allah. I asked him only so that he might feed me, but he also continued on without doing so.
Abū al-Qāsim ﷺ then passed by me. When he saw me, he smiled and recognized from my face what I was feeling within myself. He said:
“O Abū Hirr!”
I replied, “At your service, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Follow me.”
He continued on, and I followed him. He entered his home. I sought permission to enter, and he gave me permission. Upon entering, he found some milk in a cup and asked:
“Where did this milk come from?”
They replied, “So-and-so, a man or a woman, gave it to you as a gift.”
He said:
“Abū Hirr!”
I replied, “At your service, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Go to the people of al-Ṣuffah and invite them to come to me.”
The people of al-Ṣuffah were the guests of Islam. They had neither families nor wealth to support them, nor anyone upon whom they could depend. Whenever charity came to the Prophet ﷺ, he would send it to them without taking any of it himself. Whenever a gift came to him, he would send for them, take some of it himself, and share it with them.
His command troubled me, and I thought: What will this small amount of milk do for all the people of al-Ṣuffah? I was more in need of drinking from it so that I might regain my strength. If they came, he would order me to serve them. What amount of milk could possibly remain for me?
Nevertheless, obedience to Allah and His Messenger ﷺ was unavoidable. I went to the people of al-Ṣuffah and invited them. They came, sought permission to enter, and were admitted. They took their places in the house.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“O Abū Hirr!”
I replied, “At your service, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Take the cup and give it to them.”
I took the cup and began handing it to each man. He would drink until fully satisfied and return the cup to me. I would then give it to the next man, who would drink until fully satisfied and return it to me. This continued until I reached the Prophet ﷺ after all the people had drunk their fill.
He took the cup and held it in his hand. He looked at me, smiled, and said:
“Abū Hirr!”
I replied, “At your service, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Only you and I remain.”
I replied, “You have spoken the truth, O Messenger of Allah.”
He said:
“Sit down and drink.”
I sat and drank. He again said:
“Drink.”
So I drank again. He continued telling me, “Drink,” until I said, “No, by the One Who sent you with the truth, I can find no more room for it.”
He said:
“Then give it to me.”
I gave him the cup. He praised Allah, pronounced His name, and drank what remained.

9686 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported:
Umm Mālik used to present clarified butter to the Prophet ﷺ in a leather container. Her sons would later come to her asking for something to eat with their bread when they had nothing else. She would go to the container in which she had sent the butter to the Prophet ﷺ and find butter in it.
It continued providing the condiment needed by her household until she squeezed the container completely. She then came to the Prophet ﷺ, who asked:
“Did you squeeze it?”
She replied, “Yes.”
He said:
“Had you left it alone, it would have continued to provide for you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2280:8).
The words “it would have continued” mean that it would have remained present and available.

9687 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported:
A man came to the Prophet ﷺ asking for food. The Prophet ﷺ gave him half a wasq of barley. The man, his wife, and their guests continued eating from it until he measured it.
He then came to the Prophet ﷺ, who said:
“Had you not measured it, you would have continued eating from it, and it would have remained available for you.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2281:9).

9688 — Abū Hurayrah said:
We were traveling with the Prophet ﷺ when the people’s provisions were exhausted, until they considered slaughtering some of the animals carrying their loads.
ʿUmar said, “O Messenger of Allah, why not gather whatever remains of the people’s provisions and supplicate to Allah to bless them?”
The Prophet ﷺ did so. Those who had wheat brought their wheat, and those who had dates brought their dates.
Mujāhid added, “Those who had date stones brought their date stones.”
The narrator asked, “What did they do with the date stones?”
He replied, “They would suck them and then drink water over them.”
The Prophet ﷺ invoked Allah’s blessing upon the provisions until the people had filled their provision bags. He then said:
“I bear witness that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that I am the Messenger of Allah. No servant will meet Allah affirming these two testimonies without doubting them except that he will enter Paradise.”
Another narration states:
During the campaign of Tabūk, the people suffered from famine. They said, “O Messenger of Allah, if only you would permit us to slaughter our camels used for drawing water, so that we might eat their meat and use their fat.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Do so.”
ʿUmar then came and said, “O Messenger of Allah, if you do that, there will be too few riding animals left. Instead, ask the people to bring the remnants of their provisions and then supplicate to Allah to bless them. Perhaps Allah will place blessing in them.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Yes.”
He called for a leather mat and spread it out. He then asked the people to bring the remnants of their provisions. One man brought a handful of millet, another brought a handful of dates, and another brought a piece of bread, until a small quantity had been collected upon the mat.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ invoked blessing upon it and then said:
“Fill your containers from it.”
They filled their containers until there was not a single container in the entire encampment that they had not filled. They ate until they were satisfied, and some food still remained.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then said:
“I bear witness that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that I am the Messenger of Allah. No servant will meet Allah affirming these two testimonies without doubt and then be barred from Paradise.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (27).

9689 — Abū Hurayrah reported:
A sheep was cooked, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Give me a foreleg.”
He was handed one. He again said:
“Give me a foreleg.”
He was handed another. He then said:
“Give me a foreleg.”
The man said, “O Messenger of Allah, a sheep has only two forelegs!”
He replied:
“Had you continued looking for them, you would have found them.”
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (10706).

9690 — Abū ʿUbayd reported:
I prepared a pot of meat for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Hand me its foreleg.”
I handed him one. He again said:
“Hand me its foreleg.”
I handed him another. He then said:
“Hand me its foreleg.”
I asked, “O Prophet of Allah, how many forelegs does a sheep have?”
He replied:
“By Him in Whose hand is my soul, had you remained silent, you would have continued handing me a foreleg each time I requested one.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (15967), al-Ṭabarānī in Al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr (22/335–336), and al-Tirmidhī in Al-Shamāʾil (170).

9691 — Samurah ibn Jundab said:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, taking turns eating from a single large dish from morning until night. Ten people would rise after eating, and another ten would sit down to eat.
We asked, “From where was the food continually replenished?”
He replied, “Why are you astonished? It was replenished only from there,” and he pointed with his hand toward the sky.
Authentic: Narrated and authenticated by al-Tirmidhī (3625), narrated by Aḥmad (20196), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6529).

9692 — Abū ʿAmrah al-Anṣārī said:
We were with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ on a military expedition when the people were afflicted by severe hunger. They sought the permission of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to slaughter some of their riding animals, saying, “Allah will enable us to reach our destination by means of their meat.”
When ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb saw that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was about to permit them to slaughter some of their mounts, he said:
“O Messenger of Allah, what will become of us if we encounter the enemy tomorrow while hungry and on foot? Instead, if you deem it appropriate, ask them to bring the remnants of their provisions. Gather them together and supplicate to Allah to bless them. Allah, Blessed and Most High, will enable us to reach our destination through your supplication”—or he said, “Allah will bless your supplication for us.”
The Prophet ﷺ called for the remnants of their provisions. The people began bringing handfuls of food or a little more. The largest contribution anyone brought was one ṣāʿ of dates.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ gathered it all together, stood, and supplicated for as long as Allah willed. He then called upon the army to bring their containers and instructed them to scoop food into them.
Not a single container remained in the army without being filled, yet an equal amount of food still remained.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ laughed until his molar teeth became visible and said:
“I bear witness that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that I am the Messenger of Allah. No servant who believes in these two testimonies will meet Allah without the Fire being screened from him on the Day of Resurrection.”
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (15449).

9693 — Abū Hurayrah said:
I brought some dates to the Prophet ﷺ and said, “O Messenger of Allah, ask Allah to bless them.”
He gathered them together and invoked Allah’s blessing upon them for me. He then said:
“Take them and place them in this provision bag of yours—or in this bag. Whenever you wish to take something from it, put your hand inside and remove what you need, but do not empty it out by scattering its contents.”
From those dates I supplied many wasqs in the cause of Allah. We continued eating from them and feeding others. The bag never left my waist until the day ʿUthmān was killed, when it was cut away and lost.
Good: Narrated and graded it good by al-Tirmidhī (3839), and narrated by Aḥmad (8628) and Ibn Ḥibbān (6532).

9694 — Dukayn ibn Saʿīd al-Muzanī said:
Four hundred and forty riders came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ asking him for food. He said to ʿUmar:
“Go and give them food.”
ʿUmar replied, “O Messenger of Allah, only a few ṣāʿs of dates remain—hardly enough to sustain me.”
He said:
“Go and give them food.”
ʿUmar replied, “I hear and obey.”
ʿUmar took a key from his waistband and opened the door. There was a pile of dates resembling a young camel lying upon the ground. He told us, “Take what you need.”
Each man took as much as he wished. I then looked back, as I was among the last of the people, and it appeared as though we had not diminished the pile by even a single date.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (17577), whose wording this is; Abū Dāwūd (5238) in abbreviated form; and Ibn Ḥibbān (6528).
The expression “hardly enough to sustain me” means “not enough to suffice me.” A “young camel lying down” refers to the offspring of a she-camel sitting firmly upon the ground. The words “we had not diminished it” mean that we appeared neither to have reduced nor taken anything from it.

9695 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd said:
We used to hear the food glorifying Allah while it was being eaten.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Signs of Prophethood (3579), from Muḥammad ibn al-Muthannā, from Abū Aḥmad al-Zubayrī, from Isrāʾīl, from Manṣūr, from Ibrāhīm, from ʿAlqamah, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd.

9696 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said:
There was a palm trunk beside which the Prophet ﷺ used to stand. When the pulpit was erected for him, we heard the trunk making sounds like those of pregnant she-camels, until the Prophet ﷺ descended and placed his hand upon it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (918).

9697 — Ibn ʿUmar said:
The Prophet ﷺ used to deliver his sermons beside a palm trunk. When a pulpit was made for him, he moved to it, whereupon the trunk cried with longing. He went to it and passed his hand over it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3583).

9698 — Abū Ḥāzim reported:
Some people came to Sahl ibn Saʿd and asked, “From what was the pulpit of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ made?”
He replied, “No one remaining among the people knows more about it than I do. It was made from the tamarisk wood of al-Ghābah. So-and-so, the freed slave of a certain woman, made it for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ used to lean against a palm trunk in the mosque, standing beside it when delivering a sermon. When the pulpit was made and he sat upon it, the trunk cried with longing. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ went to it and soothed it—although Abū Ḥāzim’s narration does not contain the words “and soothed it”—until it became quiet.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah (32406) and Muslim (544:45).

9699 — Anas said:
The people of Madinah suffered a drought during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. While he was delivering the Friday sermon, a man stood and said, “O Messenger of Allah, the horses and camels are perishing and the sheep are dying. Supplicate to Allah to send us rain.”
The Prophet ﷺ extended his hands and supplicated.
Anas said:
The sky had been as clear as glass. A wind then arose and formed clouds. The clouds gathered together, and the sky opened its channels of rain. We left the mosque wading through water until we reached our homes.
It continued raining until the following Friday. The same man—or another man—stood and said, “O Messenger of Allah, the houses are collapsing. Supplicate to Allah to withhold the rain.”
The Prophet ﷺ smiled and said:
“O Allah, let it fall around us and not upon us.”
I looked at the clouds as they separated around Madinah until they resembled a circular crown.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3582) and Muslim (897).

9700 — Anas ibn Mālik said:
The people were afflicted by drought during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. While the Messenger of Allah ﷺ was delivering the Friday sermon from the pulpit, a Bedouin stood and said, “O Messenger of Allah, our wealth and livestock are perishing and our families are hungry. Supplicate to Allah to send us rain.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised his hands while there was not a single wisp of cloud in the sky. Clouds then arose resembling mountains. He had not yet descended from the pulpit when I saw rainwater flowing down his beard.
Rain fell upon us that day, the following day, the day after that, and the next day, continuing until the following Friday.
That Bedouin—or another man—then stood and said, “O Messenger of Allah, the buildings are collapsing and our wealth is being submerged. Supplicate to Allah for us.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised his hands and said:
“O Allah, let it fall around us and not upon us.”
Whenever he pointed toward any part of the sky, the clouds dispersed from that direction, until Madinah resembled an open hollow surrounded by clouds. The valley of Qanāh continued flowing for a month. No one came from any direction without reporting abundant rainfall.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1033) and Muslim (897:9).

9701 — ʿUrwah reported:
The Prophet ﷺ gave him one dinar with which to purchase a sheep. He bought two sheep for that dinar and then sold one of them for a dinar. He returned to the Prophet ﷺ with both the original dinar and a sheep.
The Prophet ﷺ supplicated that his business transactions be blessed. Thereafter, even if he had purchased dust, he would have made a profit from it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3642).

9702 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said:
I participated in a military expedition with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Prophet ﷺ caught up with me while I was riding a water-carrying camel of ours that had become exhausted and could scarcely move.
He asked:
“What is wrong with your camel?”
I replied, “It has become exhausted.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ fell behind with it, urged it forward, and supplicated for it. Thereafter, it continued traveling at the front of the other camels, ahead of them.
The complete hadith appears in the account of the sale of the camel.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2967) and Muslim (715:110).

9703 — Anas said:
The people were alarmed, so the Messenger of Allah ﷺ mounted a slow horse belonging to Abū Ṭalḥah and rode off alone at a gallop. The people then mounted their horses and galloped after him.
He said:
“You have nothing to fear. This horse is truly like a sea in its speed.”
After that day, the horse was never outrun.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2969) and Muslim (2307:48).

9704 — Anas ibn Mālik reported:
When ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām learned that the Prophet ﷺ had arrived in Madinah, he came to ask him several questions. He said:
“I shall ask you three questions whose answers are known only to a prophet: What is the first sign of the Hour? What is the first food the people of Paradise will eat? And what causes a child to resemble either its father or its mother?”
The Prophet ﷺ replied:
“Gabriel informed me of these things just now.”
Ibn Salām said, “He is the enemy of the Jews among the angels.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“As for the first sign of the Hour, it will be a fire that gathers the people from the east to the west. The first food eaten by the people of Paradise will be the extra lobe of a fish’s liver. As for the child, when the man’s fluid precedes the woman’s fluid, the child resembles him; and when the woman’s fluid precedes the man’s fluid, the child resembles her.”
ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām said, “I bear witness that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that you are the Messenger of Allah.”
He then said, “O Messenger of Allah, the Jews are a people of falsehood and slander. Ask them about me before they learn that I have embraced Islam.”
The Jews came, and the Prophet ﷺ asked them:
“What kind of man is ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām among you?”
They replied, “He is the best of us and the son of the best of us, the most virtuous of us and the son of the most virtuous of us.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“What would you think if ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām were to embrace Islam?”
They replied, “May Allah protect him from doing so.”
He repeated the question, and they gave the same answer.
ʿAbdullāh then came out before them and said, “I bear witness that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.”
They immediately said, “He is the worst of us and the son of the worst of us,” and began disparaging him.
ʿAbdullāh said, “This is what I feared, O Messenger of Allah.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3938).

9705 — Thawbān, the freed slave of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, said:
I was standing beside the Messenger of Allah ﷺ when a Jewish rabbi came and said, “Peace be upon you, Muhammad.”
I pushed him so forcefully that he nearly fell. He asked, “Why did you push me?”
I replied, “Why did you not say, ‘O Messenger of Allah’?”
The Jewish man said, “We call him only by the name his family gave him.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“My name is Muhammad—the name my family gave me.”
The Jewish man said, “I have come to ask you some questions.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ asked:
“Will it benefit you if I answer you?”
He replied, “I shall listen with my own ears.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ began drawing upon the ground with a stick he held and said:
“Ask.”
The Jewish man asked, “Where will the people be on the day when the earth is changed into another earth, and the heavens as well?”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ replied:
“They will be in darkness before the bridge.”
He asked, “Who will be the first people permitted to cross?”
He replied:
“The poor among the Emigrants.”
The Jewish man asked, “What will be presented to them when they enter Paradise?”
He replied:
“The extra lobe of a fish’s liver.”
He asked, “What will their meal be after that?”
He replied:
“The bull of Paradise, which used to graze along its edges, will be slaughtered for them.”
He asked, “What will they drink with it?”
He replied:
“They will drink from a spring therein called Salsabīl.”
The Jewish man said, “You have spoken the truth.”
He then said, “I have also come to ask you about something that no one on earth knows except a prophet, or perhaps one or two other men.”
The Prophet ﷺ asked:
“Will it benefit you if I tell you?”
He replied, “I shall listen with my own ears. I have come to ask you about the child.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“The man’s reproductive fluid is white, and the woman’s reproductive fluid is yellow. When they meet and the man’s fluid prevails over the woman’s fluid, it results in a male by Allah’s permission. When the woman’s fluid prevails over the man’s fluid, it results in a female by Allah’s permission.”
The Jewish man said, “You have indeed spoken the truth, and you are certainly a prophet.” He then turned and departed.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“This man asked me about matters of which I had no knowledge until Allah gave me knowledge of them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (315:34).

9706 — Anas ibn Mālik said:
A Christian man embraced Islam and learned Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat Āl ʿImrān. He used to write for the Prophet ﷺ, but he later returned to Christianity and would say, “Muhammad knows only what I wrote for him.”
Allah caused him to die, and his people buried him. By morning, the earth had cast him out.
They said, “Muhammad and his Companions did this. Because he fled from them, they dug up our companion and threw him out.”
They dug another grave for him and made it deeper. By morning, the earth had again cast him out.
They again said, “Muhammad and his Companions did this. Because he fled from them, they dug up our companion and threw him out.”
They then dug as deeply into the ground as they possibly could and buried him. By morning, the earth had once more cast him out. They realized that this was not the work of human beings, so they left his body exposed.
Another wording states:
There was a man from Banū al-Najjār among us who had learned Sūrat al-Baqarah and Sūrat Āl ʿImrān and used to write for the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. He fled and joined the People of the Book.
They held him in high esteem, saying, “This man used to write for Muhammad,” and they admired him.
It was not long before Allah caused him to die among them. They dug a grave and concealed him in it, but by morning the earth had thrown him onto its surface. They dug another grave and buried him, but by morning the earth had again thrown him onto its surface. They dug and buried him a third time, but by morning the earth had once more thrown him onto its surface, so they left him lying exposed.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3617) and Muslim (2781).

9707 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib said:
Abū Bakr came to my father at his home and purchased a camel saddle from him. He said to ʿĀzib, “Send your son with me to carry it.”
I carried it with him, while my father accompanied us to receive its price. My father said, “Abū Bakr, tell me what happened when you traveled by night with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Abū Bakr replied:
Yes. We traveled throughout that night and continued through the following day until midday arrived and the road became deserted, with no one passing along it.
A tall rock appeared before us with a shaded area untouched by the sun. We stopped beside it. With my own hands, I leveled a place where the Prophet ﷺ could sleep. I spread a hide over it and said, “Sleep, O Messenger of Allah, while I examine and clear the surroundings for you.”
He slept, and I went out to inspect the surroundings. I then saw a shepherd approaching the rock with his sheep, seeking the same shade that we had sought.
I asked him, “Whose servant are you, young man?”
He replied, “A man from Madinah”—or Makkah.
I asked, “Do your sheep have any milk?”
He replied, “Yes.”
I asked, “Will you milk one for us?”
He replied, “Yes.”
He took hold of a sheep, and I told him, “Clear its udder of dust, hair, and dirt.”
At this point, I saw al-Barāʾ striking one hand against the other to demonstrate how the shepherd cleaned it.
The shepherd milked a quantity of milk into a wooden bowl. I had with me a leather water vessel that I had carried for the Prophet ﷺ so that he could drink and perform ablution from it.
I returned to the Prophet ﷺ. I disliked awakening him, but I arrived just as he awoke. I poured water over the milk until its lower portion became cool and said, “Drink, O Messenger of Allah.”
He drank until I was satisfied that he had had enough. He then asked:
“Has the time not come to depart?”
I replied, “Yes.”
We resumed our journey after the sun had passed its zenith. Surāqah ibn Mālik began pursuing us. I said, “They have reached us, O Messenger of Allah!”
He replied:
“Do not grieve; indeed, Allah is with us.”
The Prophet ﷺ supplicated against Surāqah, and his horse sank into the firm ground up to its belly—or approximately that far; Zuhayr was uncertain.
Surāqah said, “I believe that you have supplicated against me. Supplicate for me now, and I swear by Allah that I shall turn the pursuers away from you.”
The Prophet ﷺ supplicated for him, and he was saved. Thereafter, whenever Surāqah encountered anyone searching for them, he would say, “I have dealt with everything in this direction for you.” He turned back everyone he met.
Abū Bakr said:
“He faithfully fulfilled his promise to us.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3615) and Muslim (2009:75).

9708 — Al-Barāʾ ibn ʿĀzib reported:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent a group of Anṣār to Abū Rāfiʿ the Jew and appointed ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAtīk as their leader. Abū Rāfiʿ used to harm the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and assist others against him. He lived in a fortress in the land of the Ḥijāz.
When the men drew near after sunset, the people were returning with their grazing animals. ʿAbdullāh said to his companions, “Remain where you are. I shall go ahead and deal tactfully with the gatekeeper so that I may be able to enter.”
He approached the gate and, when he came near it, covered himself with his garment as though relieving himself. By then, the people had entered the fortress.
The gatekeeper called to him, “Servant of Allah, if you wish to enter, then enter, for I am about to close the gate.”
ʿAbdullāh continued:
I entered and concealed myself. When all the people had entered, the gatekeeper closed the gate and hung its keys upon a peg. I went to the keys, took them, and opened the gate.
People used to gather with Abū Rāfiʿ in the evening, and he was staying in some upper rooms belonging to him. When his evening guests departed, I climbed up to him. Whenever I opened a door, I locked it behind me from the inside. I thought: If the people discover me, they will not be able to reach me before I kill him.
I eventually reached him. He was in a dark room surrounded by his family, and I did not know where in the room he was. I called, “Abū Rāfiʿ!”
He asked, “Who is that?”
I moved toward the sound and struck him with my sword. I was startled and confused, so the blow accomplished nothing decisive. He cried out, and I left the room.
I waited nearby for a short time and then reentered, saying, “What was that sound, Abū Rāfiʿ?”
He replied, “May your mother be bereaved of you! A man in the house struck me with a sword a moment ago.”
I struck him again with a blow that wounded him severely but did not kill him. I then placed the point of my sword against his abdomen and drove it through until it reached his back. I knew then that I had killed him.
I began opening the doors one after another until I reached a stairway belonging to the house. Thinking I had reached the ground, I stepped forward and fell. It was a moonlit night, and my leg broke. I bound it with my turban.
I then went and sat at the gate, saying to myself: I shall not leave tonight until I know whether I killed him.
When the rooster crowed, a herald stood upon the wall and announced, “I proclaim the death of Abū Rāfiʿ, the merchant of the people of the Ḥijāz.”
I went to my companions and said, “Escape! Allah has killed Abū Rāfiʿ.”
When I reached the Prophet ﷺ, I told him what had happened. He said:
“Extend your leg.”
I extended it, and he passed his hand over it. Thereafter, it was as though I had never suffered any pain in it.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4039).

9709 — Yazīd ibn Abī ʿUbayd said:
I saw the mark of a wound upon Salamah’s leg and asked, “Abū Muslim, what is this wound?”
He replied:
“This is a wound I received on the day of Khaybar. The people said, ‘Salamah has been wounded.’ I went to the Prophet ﷺ, and he blew lightly upon it three times. I have not felt any pain from it up to this very moment.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Military Campaigns (4206), from al-Makkī ibn Ibrāhīm, from Yazīd ibn Abī ʿUbayd, who related it.

9710 — Abū Hurayrah said:
I said, “O Messenger of Allah, I hear many hadiths from you, but I forget them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Spread out your outer garment.”
I spread it out. He made a scooping motion with his hand into it and then said:
“Gather it and hold it against yourself.”
I gathered it against myself, and after that I never forgot a hadith.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3648).

9711 — Abū Zayd ʿAmr ibn Akhṭab al-Anṣārī said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to me:
“Come near me.”
He passed his hand over my head and beard and then supplicated:
“O Allah, make him handsome and preserve his handsomeness.”
He lived to be more than one hundred years old, yet there were only a few scattered white hairs upon his head and in his beard. His face remained smooth and radiant and did not become wrinkled or contracted before his death.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (20733), whose wording this is; al-Tirmidhī (3629); Abū Yaʿlā (6847); and Ibn Ḥibbān (7171).

9712 — Al-Juʿayd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān said:
I saw al-Sāʾib ibn Yazīd when he was ninety-four years old, still strong and upright. He said:
“I know that the continued enjoyment of my hearing and eyesight is due only to the supplication of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. My maternal aunt took me to him and said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, my sister’s son is ill, so supplicate to Allah for him.’ He then supplicated for me.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3540) and Muslim (2345:111).

9713 — Abū al-ʿAlāʾ ibn ʿUmayr said:
I was present with Qatādah ibn Milḥān when death approached him. A man passed by at the far end of the house, and I saw his reflection upon Qatādah’s face. Whenever I looked at him, it appeared as though oil had been applied to his face. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ had passed his hand over his face.
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (20317).

9714 — Sahl ibn Saʿd reported that on the day of Khaybar, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I shall certainly give this banner to a man through whose hands Allah will grant victory. He loves Allah and His Messenger, and Allah and His Messenger love him.”
The people spent the night discussing among themselves which of them would be given the banner. When morning came, they all went early to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, each hoping that it would be given to him.
He asked:
“Where is ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib?”
They replied, “O Messenger of Allah, he is suffering from pain in his eyes.”
The Prophet ﷺ supplicated for him, and he was healed until it was as though he had never suffered any pain.
The remainder of the hadith concerns the account of Khaybar.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4210) and Muslim (2406:34).

9715 — ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib said:
“I have neither suffered inflammation of the eyes nor experienced a headache since the Messenger of Allah ﷺ passed his hand over my face and spat lightly into my eye on the day of Khaybar, when he gave me the banner.”
Good: Narrated in abridged form by Aḥmad (579), Abū Yaʿlā (593)—whose wording this is—and Abū Dāwūd al-Ṭayālisī (185).

9716 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb said:
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ spat lightly upon the leg of ʿAmr ibn Muʿādh when his leg had been severed, and it was healed.
Good: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6509) and Abū Nuʿaym in Maʿrifat al-Ṣaḥābah (4/2036).

9717 — ʿĀʾishah said:
During the illness in which the Prophet ﷺ passed away, he called his daughter Fāṭimah and whispered something to her, whereupon she wept. He then called her again and whispered something to her, whereupon she laughed.
I later asked her about that, and she said:
“The Prophet ﷺ privately informed me that he would pass away during the illness from which he was then suffering, so I wept. He then privately informed me that I would be the first member of his household to follow him, so I laughed.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3625, 3626) and Muslim (2450).

9718 — ʿĀʾishah, Mother of the Believers, said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The first of you to join me will be the one with the longest hand.”
She said:
They began measuring themselves to determine which of them had the longest hand. Zaynab proved to have the longest hand among us because she used to work with her hands and give generously in charity.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2452).
What the Prophet ﷺ meant by “the longest hand” was abundance in charity, not literal physical length. According to the unanimous agreement of the scholars of prophetic biography, Zaynab was the first of his wives to join him after his death, and she was known for giving abundant charity. From this, the omission that occurred in the following narration of al-Bukhārī becomes apparent:

9719 — ʿĀʾishah reported that some of the Prophet’s wives asked him ﷺ:
“Which of us will be the first to join you?”
He replied:
“The one of you with the longest hand.”
They took a reed and began measuring their hands with it, and Sawdah had the longest hand among them. We later came to understand that the meaning of having a long hand was giving in charity. She was the first of us to join him, and she loved giving charity.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1420). Her statement, “She was the first of us to join him,” refers to Zaynab, whose name was omitted from this narration. Zaynab died in the year 20 AH, whereas Sawdah died near the end of ʿUmar’s caliphate, who died in 23 AH.

9720 — Anas reported:
The Prophet ﷺ announced the deaths of Jaʿfar and Zayd before news of their deaths arrived, while tears flowed from his eyes.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3630).

9721 — Anas reported:
Before news reached the people, the Prophet ﷺ announced to them the deaths of Zayd, Jaʿfar, and Ibn Rawāḥah. He said:
“Zayd took the banner and was struck down. Then Jaʿfar took it and was struck down. Then Ibn Rawāḥah took it and was struck down.”
His eyes were shedding tears as he continued:
“Then one of the swords of Allah took the banner, and Allah granted them victory through him.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4262).

9722 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Hour will not be established until two groups fight one another and a great slaughter occurs between them, although their claim is one and the same.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3609) and Muslim (2888:17).
This prophecy was fulfilled through what occurred between ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and Muʿāwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān at Ṣiffīn, between the forces of Syria and Iraq.
The statement, “Their claim is one and the same,” means that their religion is one: both groups affiliate themselves with Islam, and each claims to be upon the truth.

9723 — Abū Bakrah said:
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ upon the pulpit with al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī beside him. He would turn toward the people at one moment and toward al-Ḥasan at another, saying:
“This son of mine is a leader, and perhaps Allah will bring reconciliation through him between two great groups of Muslims.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2704).

9724 — ʿAmr ibn Maymūn reported that he heard ʿAbdullāh ibn Masʿūd narrate from Saʿd ibn Muʿādh, who said:
“Saʿd was a friend of Umayyah ibn Khalaf. Whenever Umayyah passed through Madīnah, he would stay with Saʿd, and whenever Saʿd passed through Makkah, he would stay with Umayyah.
“After the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ arrived in Madīnah, Saʿd set out to perform ʿumrah and stayed with Umayyah in Makkah. He said to Umayyah, ‘Find me a quiet time when I may perhaps circumambulate the House.’
“So Umayyah took him out around midday. Abū Jahl met them and said, ‘O Abū Ṣafwān, who is this with you?’
“He replied, ‘This is Saʿd.’
“Abū Jahl said to him, ‘I see you circumambulating Makkah in safety even though you have given refuge to those who have abandoned our religion, and you claim that you support and assist them. By Allāh, were it not that you are with Abū Ṣafwān, you would not return safely to your family.’
“Saʿd raised his voice at him and said, ‘By Allāh, if you prevent me from this, I will surely prevent you from something even more severe for you: your route through Madīnah.’
“Umayyah said to him, ‘Do not raise your voice, O Saʿd, against Abū al-Ḥakam, the chief of the people of the valley.’
“Saʿd said, ‘Leave us alone, O Umayyah. By Allāh, I heard the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ say that they will kill you.’
“He asked, ‘In Makkah?’
“Saʿd replied, ‘I do not know.’
“Umayyah became extremely frightened by this.
“When Umayyah returned to his family, he said, ‘O Umm Ṣafwān, did you not hear what Saʿd said to me?’
“She asked, ‘What did he say?’
“He replied, ‘He claims that Muḥammad informed them that they will kill me. I asked him, “In Makkah?” and he said, “I do not know.”’
“Umayyah said, ‘By Allāh, I will not leave Makkah.’
“Then, when the Day of Badr came, Abū Jahl called the people to arms, saying, ‘Go and protect your caravan!’
“Umayyah disliked going out. Abū Jahl came to him and said, ‘O Abū Ṣafwān, if the people see that you have stayed behind while you are the chief of the people of the valley, they will remain behind with you.’
“Abū Jahl continued urging him until Umayyah said, ‘Since you have overcome me, by Allāh, I will buy the finest camel in Makkah.’
“Then Umayyah said, ‘O Umm Ṣafwān, prepare me for the journey.’
“She said, ‘O Abū Ṣafwān, have you forgotten what your Yathribī brother said to you?’
“He replied, ‘No. I only intend to accompany them for a short distance.’
“Whenever Umayyah stopped at a place after leaving Makkah, he would tether his camel, remaining prepared to flee. He continued doing so until Allāh, Mighty and Majestic, caused him to be killed at Badr.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3950).

9725 — Sahl ibn Saʿd al-Sāʿidī reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ and the polytheists met in battle and fought one another. When the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ returned toward his camp and the others returned toward their camp, there was a man among the Companions of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ who would pursue every isolated enemy fighter and strike him with his sword.
The people said, “No one among us has been as effective today as so-and-so.”
The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said: “Indeed, he is among the people of the Fire.”
A man from the people said, “I will remain with him continuously.”
So he went out with him. Whenever the man stopped, he stopped with him, and whenever he moved quickly, he moved quickly with him.
The man was then severely wounded and became impatient for death. He placed the point of his sword on the ground and its tip against his chest, then threw his weight upon the sword and killed himself.
The man who had followed him went to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ and said, “I testify that you are the Messenger of Allāh.”
He ﷺ asked: “Why is that?”
He said, “The man whom you mentioned earlier as being among the people of the Fire—the people found that difficult to comprehend, so I said, ‘I will keep watch over him for you.’ I followed him, and then he was severely wounded. He became impatient for death, placed the point of his sword on the ground and its tip between his breasts, then threw himself upon it and killed himself.”
The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ then said:
“Indeed, a man may perform the deeds of the people of Paradise, as it appears to the people, while he is among the people of the Fire; and a man may perform the deeds of the people of the Fire, as it appears to the people, while he is among the people of Paradise.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4203) and Muslim (179:112).
The name of the man who killed himself was Quzmān al-Ẓafarī, and he was among the hypocrites. See: Sharḥ al-Nawawī and Fatḥ al-Bārī (7/473).

9726 — Abū Hurayrah said:
“We were present with the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ at Khaybar. The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said concerning a man who was with him and claimed Islām:
‘This man is among the people of the Fire.’
“When the fighting began, the man fought with extreme intensity and suffered many wounds that incapacitated him.
