“One of the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ came and said, ‘O Messenger of Allāh, what about the man whom you said was among the people of the Fire? He fought in the cause of Allāh with the fiercest fighting and has suffered many wounds.’
“The Prophet ﷺ said: ‘Indeed, he is among the people of the Fire.’
“Some of the Muslims almost began to doubt. While matters stood thus, the man felt the pain of his wounds, reached toward his quiver, pulled out an arrow, and killed himself with it.
“Some Muslim men hurried to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ and said, ‘O Messenger of Allāh, Allāh has confirmed the truth of what you said. So-and-so has killed himself.’
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
‘O Bilāl, stand and announce: None shall enter Paradise except a believer, and indeed Allāh may support this religion by means of a wicked man.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (6606) and Muslim (178:111). The wording is that of al-Bukhārī.
Note: In one narration of Muslim, “Ḥunayn” occurs instead of “Khaybar.” Al-Qāḍī ʿIyāḍ said: The correct wording is Khaybar, with the letter khāʾ.

9727 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn Kaʿb ibn Mālik reported that one of those who witnessed Khaybar with the Prophet ﷺ informed him that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said concerning a man who was with him:
“Indeed, this man is among the people of the Fire.”
When the fighting began, the man fought with extreme intensity until his wounds became numerous.
Some men from among the Companions of the Prophet ﷺ came to him and said, “O Messenger of Allāh, what about the man whom you said was among the people of the Fire? By Allāh, he has fought in the cause of Allāh with the fiercest fighting, and his wounds have become numerous.”
The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“Indeed, he is among the people of the Fire.”
Some of the people nearly began to doubt. While they were in that state, the man felt the pain of his wounds, reached for his quiver, took an arrow from it, and killed himself with it.
A Muslim man hurried to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ and said, “O Prophet of Allāh, Allāh has confirmed the truth of what you said. So-and-so has killed himself.”
Authentic: Narrated by Aḥmad (17218).

9728 — Abū Hurayrah said:
“When Khaybar was conquered, a poisoned sheep was presented to the Prophet ﷺ.
“The Prophet ﷺ said: ‘Gather for me the Jews who are here.’
“They were gathered before him, and he said: ‘I am going to ask you about something. Will you tell me the truth about it?’
“They replied, ‘Yes.’
“The Prophet ﷺ asked them: ‘Who is your father?’
“They replied, ‘So-and-so.’
“He said: ‘You have lied. Rather, your father is so-and-so.’
“They said, ‘You have spoken the truth.’
“He asked: ‘Will you tell me the truth about something if I ask you?’
“They replied, ‘Yes, O Abū al-Qāsim. If we lie, you will recognize our lie just as you recognized it concerning our father.’
“He asked them: ‘Who are the people of the Fire?’
“They replied, ‘We will remain in it only for a short time, then you will replace us in it.’
“The Prophet ﷺ said: ‘Remain disgraced in it! By Allāh, we will never replace you in it.’
“Then he asked: ‘Will you tell me the truth about something if I ask you?’
“They replied, ‘Yes, O Abū al-Qāsim.’
“He asked: ‘Did you put poison in this sheep?’
“They replied, ‘Yes.’
“He asked: ‘What caused you to do that?’
“They replied, ‘We wanted that, if you were a liar, we would be rid of you; and if you were a prophet, it would not harm you.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3169).

9729 — Sulaymān ibn Ṣurad said:
“I heard the Prophet ﷺ say when the Confederates had been driven away from him:
‘Now we will attack them, and they will not attack us. We will march against them.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4110).
His saying, “when the Confederates had been driven away from him,” means: when they had withdrawn and departed from him.

9730 — Abū Ḥumayd said:
“We went out with the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ on the expedition of Tabūk. We came to Wādī al-Qurā and stopped by a garden belonging to a woman.
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said: ‘Estimate its produce.’
“We estimated it, and the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ estimated it at ten wasqs. He said to the woman: ‘Keep account of it until we return to you, if Allāh wills.’
“We continued until we reached Tabūk. The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
‘A severe wind will blow upon you tonight. None of you should stand during it, and whoever has a camel should firmly tie its hobble.’
“A severe wind then blew. A man stood up, and the wind carried him away until it threw him at the two mountains of Ṭayyiʾ.
“The messenger of Ibn al-ʿUlamāʾ, the ruler of Aylah, came to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ carrying a letter and presented him with a white mule. The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ wrote back to him and gave him a mantle as a gift.
“Then we returned until we reached Wādī al-Qurā. The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ asked the woman about her garden:
‘How much did its produce amount to?’
“She replied, ‘Ten wasqs.’
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ then said:
‘I am going to travel quickly. Whoever wishes may hasten with me, and whoever wishes may remain.’
“We continued until we overlooked Madīnah. He said:
‘This is Ṭābah, and this is Uḥud. It is a mountain that loves us and that we love.’
“Then he said:
‘The best dwellings of the Anṣār are the dwellings of Banū al-Najjār, then the dwellings of Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal, then the dwellings of Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj, then the dwellings of Banū Sāʿidah; and there is good in all the dwellings of the Anṣār.’
“Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah caught up with us, and Abū Usayd said to him, ‘Have you not seen that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ ranked the dwellings of the Anṣār and placed us last?’
“Saʿd caught up with the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ and said, ‘O Messenger of Allāh, you ranked the dwellings of the Anṣār and placed us last.’
“He ﷺ said:
‘Is it not enough for you that you are among the best?’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1481) and Muslim (1392:11).
Regarding the wording “then the dwellings of Banū ʿAbd al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj,” this is an error. The correct wording is “Banū al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj,” as found in al-Bukhārī, without the word “ʿAbd.” See: al-Nawawī’s commentary on Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim.

9731 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī said:
“While we were with the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ as he was distributing some property, Dhū al-Khuwayṣirah, a man from Banū Tamīm, came to him and said, ‘O Messenger of Allāh, be just!’
“He ﷺ said:
‘Woe to you! Who would be just if I were not just? You would indeed be ruined and lost if I were not just.’
“ʿUmar said, ‘O Messenger of Allāh, give me permission to strike his neck.’
“He ﷺ said:
‘Leave him. He will have companions in comparison with whose prayer one of you will consider his own prayer insignificant, and in comparison with whose fasting one of you will consider his own fasting insignificant. They will recite the Qurʾān, but it will not go beyond their throats. They will pass out of the religion just as an arrow passes through the game animal.
‘The arrowhead will be examined, but nothing will be found upon it. Then its shaft fitting will be examined, but nothing will be found upon it. Then its shaft itself will be examined, but nothing will be found upon it. Then its feathers will be examined, but nothing will be found upon them, for it will have passed through the dung and blood before either could cling to it.
‘Their sign will be a black man, one of whose upper arms will be like a woman’s breast, or like a piece of flesh that shakes loosely. They will emerge at a time when the people are divided.’”
Abū Saʿīd said:
“I testify that I heard this hadith from the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ, and I testify that ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib fought them while I was with him. He ordered that the man described be sought out, and he was brought. I looked at him and found him exactly according to the description given by the Prophet ﷺ.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3610) and Muslim (1064:148).

9732 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“When Khosrow is destroyed, there will be no Khosrow after him; and when Caesar is destroyed, there will be no Caesar after him. By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, their treasures will surely be spent in the cause of Allāh.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3618) and Muslim (2918:75).

9733 — Jābir ibn Samurah reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“When Khosrow is destroyed, there will be no Khosrow after him; and when Caesar is destroyed, there will be no Caesar after him. By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, their treasures will surely be spent in the cause of Allāh.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3121) and Muslim (2919:77).

9734 — ʿAdī ibn Ḥātim said:
“While I was with the Prophet ﷺ, a man came to him and complained of poverty. Then another man came and complained about the danger of highway robbery.
“He said to me, ‘O ʿAdī, have you seen al-Ḥīrah?’
“I replied, ‘I have not seen it, but I have been informed about it.’
“He said: ‘If you live long enough, you will surely see a woman traveling in her howdah from al-Ḥīrah until she circumambulates the Kaʿbah, fearing no one except Allāh.’
“I said to myself, ‘Then where will the brigands of Ṭayyiʾ be, who have spread havoc throughout the land?’
“He continued: ‘And if you live long enough, the treasures of Khosrow will surely be conquered.’
“I asked, ‘Khosrow ibn Hurmuz?’
“He replied, ‘Khosrow ibn Hurmuz. And if you live long enough, you will surely see a man bring out a handful of gold or silver, seeking someone who will accept it from him, yet he will find no one willing to accept it.
“‘And each one of you will surely meet Allāh on the Day he meets Him, with no interpreter between him and Allāh to translate for him. Allāh will say, “Did I not send you a messenger who conveyed the message to you?” He will reply, “Yes.” He will say, “Did I not give you wealth and children and bestow favors upon you?” He will reply, “Yes.” Then he will look to his right and see nothing but Hell, and he will look to his left and see nothing but Hell.’”
ʿAdī said:
“I heard the Prophet ﷺ say: ‘Protect yourselves from the Fire even with half a date, and whoever cannot find even half a date, then with a good word.’
“I later saw a woman traveling in her howdah from al-Ḥīrah until she circumambulated the Kaʿbah, fearing none but Allāh. And I was among those who conquered the treasures of Khosrow ibn Hurmuz. And if you live long enough, you will indeed see what the Prophet Abū al-Qāsim ﷺ said: ‘A man will bring out a handful.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3595) and Muslim (1016:67).

9735 — Jābir ibn Samurah said:
“I heard the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ say:
‘A group of Muslims—or believers—will surely conquer the treasure of the family of Khosrow that is in al-Abyaḍ.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2919:78).

9736 — Khabbāb ibn al-Aratt said:
“We complained to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ while he was reclining upon his cloak in the shade of the Kaʿbah. We said to him, ‘Will you not seek victory for us? Will you not supplicate to Allāh for us?’
“He said:
‘Among those before you, a man would have a pit dug for him in the ground and be placed inside it. Then a saw would be brought and placed upon his head, and he would be split into two halves, yet that would not turn him away from his religion. Iron combs would be drawn through his flesh down to his bones and sinews, yet that would not turn him away from his religion.
‘By Allāh, this matter will surely be completed until a rider travels from Ṣanʿāʾ to Ḥaḍramawt, fearing none but Allāh—or the wolf concerning his sheep—but you are being impatient.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Signs of Prophethood (3612), from Muḥammad ibn al-Muthannā, from Yaḥyā, from Ismāʿīl, from Qays, from Khabbāb ibn al-Aratt.

9737 — Abū Nawfal said:
“I saw ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr on the pass of Madīnah, and Quraysh and the people were passing by him, until ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar passed by. He stopped and said:
‘Peace be upon you, O Abū Khubayb. Peace be upon you, O Abū Khubayb. Peace be upon you, O Abū Khubayb.
‘By Allāh, I used to forbid you from this. By Allāh, I used to forbid you from this. By Allāh, I used to forbid you from this.
‘By Allāh, as far as I knew you, you were one who fasted often, prayed much, and maintained ties of kinship. By Allāh, a nation of which you are the worst member is certainly a good nation.’
“Then ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar departed.
“When al-Ḥajjāj was informed of where ʿAbdullāh had stood and what he had said, he sent someone to the body, and Ibn al-Zubayr was taken down from the wooden beam and thrown into the graves of the Jews.
“Then al-Ḥajjāj sent for his mother, Asmāʾ bint Abī Bakr, but she refused to come to him. He sent the messenger back to her saying, ‘Either you come to me, or I will send someone who will drag you by your braids.’
“She refused and said, ‘By Allāh, I will not come to you until you send someone to drag me by my braids.’
“He said, ‘Bring me my sandals.’ He took his sandals and hurried along until he entered upon her.
“He said, ‘What do you think of what I have done to the enemy of Allāh?’
“She replied, ‘I see that you have ruined his worldly life, while he has ruined your Hereafter.
‘I have been informed that you used to say to him, “O son of the woman of the two waist-belts!” By Allāh, I am indeed the woman of the two waist-belts. As for one of them, I used it to secure the food of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ and Abū Bakr upon the riding animals. As for the other, it was the waist-belt that a woman cannot do without.
‘The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ informed us that “Among Thaqīf there will be a liar and a destroyer.” As for the liar, we have already seen him. As for the destroyer, I do not think it is anyone but you.’
“So he stood up and left her without responding.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2545:229).
His saying “a liar” refers to al-Mukhtār ibn Abī ʿUbayd al-Thaqafī.
His saying “a destroyer” means one who causes great destruction and killing.
Al-Tirmidhī mentioned from Hishām ibn Ḥassān that he said: “They counted those whom al-Ḥajjāj killed in captivity, and the number reached one hundred and twenty thousand.”

9738 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Musaylimah the Liar came during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ, saying, ‘If Muḥammad appoints me as his successor after him, I will follow him.’
“He came with a large number of his people. The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ went toward him accompanied by Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās, while the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ held a piece of palm stalk in his hand.
“He stood before Musaylimah among his companions and said:
‘Even if you asked me for this piece, I would not give it to you. You will never escape what Allāh has decreed concerning you. If you turn away, Allāh will surely destroy you. I indeed see you as the one concerning whom I was shown what I was shown. And this is Thābit; he will answer you on my behalf.’
“Then he turned away from him.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“I asked about the statement of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ: ‘I indeed see you as the one concerning whom I was shown what I was shown.’
“Abū Hurayrah informed me that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
‘While I was sleeping, I saw two bracelets of gold on my hands, and their matter concerned me. It was revealed to me in the dream that I should blow upon them, so I blew upon them and they flew away. I interpreted them as two liars who would appear after me.’
“One of them was al-ʿAnsī, and the other was Musaylimah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4373, 4374) and Muslim (2273, 2274:21).
His saying “If you turn away, Allāh will surely destroy you” means: if you turn away from obedience to me, Allāh will kill you. This was one of the miracles of Prophethood, for Allāh caused him to be killed on the Day of al-Yamāmah.

9739 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“While I was sleeping, the treasures of the earth were brought to me, and two bracelets of gold were placed in my hands. They became burdensome to me, so it was revealed to me that I should blow upon them. I blew upon them and they disappeared. I interpreted them as the two liars between whom I am situated: the man of Ṣanʿāʾ and the man of al-Yamāmah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4375) and Muslim (2274:22).
The man of Ṣanʿāʾ was al-Aswad al-ʿAnsī, and the man of al-Yamāmah was Musaylimah the Liar, as explicitly stated in al-Bukhārī (3621).

9740 — Jābir reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ returned from a journey. When he came near Madīnah, a violent wind arose that almost buried the rider.
He reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“This wind has been sent because of the death of a hypocrite.”
When he arrived in Madīnah, it was found that a prominent hypocrite had died.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2782:15).

9741 — Abū Shahm said:
“A slave girl passed by me in Madīnah, and I grabbed her by her side.
“The next morning, the Messenger—meaning the Prophet ﷺ—was accepting the people’s pledges of allegiance. I came to him, but he did not accept my pledge.
“He said: ‘The one who grabbed her!’
“I said, ‘By Allāh, I will never do it again.’
“Then he accepted my pledge.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (7288) and Aḥmad (22511), and authenticated by al-Ḥākim (4/377).

9742 — Al-Faltān ibn ʿĀṣim said:
“We were sitting with the Prophet ﷺ in the mosque when he fixed his gaze upon a man walking in the mosque.
“He said, ‘O so-and-so, do you testify that I am the Messenger of Allāh?’
“He replied, ‘No.’
“He asked, ‘Do you read the Torah?’
“He replied, ‘Yes.’
“He asked, ‘And the Gospel?’
“He replied, ‘Yes.’
“He asked, ‘And the Qurʾān?’
“He replied, ‘By the One in Whose hand is my soul, if I wished, I could recite it.’
“Then he questioned him and said:
‘Do you find mention of me in the Torah and the Gospel?’
“He replied, ‘We find someone like you, a nation like your nation, and a place of emergence like yours. We had hoped that he would arise among us. But when you appeared, we feared that you might be him. Then we examined the description and found that you were not he.’
“He ﷺ asked, ‘Why is that?’
“The man replied, ‘With him will be seventy thousand from his nation upon whom there will be no reckoning and no punishment, whereas only a small number are with you.’
“He ﷺ said:
‘By the One in Whose hand is my soul, I am indeed that man, and it is indeed my nation. And they will be more than seventy thousand, and seventy thousand, and seventy thousand.’”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (3700), Ibn Ḥibbān (6580)—whose wording this is—and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (18/332–333).

9743 — A man from the Anṣār said:
“We went out with the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ for a funeral. When we returned, we met the messenger of a Qurashī woman.
“He said, ‘O Messenger of Allāh, so-and-so invites you and those with you to a meal.’
“So he turned aside toward her, and we went with him. We sat in the places of boys in front of their fathers.
“Then the food was brought. The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ put his hand toward it, and the people put their hands toward it.
“They noticed that he was chewing a morsel but could not swallow it, so they withdrew their hands. They initially forgot about us, then remembered us and took hold of our hands. A man would strike the morsel from our hands until it fell.
“Then they kept hold of our hands and watched what the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ would do. He spat the morsel out and threw it away, saying:
‘I sense that this is the meat of a sheep that was taken without its owners’ permission.’
“The woman stood and said, ‘O Messenger of Allāh, I had wanted to gather you and those with you for a meal. I sent someone to al-Baqīʿ but found no sheep for sale. ʿĀmir ibn Abī Waqqāṣ had purchased a sheep from al-Baqīʿ yesterday, so I sent someone to him saying, “Find me a sheep in al-Baqīʿ.” None was found, and I was told that you had purchased a sheep, so I sent to you asking that it be sent to me. The messenger did not find him, but found his family, and they handed it over to my messenger.’
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
‘Feed it to the prisoners.’”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (22509)—whose wording this is—and Abū Dāwūd (3332).

9744 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās said:
“During the illness from which the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ died, he came out wrapped in a sheet, with a dark-colored band tied around his head, until he sat upon the pulpit.
“He praised Allāh and extolled Him, then said:
‘To proceed: The people will increase in number while the Anṣār will decrease, until they become among the people like salt in food. Whoever among you is placed in authority over something by which he may harm some people and benefit others, let him accept the good deeds of the virtuous among the Anṣār and overlook the faults of those among them who do wrong.’
“This was the last gathering in which the Prophet ﷺ sat.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3628).

9745 — Buraydah ibn al-Ḥuṣayb said that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“One hundred years will not pass while an eye that now blinks remains alive.”
In another narration:
“Indeed, Allāh, Blessed and Most High, will send a wind at the end of one hundred years, and it will take the soul of every believer.”
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār (228, 229) in Kashf al-Astār.
This prophecy was fulfilled. Abū al-Ṭufayl ʿĀmir ibn Wāthilah al-Kinānī, who was born in the year of Uḥud and lived for eight years during the lifetime of the Prophet ﷺ, was the last of the Companions to die, in the year 110 AH. Before his death, he used to say: “There is no man on the face of the earth today who saw the Prophet ﷺ other than me.”

9746 — Nāfiʿ ibn ʿUtbah said:
“We were with the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ on an expedition when some people came to the Prophet ﷺ from the direction of the west, wearing woolen garments. They met him near a small hill while they were standing and the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ was seated.
“I said to myself, ‘Go to them and stand between them and him, lest they assassinate him.’
“Then I thought, ‘Perhaps he is speaking privately with them.’
“So I went and stood between them and him. I memorized four statements from him, counting them on my hand. He said:
‘You will fight the Arabian Peninsula, and Allāh will grant you victory over it. Then Persia, and Allāh will grant you victory over it. Then you will fight the Romans, and Allāh will grant you victory over them. Then you will fight the Dajjāl, and Allāh will grant you victory over him.’
“Nāfiʿ then said, ‘O Jābir, we do not think that the Dajjāl will emerge until the Romans have been conquered.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2900:38).

9747 — ʿAwf ibn Mālik reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ stood among his Companions and said:
“Is it poverty that you fear, or need, or is it this worldly life that concerns you? Indeed, Allāh will open for you the lands of Persia and Rome, and the wealth of this world will be poured upon you abundantly, until nothing after me will cause you to deviate, if anything does, except it.”
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (23982) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (18/52).

9748 — Abū Dharr reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“You will conquer Egypt, a land in which the qīrāṭ is commonly mentioned. When you conquer it, treat its people well, for they have a covenant and ties of kinship—or he said: a covenant and ties through marriage.
“And when you see two men disputing there over the space of a single brick, then leave it.”
Abū Dharr said:
“I later saw ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Shuraḥbīl ibn Ḥasanah and his brother Rabīʿah disputing over the space of a single brick, so I left Egypt.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2543:227).

9749 — Abū Bakrah said:
“Allāh benefited me through a statement that I heard from the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ during the events of the Camel, after I had nearly joined the people of the Camel and fought alongside them.
“When the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ was informed that the people of Persia had appointed the daughter of Khosrow as their ruler, he said:
‘A people who entrust their affairs to a woman will never prosper.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Military Campaigns (4425).

9750 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“When Quraysh accused me of lying, I stood in al-Ḥijr, and Allāh displayed Bayt al-Maqdis before me. I then began describing its signs to them while looking directly at it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (3886) and Muslim (170:276).

9751 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“I remember myself standing in al-Ḥijr while Quraysh questioned me about my Night Journey. They asked me about certain details of Bayt al-Maqdis that I had not firmly retained, and I became distressed with a distress unlike anything I had ever experienced.
“Then Allāh raised it before me so that I could look at it. They did not ask me about anything except that I informed them of it…” The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (172:278).

9752 — Anas reported that when news reached the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ that Abū Sufyān was approaching, he consulted his Companions.
“Abū Bakr spoke, but he turned away from him. Then ʿUmar spoke, but he turned away from him.
“Saʿd ibn ʿUbādah stood and said, ‘Is it us you mean, O Messenger of Allāh? By the One in Whose hand is my soul, if you commanded us to plunge into the sea, we would plunge into it. And if you commanded us to drive our camels all the way to Bark al-Ghimād, we would do so.’
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ then called the people to march. They set out until they encamped at Badr.
“Some water-carriers of Quraysh came to them, among them a black slave belonging to Banū al-Ḥajjāj. They seized him.
“The Companions of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ began asking him about Abū Sufyān and his companions.
“He would say, ‘I have no knowledge of Abū Sufyān. But Abū Jahl, ʿUtbah, Shaybah, and Umayyah ibn Khalaf are here.’
“Whenever he said this, they would strike him.
“He would then say, ‘Yes, I will tell you. Abū Sufyān is here.’
“When they stopped striking him and questioned him again, he would say, ‘I have no knowledge of Abū Sufyān, but Abū Jahl, ʿUtbah, Shaybah, and Umayyah ibn Khalaf are among the people.’
“Whenever he said this, they would strike him again.
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ was standing in prayer. When he saw this, he finished and said:
‘By the One in Whose hand is my soul, you strike him when he tells you the truth, and you leave him alone when he lies to you!’
“Then the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
‘This is the place where so-and-so will be killed.’
“He would place his hand upon the ground and say:
‘Here, and here.’
“Not one of them moved away from the place where the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ had placed his hand.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1779:83).
His saying “none of them moved away” means: none of them fell at a distance from the place indicated.

9753 — Thawbān reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“Indeed, Allāh drew the earth together for me, and I saw its eastern and western extremities. The dominion of my nation will surely reach all that was drawn together for me…” The hadith continues.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (2889:19).
Al-Nawawī said:
“This hadith contains an indication that the dominion of this nation would extend primarily in the directions of the east and the west, and this is exactly what occurred. As for its extension toward the south and the north, it was comparatively less than its extension toward the east and the west.”

9754 — Ḥārithah ibn Wahb said:
I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say:
“Give in charity, for soon a man will walk about carrying his charity, but the person to whom it is offered will say, ‘Had you brought it to us yesterday, I would have accepted it, but now I have no need of it.’ He will then find no one willing to accept it.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1411) and Muslim (1011:58).

9755 — Abū Mūsā reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“A time will certainly come upon the people when a man will go about carrying charity in gold but will find no one willing to take it from him. One man will be seen followed by forty women who seek refuge with him because of the scarcity of men and the abundance of women.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Zakāh (1414) and Muslim in the Book of Zakāh (1012:59), both of them from Abū Kurayb Muḥammad ibn al-ʿAlāʾ: Abū Usāmah narrated to us, from Burayd, from Abū Burdah, from Abū Mūsā, and he mentioned it.

9756 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Prophet ﷺ said:
“The Hour will not be established until wealth becomes so abundant among you that it overflows, until the owner of wealth becomes concerned about who will accept his charity. He will offer it to someone, but the person to whom it is offered will say, ‘I have no need of it.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (1412) and Muslim (157:60).
Muslim also narrated (157:60) that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Hour will not be established until wealth becomes abundant and overflows, until a man goes out carrying the zakāh of his wealth but finds no one willing to accept it from him, and until the land of Arabia returns to being meadows and rivers.”
I say: What the Prophet ﷺ foretold was indeed fulfilled, as wealth became so abundant that all the people were free of need in the year 100 AH, during the caliphate of ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, may Allah have mercy upon him. Al-Bayhaqī stated something similar in Dalāʾil al-Nubuwwah (6/323).

9757 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“The Hour will not be established until a fire emerges from the land of the Ḥijāz that illuminates the necks of the camels at Buṣrā.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7118) and Muslim (2902:42).
Al-Nawawī said:
“A fire did indeed emerge in our time at Madīnah in the year 654 AH. It was an exceedingly great fire on the eastern side of Madīnah, beyond the volcanic tract. Knowledge of it was mass-transmitted among all the people of al-Shām and the other lands, and some of the people of Madīnah who witnessed it informed me about it.”

9758 — Al-Ḥasan ibn Ayyūb al-Ḥaḍramī said:
ʿAbdullāh ibn Busr showed me a mark upon the side of his head, so I placed my finger upon it. He said:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ placed his finger upon it and then said, ‘You will certainly reach a century.’”
Abū ʿAbdullāh said: He had long hair reaching his shoulders.
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (17689) and Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Āḥād wa-al-Mathānī (1343).

9759 — Abū Hurayrah said:
My truthful beloved, the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, told me:
“There will be an expedition from this nation to Sindh and India.”
If I live to witness it and am martyred, then that will be so; but if I—then he mentioned a word—return, I shall be Abū Hurayrah, the one set free, whom Allah has delivered from the Fire.
Good: Narrated by Aḥmad (8823).
The Muslims campaigned in India during the time of Muʿāwiyah in the year 44 AH. Thereafter, campaigns continued at the hands of Muḥammad ibn al-Qāsim, Maḥmūd ibn Sabuktakīn, and others. See al-Bidāyah wa-al-Nihāyah (9/218–219). India remained part of the abode of Islam for approximately eight centuries, and thus the glad tidings of the Prophet ﷺ were fulfilled.

9760 — Jābir ibn ʿAbdullāh said:
We returned with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ from a journey. When we reached one of the gardens belonging to Banū al-Najjār, there was a camel inside it that would charge anyone who entered the garden.
They mentioned this to the Prophet ﷺ. He came to the garden and called the camel. It approached him with its muzzle lowered toward the ground until it knelt before him.
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Bring me its halter.”
He placed the halter upon it and handed it over to its owner. He then turned toward the people and said:
“There is nothing between heaven and earth except that it knows I am the Messenger of Allah, apart from the disobedient among the jinn and humankind.”
Good: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (14333), ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd (1122), and al-Dārimī (18).

9761 — Ṭāriq ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Muḥāribī said:
I saw the Messenger of Allah ﷺ in the marketplace of Dhū al-Majāz wearing a red suit of clothing and saying:
“O people, say, ‘There is no deity worthy of worship except Allah,’ and you will succeed.”
A man followed him, throwing stones at him until he had made his heels and the tendons above them bleed. The man was saying:
“O people, do not obey him, for he is a liar.”
I asked, “Who is this?”
It was said, “This is the young man of Banū ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.”
I asked, “Then who is this man following him and throwing stones at him?”
They replied, “This is ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā, Abū Lahab.”
He continued:
When Islam became dominant, we set out until we camped near Madīnah, accompanied by one of our women. While we were sitting, a man wearing two white garments came to us. He greeted us and asked:
“From where have the people come?”
We replied, “From al-Rabadhah.”
We had a camel with us, and he asked:
“Will you sell this camel?”
We replied, “Yes.”
He asked, “For how much?”
We said, “For such-and-such a number of ṣāʿs of dates.”
He took it without asking us to reduce the price and said:
“I have taken it.”
He then disappeared among the gardens of Madīnah. We began reproaching one another, saying:
“You gave your camel to a man whom you do not know!”
The woman said:
“Do not reproach one another. I saw the face of a man who would never treat you contemptuously. I have never seen anything more closely resembling the moon on the night when it is full than his face.”
In the evening, a man came to us, greeted us, and said:
“I am the messenger of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to you. He says, ‘You may eat until you are satisfied and measure out for yourselves until you receive your payment in full.’”
He said:
We ate until we were satisfied and measured out the dates until we had received our payment in full. The following day, we entered Madīnah and found the Messenger of Allah ﷺ standing upon the pulpit delivering a sermon. He was saying:
“The hand of the giver is the upper hand. Begin with those whom you support: your mother and your father, your sister and your brother, and then those closest to you, followed by those next closest.”
A man stood up and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Banū Thaʿlabah ibn Yarbūʿ killed so-and-so during the pre-Islamic period, so take vengeance for us upon them.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ raised his hands until I could see the whiteness of his armpits and said:
“Indeed, no mother shall bear the guilt of her child! Indeed, no mother shall bear the guilt of her child!”
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī (4839) and Ibn Mājah (2670), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6562) and al-Ḥākim (2/611–612), all through Yazīd ibn Ziyād ibn Abī al-Jaʿd, from Jāmiʿ ibn Shaddād, from Ṭāriq ibn ʿAbdullāh al-Muḥāribī. The wording is Ibn Ḥibbān’s; al-Nasāʾī and Ibn Mājah confined themselves to the portion concerning liability. Its chain is good because of Yazīd ibn Ziyād ibn Abī al-Jaʿd.

9762 — Anas ibn Mālik said:
We were forbidden from asking the Messenger of Allah ﷺ unnecessary questions, so we were pleased whenever an intelligent man from the desert came and questioned him while we listened.
A Bedouin man came and said:
“O Muḥammad, your messenger came to us and claimed that you say Allah has sent you.”
He replied, “He spoke the truth.”
The man asked, “Who created the sky?”
He replied, “Allah.”
He asked, “Who created the earth?”
He replied, “Allah.”
He asked, “Who erected these mountains and placed within them whatever He placed?”
He replied, “Allah.”
The man said, “By the One who created the sky, created the earth, and erected these mountains, did Allah send you?”
He replied, “Yes.”
The man said:
“Your messenger also claimed that we are required to perform five prayers during each day and night.”
He replied, “He spoke the truth.”
The man asked, “By the One who sent you, did Allah command you to do this?”
He replied, “Yes.”
The man said, “Your messenger claimed that zakāh is due from our wealth.”
He replied, “He spoke the truth.”
The man asked, “By the One who sent you, did Allah command you to do this?”
He replied, “Yes.”
The man said, “Your messenger claimed that we must fast the month of Ramadan each year.”
He replied, “He spoke the truth.”
The man asked, “By the One who sent you, did Allah command you to do this?”
He replied, “Yes.”
The man said:
“Your messenger claimed that whoever among us is able to find a way to the House must perform its pilgrimage.”
He replied, “He spoke the truth.”
The man then turned away, saying:
“By the One who sent you with the truth, I shall neither add anything to these nor omit anything from them.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“If he is truthful, he will certainly enter Paradise.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (10:12) and al-Bukhārī (63).

9763 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Tomorrow, a people will come to you whose hearts are more tender toward Islam than yours.”
The Ashʿarīs then arrived, among them Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī. As they drew near Madīnah, they began chanting:
“Tomorrow we shall meet the beloved ones:
 Muḥammad and his companions.”
When they arrived, they shook hands with one another, and they were the first to introduce handshaking.
Authentic: Narrated by Imām Aḥmad (12582) and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (7193).

9764 — Abū Hurayrah said:
While travelling to join the Prophet ﷺ, I recited:
“What a night, for all its length and hardship—
 yet it delivered me from the abode of disbelief.”
A servant of mine ran away during the journey. When I reached the Prophet ﷺ, I pledged allegiance to him. While I was with him, the servant suddenly appeared.
The Prophet ﷺ said to me:
“O Abū Hurayrah, here is your servant.”
I replied:
“He is free for the sake of Allah.”
I then set him free.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4393).

9765 — Jābir reported that al-Ṭufayl ibn ʿAmr al-Dawsī came to the Prophet ﷺ and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, would you like the protection of a strong fortress and a place of defence?”
The narrator said: It was a fortress belonging to Daws during the pre-Islamic period.
The Prophet ﷺ declined because of what Allah had reserved for the Anṣār. When the Prophet ﷺ emigrated to Madīnah, al-Ṭufayl ibn ʿAmr emigrated to join him, accompanied by a man from his tribe. They found the climate of Madīnah unhealthy. The man became ill and lost patience. He took some broad-headed arrows and cut the joints of his fingers with them. His hands bled profusely until he died.
Al-Ṭufayl ibn ʿAmr later saw him in a dream. He appeared in a good condition, but al-Ṭufayl saw him covering his hands. He asked him:
“What has your Lord done with you?”
He replied:
“He forgave me because of my emigration to His Prophet ﷺ.”
Al-Ṭufayl asked:
“Why do I see you covering your hands?”
He replied:
“I was told, ‘We will not restore what you yourself have ruined.’”
Al-Ṭufayl related the dream to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, whereupon the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“O Allah, forgive even his hands.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (116).

9766 — Abū Hurayrah said:
Al-Ṭufayl and his companions came and said:
“O Messenger of Allah, Daws has persisted in disbelief and refused to accept Islam, so supplicate to Allah against them.”
It was said, “Daws is ruined!”
But he ﷺ said:
“O Allah, guide Daws and bring them to me.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2937) and Muslim (2524).

9767 — Abū Jamrah said:
I used to sit with Ibn ʿAbbās, and he would seat me upon his couch. He said to me:
“Stay with me so that I may assign you a share from my wealth.”
I remained with him for two months. He then said:
When the delegation of ʿAbd al-Qays came to the Prophet ﷺ, he asked:
“Who are these people—or who is this delegation?”
They replied, “Rabīʿah.”
He said:
“Welcome to the people—or the delegation—who shall suffer neither disgrace nor regret.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, we are unable to come to you except during the sacred month, for between us and you lies this tribe of the disbelievers of Muḍar. Therefore, command us with a decisive matter that we may convey to those whom we have left behind and by which we may enter Paradise.”
They also asked him about drinks. He commanded them to observe four things and forbade them four things. He commanded them to believe in Allah alone and asked:
“Do you know what belief in Allah alone means?”
They replied:
“Allah and His Messenger know best.”
He said:
“To testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah; to establish prayer, give zakāh, fast Ramadan, and give one-fifth of the spoils of war.”
He forbade them four types of vessels: al-ḥantam, al-dubbāʾ, al-naqīr, and al-muzaffat—perhaps he said al-muqayyar—and told them:
“Memorize these instructions and convey them to those whom you have left behind.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (53) and Muslim (17).
Muslim added in another narration that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said to al-Ashajj—the Ashajj of ʿAbd al-Qays:
“You possess two qualities that Allah loves: forbearance and deliberateness.”
The term “al-muqayyar” means al-muzaffat: a vessel coated with pitch or tar.
Al-Ḥāfiẓ Ibn Kathīr said:
“The context of Ibn ʿAbbās’s hadith indicates that the delegation of ʿAbd al-Qays arrived before the Conquest of Makkah, because they said: ‘Between us and you lies this tribe of Muḍar, and we cannot reach you except during a sacred month.’”
Al-Bidāyah wa-al-Nihāyah (7/251).

9768 — Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī said:
Some people from ʿAbd al-Qays came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and said:
“O Prophet of Allah, we are a tribe of Rabīʿah, and between us and you are the disbelievers of Muḍar. We are unable to reach you except during the sacred months. Therefore, command us with something that we may command those whom we have left behind and by which we may enter Paradise if we adhere to it.”
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“I command you to observe four things and forbid you four things. Worship Allah and associate nothing with Him, establish prayer, give zakāh, fast Ramadan, and give one-fifth of the spoils of war. I forbid you four vessels: al-dubbāʾ, al-ḥantam, al-muzaffat, and al-naqīr.”
They asked:
“O Prophet of Allah, what do you know about al-naqīr?”
He replied:
“Indeed, it is a tree trunk that you hollow out. You place small dates—or Saʿīd said: dates—inside it and then pour water over them. When its fermentation subsides, you drink it until one of you strikes his cousin with the sword.”
There was among the delegation a man who had suffered such an injury. He said:
“I used to conceal it out of embarrassment before the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
I asked:
“Then from what should we drink, O Messenger of Allah?”
He replied:
“From leather waterskins whose mouths are tied with cords.”
They said:
“O Messenger of Allah, our land has many rats, and leather waterskins do not survive there.”
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ replied:
“Even if the rats eat them; even if the rats eat them; even if the rats eat them.”
The Prophet of Allah ﷺ also said to al-Ashajj of ʿAbd al-Qays:
“You possess two qualities that Allah loves: forbearance and deliberateness.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (18).

9769 — Thumāmah ibn Ḥazn al-Qushayrī said:
I met ʿĀʾishah and asked her about nabīdh. She informed me that the delegation of ʿAbd al-Qays came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked him about nabīdh. He forbade them from preparing it in gourds, hollowed-out tree trunks, pitch-coated vessels, and earthenware jars.
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1995:37).

9770 — Saʿīd ibn al-Musayyib said:
I heard ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar speaking beside this pulpit—and he pointed toward the pulpit of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ—saying:
“The delegation of ʿAbd al-Qays came to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ and asked him about drinks. He forbade them gourds, hollowed-out tree trunks, and earthenware jars.”
I asked him:
“O Abū Muḥammad, what about pitch-coated vessels?”
We thought he had forgotten to mention them. He replied:
“I did not hear it from ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUmar that day, although he disliked them.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1997:58).

9771 — Ṭalq ibn ʿAlī said:
We were sitting with the Prophet ﷺ when the delegation of ʿAbd al-Qays arrived. He asked:
“Why have your complexions turned yellow, your stomachs become enlarged, and your veins become prominent?”
They replied:
“Our leader came to you and asked you about a drink that suited us, but you forbade him from it, and we live in a fever-ridden land.”
He said:
“Then drink whatever wholesome drink suits you.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Abī Shaybah (24368).
His statement, “Drink whatever wholesome drink suits you,” applies if it is not intoxicating. As for an intoxicating drink, it is not permitted, because its prohibition has already been established.

9772 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
A delegation from Banū Asad came to the Prophet ﷺ. They spoke clearly and eloquently, saying:
“O Messenger of Allah, all of Muḍar fought against you, but we did not fight you. We are neither the least numerous among them nor the weakest in strength, and we maintained your ties of kinship.”
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ heard their words, he asked Abū Bakr and ʿUmar:
“Do they speak in this manner?”
They replied:
“O Messenger of Allah, their understanding is indeed limited, and Satan is certainly speaking upon their tongues.”
Another narration adds that the following verse was revealed:
“They regard their acceptance of Islam as a favour to you. Say, ‘Do not regard your Islam as a favour to me. Rather, Allah has conferred favour upon you by guiding you to faith, if you are truthful.’” [Al-Ḥujurāt: 17]
Good: Narrated by al-Nasāʾī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (11455), Abū Yaʿlā (2363), and al-Bazzār (5141).

9773 — Al-Barāʾ said:
The Prophet ﷺ sent Khālid ibn al-Walīd to the people of Yemen to invite them to Islam, but they did not respond to him. The Prophet ﷺ then sent ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and instructed him to send Khālid and those accompanying him back, except that anyone who wished to remain and continue with ʿAlī could do so.
Al-Barāʾ said:
I was among those who remained with him. When we drew near the people, they came out to meet us. ʿAlī led us in prayer and arranged us in a single row. He then stepped forward before us and read the letter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ to them. All of Hamdān embraced Islam.
ʿAlī wrote to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ informing him of their acceptance of Islam. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ read the letter, he fell down in prostration. He then raised his head and said:
“Peace be upon Hamdān! Peace be upon Hamdān!”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bayhaqī in al-Sunan al-Kubrā (2/369).

9774 — Ḥudhayfah said:
“Al-ʿĀqib and al-Sayyid, the two leaders of Najrān, came to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ intending to invoke mutual cursing with him.
“One of them said to the other, ‘Do not do it. By Allāh, if he truly is a Prophet and we invoke mutual cursing against him, neither we nor our descendants after us will prosper.’
“They said, ‘We will give you what you have asked of us, but send with us a trustworthy man, and do not send with us anyone except one who is truly trustworthy.’
“He ﷺ said:
‘I will certainly send with you a man who is trustworthy in the fullest sense of the word.’
“The Companions of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ eagerly looked forward to being chosen.
“He said:
‘Stand, O Abū ʿUbaydah ibn al-Jarrāḥ.’
“When he stood, the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
‘This is the trustworthy man of this nation.’”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4380) and Muslim (2420:55).

9775 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ concluded a treaty with the people of Najrān for two thousand garments: half to be paid in Ṣafar and half in Rajab, which they would deliver to the Muslims.
“They were also to lend thirty coats of armor, thirty horses, thirty camels, and thirty items of every category of weaponry for use in military expeditions. The Muslims would be responsible for guaranteeing those items until they were returned to them if treachery or hostile action occurred in Yemen.
“The agreement stipulated that no church of theirs would be demolished, no priest of theirs would be expelled, and they would not be forced away from their religion, so long as they did not introduce some violation or consume usury.”
Ismāʿīl said: “They did consume usury.”
Abū Dāwūd said: “If they violated any part of what had been stipulated upon them, then they had committed a breach.”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3041), al-Bayhaqī (9/187), and al-Ḍiyāʾ al-Maqdisī in al-Mukhtārah (9/508).

9776 — ʿImrān ibn Ḥuṣayn said:
“Banū Tamīm came to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ, and he said:
‘Receive glad tidings, O Banū Tamīm.’
“They replied, ‘Since you have given us glad tidings, then give us something.’
“The expression of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ changed.
“Then some people from Yemen came, and the Prophet ﷺ said:
‘Accept the glad tidings, since Banū Tamīm did not accept them.’
“They said, ‘We accept them, O Messenger of Allāh.’”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4386).

9777 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“The people of Yemen have come to you. They have the most tender hearts and the softest hearts. Faith is Yemeni, and wisdom is Yemeni. Pride and arrogance are among the owners of camels, while tranquility and dignity are among the people who tend sheep.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4388) and Muslim (52:91).

9778 — ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Zubayr reported:
“A group from Banū Tamīm came to the Prophet ﷺ.
“Abū Bakr said, ‘Appoint al-Qaʿqāʿ ibn Maʿbad ibn Zurārah as their leader.’
“ʿUmar said, ‘Rather, appoint al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis.’
“Abū Bakr said, ‘You only intended to contradict me.’
“ʿUmar replied, ‘I did not intend to contradict you.’
“They continued disputing until their voices became raised.
“Then concerning that, the verse was revealed:
‘O you who believe, do not put yourselves forward…’ [al-Ḥujurāt: 1], continuing to the end of the passage.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4367).

9779 — Ibn Abī Mulaykah said:
“The two excellent men, Abū Bakr and ʿUmar, nearly came to ruin when the delegation of Banū Tamīm came to the Prophet ﷺ.
“One of them suggested al-Aqraʿ ibn Ḥābis al-Ḥanẓalī, the brother of Banū Mujāshiʿ, while the other suggested someone else.
“Abū Bakr said to ʿUmar, ‘You only intended to contradict me.’
“ʿUmar replied, ‘I did not intend to contradict you.’
“Their voices became raised in the presence of the Prophet ﷺ, so the following was revealed:
‘O you who believe, do not raise your voices above the voice of the Prophet…’ until His saying, ‘a tremendous reward.’ [al-Ḥujurāt: 2–3]
Ibn Abī Mulaykah said that Ibn al-Zubayr said:
“After that, whenever ʿUmar spoke to the Prophet ﷺ, he would speak almost like someone whispering a secret. The Prophet ﷺ could scarcely hear him until he asked him to repeat himself.”
He did not mention this concerning his father, meaning Abū Bakr.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7302).

9780 — Al-Ashʿath ibn Qays said:
“I came to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ as part of the delegation of Kindah, and they regarded me as the best among them.
“I said, ‘O Messenger of Allāh, are you not from us?’
“He replied:
‘We are the descendants of al-Naḍr ibn Kinānah. We do not falsely attribute ourselves through our mother, nor do we deny our father.’”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Mājah (2616) and Aḥmad (21839).
Ibn Isḥāq also said:
“Al-Ashʿath ibn Qays came to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ with the delegation of Kindah.
“Al-Zuhrī ibn Shihāb informed me that he came to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ with eighty riders from Kindah. They entered the mosque of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ having combed their long hair and applied kohl. They were wearing striped robes whose edges had been trimmed with silk.
“When they entered upon the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ, he said:
‘Have you not embraced Islām?’
“They replied, ‘Yes.’
“He said:
‘Then what is this silk around your necks?’
“So they tore it off and threw it away.”
Sīrat Ibn Hishām (2/585).

9781 — Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“Musaylimah the Liar came during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ, saying, ‘If Muḥammad gives me authority after him, I will follow him.’
“He came with a large number of his people.
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ went to him accompanied by Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās, while the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ held a piece of palm stalk in his hand.
“He stood before Musaylimah among his companions and said:
‘Even if you asked me for this piece, I would not give it to you. You will not escape what Allāh has decreed concerning you. If you turn away, Allāh will surely destroy you. I indeed see you as the one concerning whom I was shown what I was shown. And this is Thābit; he will answer you on my behalf.’
“Then he turned away from him.”
Ibn ʿAbbās said:
“I asked about the statement of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ: ‘I indeed see you as the one concerning whom I was shown what I was shown.’
“Abū Hurayrah informed me that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
‘While I was asleep, I saw two bracelets of gold in my hands, and their matter troubled me. It was revealed to me in the dream that I should blow upon them. I blew upon them and they flew away. I interpreted them as two liars who would emerge after me.’
“One was al-ʿAnsī and the other was Musaylimah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4373, 4374) and Muslim (2273–2274:21).

9782 — Abū Hurayrah reported that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“While I was sleeping, the treasures of the earth were brought to me, and two bracelets of gold were placed in my hands. They became burdensome to me, so Allāh revealed to me that I should blow upon them. I blew upon them and they disappeared. I interpreted them as the two liars between whom I am situated: the man of Ṣanʿāʾ and the man of al-Yamāmah.”
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4375) and Muslim (2274:22).

9783 — Ibn ʿUbaydah ibn Nashīṭ—whose name in another place is given as ʿAbdullāh—reported that ʿUbaydullāh ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿUtbah said:
“We were informed that Musaylimah the Liar came to Madīnah and stayed in the house of the daughter of al-Ḥārith. He was married to the daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Kurayz, who was the mother of ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿĀmir.
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ went to him accompanied by Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās, who was known as the orator of the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ. The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ had a small stick in his hand.
“He stood before Musaylimah and spoke to him.
“Musaylimah said, ‘If you wish, leave the authority between us and yourself, then assign it to us after you.’
“The Prophet ﷺ replied:
‘Even if you asked me for this stick, I would not give it to you. I indeed see you as the one concerning whom I was shown what I was shown. And this is Thābit ibn Qays; he will answer you on my behalf.’
“Then the Prophet ﷺ departed.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (4378).

9784 — Nuʿaym ibn Masʿūd al-Ashjaʿī said:
“I heard the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ say to the two envoys when they read out the letter of Musaylimah the Liar:
‘And what do the two of you say?’
“They replied, ‘We say what he says.’
“The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
‘By Allāh, were it not that messengers are not to be killed, I would strike both of your necks.’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2761), Aḥmad (15989), and al-Tirmidhī in al-ʿIlal al-Kabīr (2/953).

9785 — Wahb ibn Munabbih said:
“I asked Jābir about the matter of Thaqīf when they gave the pledge of allegiance.
“He said:
‘They stipulated to the Prophet ﷺ that no charity would be required of them and that they would not be required to fight in jihād.’
“He later heard the Prophet ﷺ say:
‘They will give charity and fight in jihād once they have truly embraced Islām.’”
Good: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (3025) and Ibn Abī ʿĀṣim in al-Āḥād wa al-Mathānī (1525).

9786 — Al-Sharīd said:
“There was a man afflicted with leprosy among the delegation of Thaqīf.
“The Prophet ﷺ sent word to him:
‘We have accepted your pledge, so return.’”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim in The Book of Salām (2231).

9787 — ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥassān, a man from Banū Kaʿb of Balʿanbar, reported that his two grandmothers, Ṣafiyyah bint ʿUlayyah and Duḥaybah bint ʿUlaybah, narrated to him the account of Qaylah bint Makhramah. They had both been raised in her care, and Qaylah was the maternal grandmother of their father.
Qaylah had been married to Ḥabīb ibn Azhar, a man from Banū Jināb, and she bore him daughters. He died at the beginning of Islām. The girls’ paternal uncle, Athwāb ibn Azhar, then took them away from her. So she set out seeking companionship to reach the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ in the early period of Islām.
One of the young girls, Ḥudaybāʾ, began to cry, and Qaylah took her along. The girl was wearing a woolen wrap. While they were making the camel move quickly, a rabbit suddenly sprang out. Ḥudaybāʾ said: “By Allāh, your standing will always remain above the standing of Athwāb in this affair!” Then a fox crossed their path, and she called it by a name that ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥassān later forgot, and she said about it the same as she had said about the rabbit.
While they were again urging the camel forward, it suddenly knelt down and began trembling. Ḥudaybāʾ said: “By the trust, Athwāb’s seizure has overtaken you!”
Qaylah said: “I was forced to rely upon her, so I said, ‘Woe to you, what should I do?’”
She replied: “Turn your garments inside out, roll your back toward your belly, and overturn the saddle-cloths of your camel.”
The girl removed her woolen wrap and turned it inside out, then rolled herself over. When Qaylah did what she had been told, the camel shook itself, stood up, passed wind, and urinated.
The girl said: “Put your things back as they were.”
Qaylah did so, and they continued quickly. Suddenly Athwāb was running behind them with a drawn sword. They sought refuge in a large enclosure. Qaylah said that she could still picture the place: the camel was brought to the middle section of the tent like a docile camel, and she rushed inside. Athwāb caught up with her and struck with the sword, its edge cutting part of the hair from her head.
He said: “Give me my brother’s daughter, O foul woman!”
She handed the girl to him, and he placed her upon his shoulder and took her away. The girl knew him better than anyone in the household.
Qaylah then went to a sister of hers who was married among Banū Shaybān, still seeking someone who could accompany her to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ.
One night while she was staying with her sister and they thought she was asleep, her sister’s husband returned from an evening gathering and said: “By your father, I have found for Qaylah a trustworthy companion.”
Her sister asked: “Who is he?”
He replied: “Ḥurayth ibn Ḥassān al-Shaybānī. He will leave in the morning as the delegate of Bakr ibn Wāʾil to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ.”
Qaylah heard what they said, went early to her camel, prepared it, and searched for him. She found him not far away and asked to accompany him. He replied: “Yes, with honor.”
Their riding animals were already kneeling and prepared, so she set out with him. She found him to be a trustworthy companion until they reached the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ.
He was leading the people in the dawn prayer. The prayer had begun just as daybreak split, while the stars were still densely visible in the sky, and because of the darkness the men could scarcely recognize one another.
Qaylah joined the row with the men, for she was a woman only recently removed from the customs of Jāhiliyyah. The man beside her asked:
“Are you a woman or a man?”
She replied: “No, rather I am a woman.”
He said: “You have nearly caused me temptation. Pray with the women behind you.”
There was a row of women near the chambers that she had not noticed when she first entered, so she joined them.
When the sun had risen, she moved closer. Whenever she saw a man of impressive appearance, her gaze would rise toward him, thinking perhaps that the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ would stand out above the people.
Eventually, after the sun had risen higher, a man came and said:
“Peace be upon you, O Messenger of Allāh.”
The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ replied:
“And upon you be peace, and the mercy of Allāh, and His blessings.”
Qaylah described the Prophet ﷺ as wearing two worn garments that had once been dyed with saffron but whose color had faded. He had with him a stripped palm branch with only two leaves remaining at its top, and he was sitting with his knees drawn up.
When she saw the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ sitting with such humility, she began trembling with fear.
The man sitting beside him said:
“O Messenger of Allāh, the poor woman is trembling.”
Without turning to look at her, while she was behind him, the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said:
“O poor woman, be calm.”
When the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ said this, Allāh removed the terror that had entered her heart.
Her companion then stepped forward first and pledged allegiance to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ upon Islām, both for himself and on behalf of his people.
Then he said:
“O Messenger of Allāh, write an agreement between us and Banū Tamīm regarding al-Dahnāʾ, so that none of them may cross it toward us except as a traveler or someone temporarily residing.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“O young man, write for him concerning al-Dahnāʾ.”
When Qaylah saw that he had ordered the land to be written over to him, she became alarmed, because it was her homeland and home territory.
She said:
“O Messenger of Allāh, he has not asked you for a fair portion of land. This al-Dahnāʾ may seem to you merely a place where a camel is tethered and sheep are pastured, but beyond it are the women and children of Tamīm!”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“Stop, O young man. The poor woman has spoken the truth. The Muslim is the brother of the Muslim. Water and vegetation are sufficient for them both, and they cooperate against those who cause temptation and misguidance.”
When Ḥurayth saw that the written grant had been prevented, he struck one hand against the other and said:
“You and I have become like the saying: ‘The sheep carries its own death upon its hooves.’”
Qaylah said:
“By Allāh, you were indeed an excellent guide in the darkness, generous with your mount, and honorable toward your female companion until you brought me to the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ. But do not blame me for seeking my own right when you sought yours.”
He said:
“What right do you have in al-Dahnāʾ, may you be bereft?”
She replied:
“The place where my camel is tethered—you seek it for your wife’s camel?”
He said:
“Very well. I call the Messenger of Allāh ﷺ to witness that I will be your brother for as long as I live, since you have praised me before him in this way.”
She replied:
“Since you have begun it, I will not waste that bond.”
The Messenger of Allāh ﷺ then said words concerning her son and her grief. Qaylah wept and said:
“By Allāh, O Messenger of Allāh, I had raised him to be a resolute man. He fought with you on the day of al-Rabadhah, then went to bring me provisions from Khaybar. Its fever afflicted him, and he left the women in my care.”
The Prophet ﷺ said:
“By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, were you not a poor woman, we would have dragged you today upon your face—or I would have dragged you upon your face.” ʿAbdullāh was uncertain about the exact wording.
He then said:
“Is one of you incapable of living honorably with his companion in this world, then when someone with a greater claim over him comes between them, saying: ‘Indeed we belong to Allāh, and to Him we return’?”
Then he said:
“My Lord, console me regarding what You have taken away, and aid me regarding what You have left. By the One in Whose hand is the soul of Muḥammad, one of you may weep until his companion is moved to tears by his weeping. So, servants of Allāh, do not torment your brothers.”
He then wrote for Qaylah and the women who were the daughters of Qaylah upon a piece of red leather:
“They are not to be wronged in any right, nor compelled into marriage. Every believing Muslim is their supporter. Do good and do not do evil.”
Good: Narrated by Ibn Saʿd (1/317–320) and al-Ṭabarānī in al-Kabīr (15/7–10) at this length. Abū Dāwūd (3070, 4847), al-Tirmidhī (2814), and al-Bayhaqī (6/150) narrated it in abbreviated form. Some portions of the report contain unusual wording.
His saying “they were making their camels move quickly” means that they were urging them into rapid travel. The Arabic expression rataka yartiku ratkan wa-ratakānan means to move quickly.
His saying “al-futtān” is transmitted with either ḍammah or fatḥah on the letter fāʾ. With ḍammah, it is the plural of fātin and means those who mislead and tempt people, so each Muslim assists the other against them. With fatḥah, it refers to Satan.

9788 — Laqīṭ ibn Ṣabirah said:
I was the delegate of Banū al-Muntafiq—or was among the delegation of Banū al-Muntafiq—to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. When we arrived, we did not find the Messenger of Allah ﷺ at his home, but we found ʿĀʾishah, the Mother of the Believers. She ordered that khazīrah be prepared for us, and it was prepared. We were also brought a qināʿ—Qutaybah did not say “the qināʿ”—which is a platter containing dates.
The Messenger of Allah ﷺ then arrived and asked:
“Have you been given anything to eat, or has anything been ordered for you?”
We replied:
“Yes, O Messenger of Allah.”
While we were sitting with the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, the shepherd brought the sheep into their resting place, accompanied by a young lamb that was bleating. He ﷺ asked:
“What did it give birth to, so-and-so?”
He replied:
“A ewe lamb.”
He ﷺ said:
“Then slaughter a sheep for us in its place.”
He then said:
“Do not suppose that we slaughtered it specifically because of you. We own one hundred sheep and do not want their number to increase. Whenever the shepherd has a ewe lamb born, we slaughter a sheep in its place.”
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, I have a wife whose tongue contains something”—meaning foul or abusive speech.
He said:
“Then divorce her.”
I said:
“O Messenger of Allah, she has been my companion for a long time, and I have a child by her.”
He said:
“Then instruct her”—meaning admonish her—“for if there is goodness in her, she will comply. Do not beat your wife as you would beat your slave woman.”
I then said:
“O Messenger of Allah, tell me about ablution.”
He said:
“Perform ablution thoroughly, pass your fingers between your fingers and toes, and inhale water deeply into the nose unless you are fasting.”
Authentic: Narrated at length by Abū Dāwūd (142), whose wording is cited here, as well as by al-Tirmidhī (38), al-Nasāʾī (114), and Ibn Mājah (407).
In some narrations, it states:
“When you perform ablution, rinse your mouth.”
Al-Tirmidhī said: “good authentic hadith.”

9789 — Muṭarrif ibn ʿAbdullāh ibn al-Shikhkhīr reported that his father said:
I went as part of the delegation of Banū ʿĀmir to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. We said:
“You are our master.”
He replied:
“The Master is Allah, Blessed and Exalted.”
We said:
“And you are the most excellent of us in virtue and the greatest of us in eminence.”
He said:
“Say what you ordinarily say, or part of it, and do not allow Satan to draw you along.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (4806). It was also narrated by Imām Aḥmad (16311) and al-Bayhaqī in al-Madkhal (537), with the addition: “and the splendid serving bowl.” At its conclusion, he narrated: “Do not allow him to entice you.”
Concerning the phrase “the splendid serving bowl,” Ibn al-Athīr said in al-Nihāyah:
“The Arabs used to call a generous chief who fed others a ‘serving bowl,’ because he would set it out and feed people from it, so he was named after it. ‘Al-gharrāʾ’ means white, indicating that it was filled with fat and grease.”
As for the expression “yastajriyannakum,” with the letter rāʾ doubled, it is derived from the word meaning “to drag.” Al-Sindī said that this wording is authentic.

9790 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Prophet of Allah ﷺ wrote to Khosrow, Caesar, the Negus, and every powerful ruler, inviting them to Allah Most High. This was not the same Negus over whom the Prophet ﷺ performed the funeral prayer.
Another narration adds: “And Ukaydir of Dūmah.”
Authentic: Narrated by Muslim (1774:75) and Ibn Ḥibbān (653–654), who added: “Ukaydir of Dūmah.” Its chain of transmission is authentic.
The title “Negus” was given to every king who ruled Abyssinia. As for al-Aṣḥamah, the ruler who received Jaʿfar and his companions, he embraced Islam, and the Prophet ﷺ performed the funeral prayer for him. He died before the Conquest of Makkah, in the eighth year. The letters that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wrote to the kings, inviting them to Islam, were sent after the death of the Muslim Negus over whom he had performed the funeral prayer. The text of the Prophet’s ﷺ letter to the Negus will appear later.

9791 — Anas ibn Mālik said:
When the Prophet ﷺ intended to write to the Romans, he was told:
“They do not read a letter unless it is sealed.”
He therefore had a silver signet ring made. It is as though I can still see its whiteness upon his hand. Engraved upon it were the words:
“Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah.”
Another narration states: “He intended to write to the non-Arabs.”
A third narration states: “He intended to write to Khosrow, Caesar, and the Negus.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2938) and Muslim (2091:56). Muslim narrated the second wording in (2091:57) and the third wording in (2091:58).

9792 — Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb reported that Heraclius sent for him while he was travelling with a group of Quraysh. They were merchants in Greater Syria during the period in which the Messenger of Allah ﷺ had concluded a truce with Abū Sufyān and the disbelievers of Quraysh.
They came to Heraclius while he was in Aelia—Jerusalem. He summoned them to his court while the Roman dignitaries were gathered around him. He then called for his interpreter and asked:
“Which of you is most closely related to the man who claims to be a prophet?”
Abū Sufyān said:
I replied, “I am the closest of them to him in lineage.”
Heraclius said:
“Bring him close to me, and bring his companions near and place them behind him.”
He then told his interpreter:
“Tell them that I am going to question this man about the one who claims to be a prophet. If he lies to me, they must expose his lie.”
By Allah, had I not been ashamed that they would later report me as a liar, I would have lied about him.
The first thing he asked me was:
“What is his lineage among you?”
I replied:
“He possesses a noble lineage among us.”
He asked:
“Has anyone among you ever made this claim before him?”
I replied:
“No.”
He asked:
“Was any of his forefathers a king?”
I replied:
“No.”
He asked:
“Are the nobles of the people following him, or are their weak following him?”
I replied:
“Rather, their weak are following him.”
He asked:
“Are they increasing or decreasing?”
I replied:
“They are increasing.”
He asked:
“Does anyone among them renounce his religion out of displeasure with it after embracing it?”
I replied:
“No.”
He asked:
“Did you ever accuse him of lying before he made this claim?”
I replied:
“No.”
He asked:
“Does he act treacherously?”
I replied:
“No. We currently have a truce with him, and we do not know what he will do during it.”
Abū Sufyān said:
That was the only statement into which I was able to insert something against him.
Heraclius asked:
“Have you fought against him?”
I replied:
“Yes.”
He asked:
“How has your warfare against him been?”
I replied:
“The war between us and him has alternated; sometimes he gains the advantage over us, and sometimes we gain the advantage over him.”
He asked:
“What does he command you to do?”
I replied:
“He says: ‘Worship Allah alone and associate nothing with Him, and abandon what your forefathers used to say.’ He commands us to pray, give zakāh, speak truthfully, remain chaste, and maintain the ties of kinship.”
Heraclius then said to his interpreter:
“Tell him: I asked you about his lineage, and you stated that he possesses a noble lineage among you. In the same way, messengers are sent from the noble lineages of their people.
“I asked whether anyone among you had made this claim before him, and you said no. I reasoned that had someone made this claim before him, I would have said that he was merely imitating a statement made before him.
“I asked whether any of his forefathers had been a king, and you said no. Had one of his forefathers been a king, I would have said that he was seeking to reclaim the kingdom of his father.
“I asked whether you had accused him of lying before he made this claim, and you said no. I therefore know that a man who would not lie about people would never lie about Allah.
“I asked whether the nobles or the weak among the people followed him, and you stated that their weak followed him. Such are the followers of the messengers.
“I asked whether their numbers were increasing or decreasing, and you stated that they were increasing. Such is the nature of faith until it reaches completion.
“I asked whether anyone renounced his religion out of displeasure with it after embracing it, and you stated that no one did. Such is faith when its delight permeates the heart.
“I asked whether he acted treacherously, and you stated that he did not. Likewise, the messengers do not act treacherously.
“I asked what he commanded you to do, and you stated that he commanded you to worship Allah without associating anything with Him, that he forbade you from worshipping idols, and that he commanded you to pray, speak truthfully, and remain chaste.
“If what you have said is true, he will soon rule the very ground beneath these two feet of mine. I knew that he was about to appear, but I did not think that he would be from among you. If I knew that I could reach him, I would endure every hardship to meet him. If I were with him, I would wash his feet.”
Heraclius then called for the letter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that he had sent with Diḥyah to the ruler of Buṣrā, who had delivered it to Heraclius. Heraclius read it, and it contained:
“In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.
“From Muḥammad, the servant of Allah and His Messenger, to Heraclius, the great ruler of the Romans:
“Peace be upon whoever follows the guidance.
“To proceed: I invite you with the call of Islam. Embrace Islam and you will be safe. Allah will grant you your reward twice. If you turn away, you will bear the sin of the Arīsiyyīn.
“‘O People of the Scripture, come to a word that is equitable between us and you: that we worship none but Allah, associate nothing with Him, and do not take one another as lords besides Allah. But if they turn away, then say, “Bear witness that we are Muslims.”’” [Āl ʿImrān: 64]
Abū Sufyān said:
When Heraclius had finished speaking and the reading of the letter had ended, a great uproar arose around him and voices were raised. We were then expelled.
When we were taken out, I said to my companions:
“The affair of the son of Abū Kabshah has indeed become momentous! Even the king of the Romans fears him.”
From that time onward, I remained certain that he would prevail until Allah brought Islam into my heart.
Ibn al-Nāẓūr, the governor of Aelia, and Heraclius’s bishop over the Christians of Greater Syria, used to relate that when Heraclius arrived in Aelia, he awoke one morning in a disturbed state of mind.
Some of his patriarchs said to him:
“We find your appearance unusual today.”
Ibn al-Nāẓūr said:
Heraclius was skilled in astrology and used to examine the stars. When they questioned him, he said:
“Last night, while examining the stars, I saw that the king of those who practise circumcision had appeared. Who among the people of this age practises circumcision?”
They replied:
“No one practises circumcision except the Jews. Do not let their affair trouble you. Write to the cities of your kingdom, ordering that the Jews living within them be killed.”
While they were deliberating, a man was brought to Heraclius whom the king of Ghassān had sent with news concerning the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.
After Heraclius questioned him, he ordered:
“Go and examine whether he is circumcised.”
They examined him and informed Heraclius that he was circumcised. Heraclius then asked him about the Arabs, and the man replied:
“They practise circumcision.”
Heraclius said:
“The king of this nation has now appeared.”
Heraclius then wrote to a companion of his in Rome who was his equal in knowledge. Heraclius travelled to Ḥimṣ and had scarcely reached it when a letter arrived from his companion agreeing with Heraclius’s conclusion that the Prophet ﷺ had appeared and that he was indeed a prophet.
Heraclius summoned the Roman dignitaries to a palace of his in Ḥimṣ. He then ordered its doors to be locked. Looking down upon them, he said:
“O assembly of Romans! Do you desire success, right guidance, and the preservation of your kingdom? Then pledge allegiance to this Prophet.”
They fled wildly toward the doors like startled wild donkeys, but found them locked.
When Heraclius witnessed their aversion and despaired of their accepting faith, he said:
“Bring them back to me.”
He then told them:
“I made that statement only to test the firmness of your adherence to your religion, and I have now seen it.”
They prostrated themselves before him and became pleased with him. That was the end of the affair of Heraclius.
Agreed upon: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (7) and Muslim (1773:74). The wording cited here is that of al-Bukhārī.
The phrase “mādda fīhā” means that he concluded a peace treaty with them to cease fighting.
The word “bishop” is an Arabized term meaning a Christian scholar or religious leader.
The term “ḥazzāʾ” means a soothsayer who claims to provide information about hidden matters.
Regarding the phrase “the sin of the Arīsiyyīn,” the commentators have not agreed upon its meaning. It occurred to me that the term may be attributed to Arius the Egyptian, who appeared in the third century CE. He taught that the Messiah was a prophet and denied that he was the son of God. His followers were persecuted after Constantine embraced Christianity and adopted the belief that the Messiah was the son of God and divine.
Perhaps Heraclius, the Roman emperor, and some of his close associates followed the doctrine of Arius but concealed it out of fear of losing their authority. The Prophet ﷺ may have been informed of this and therefore told him:
“You will bear the sin of the Arīsiyyīn.”
The word “baṭāriqah” is the plural of “baṭrīq,” referring to the senior officials of his state and the members of his council.
The word “daskarah” means a palace surrounded by—or containing—residences for servants and others like them.

9793 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wrote to Caesar, saying:
“If you turn away, you will bear the sin of the Arīsiyyīn.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2936).

9794 — ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ wrote to Caesar, inviting him to Islam. He sent the letter with Diḥyah al-Kalbī and instructed him to deliver it to the ruler of Buṣrā, who would then deliver it to Caesar.
When Allah had removed the Persian armies from Caesar, he walked from Ḥimṣ to Aelia in gratitude for the favour Allah had granted him.
When the letter of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ reached Caesar and he read it, he said:
“Find someone from this man’s people who is present here so that I may question him concerning the Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī (2940).

9795 — Anas ibn Mālik reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said:
“Who will take this letter of mine to Caesar? He shall have Paradise.”
A man from among the people asked:
“Even if I am not killed?”
He replied:
“Even if you are not killed.”
The man departed with the letter and encountered Caesar as he was approaching Jerusalem. A special carpet had been laid out for Caesar upon which no one else was permitted to walk. The man threw the letter onto the carpet and stepped aside.
When Caesar reached the letter, he picked it up. He then summoned the chief Catholicos and had him read it. The Catholicos said:
