“My knowledge concerning this letter is no greater than yours.”
Caesar then proclaimed:
“Whoever brought this letter shall be granted safety.”
The man came forward, and Caesar said to him:
“When I return, come to me.”
When Caesar returned, the man came to him. Caesar ordered the doors of his palace to be locked. He then instructed a herald to proclaim:
“Behold! Caesar has followed Muḥammad ﷺ and abandoned Christianity!”
His soldiers advanced fully armed until they surrounded his palace.
Caesar said to the messenger of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ:
“You can see that I fear for my kingdom.”
He then instructed a herald to proclaim:
“Behold! Caesar is pleased with you. He merely tested you to see how firmly you would adhere to your religion. You may now return.”
They therefore dispersed.
Caesar wrote to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, claiming:
“I am a Muslim.”
He also sent him some gold dinars. When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ read the letter, he said:
“The enemy of Allah has lied. He is not a Muslim; he remains upon Christianity.”
The Prophet ﷺ then distributed the dinars.
Authentic: Narrated by Ibn Ḥibbān (4504).

9796 — Ibn ʿAbbās reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent his letter to Khosrow with ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥudhāfah al-Sahmī and instructed him to deliver it to the ruler of Bahrain. The ruler of Bahrain then delivered it to Khosrow. When Khosrow read it, he tore it apart.
Al-Zuhrī said: I believe that Ibn al-Musayyib said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ then supplicated against them, asking that they be utterly torn apart.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Military Campaigns (4424).
Ibn Saʿd stated decisively that this occurred in the seventh year, during the truce. However, al-Bukhārī’s arrangement indicates that it occurred in the ninth year.

9797 — Abū Bakrah reported:
Allah benefited me through a statement that I heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ during the days of the Battle of the Camel, after I had nearly joined the army of the Camel and fought alongside them.
When news reached the Messenger of Allah ﷺ that the people of Persia had appointed the daughter of Khosrow as their ruler, he said:
“A people who entrust their affairs to a woman will never prosper.”
Authentic: Narrated by al-Bukhārī in the Book of Military Campaigns (4425).
This occurred when news reached the Prophet ﷺ that Khosrow had been killed and that his daughter had succeeded him. This was narrated by al-Tirmidhī (2262), al-Nasāʾī (5388), and al-Ḥākim (3/118–119), all from the hadith of Abū Bakrah, who said:
“Allah protected me by means of something I had heard from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. When Khosrow perished, he asked:
‘Whom have they appointed as his successor?’
They replied:
‘His daughter.’
The Prophet ﷺ then said:
‘A people who entrust their affairs to a woman will never prosper.’
When ʿĀʾishah arrived—that is, in Basra—I remembered the statement of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, and Allah protected me through it.”
The wording is that of al-Tirmidhī, who said: “This is an authentic hadith.”

9798 — ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd al-Qārī reported that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ sent Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah to al-Muqawqis, the ruler of Alexandria, carrying his letter to him.
Al-Muqawqis kissed the letter, honoured Ḥāṭib, and provided him with gracious hospitality. He then sent him back to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, sending with him as gifts a garment, a grey mule together with its saddle, and two bondwomen. One of them was the mother of Ibrāhīm. The other was given to Jahm ibn Qays al-ʿAbdarī; she was the mother of Zakariyyā ibn Jahm, who served as ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ’s deputy over Egypt.
Authentic: Narrated by al-Ṭaḥāwī in Sharḥ al-Mushkil (2570, 4349) and Ibn ʿAbd al-Ḥakam in al-Futūḥ (p. 64).
The text of the letter was as follows:
“In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.
From Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah, to al-Muqawqis, the great ruler of the Copts:
Peace be upon whoever follows the guidance.
To proceed: I invite you with the call of Islam. Embrace Islam and you will be safe. Embrace Islam, and Allah will grant you your reward twice. If you turn away, you will bear the sin of the Copts.
‘O People of the Scripture, come to a word that is equitable between us and you: that we worship none but Allah, associate nothing with Him, and do not take one another as lords besides Allah. But if they turn away, then say, “Bear witness that we are Muslims.”’” [Āl ʿImrān: 64]
Al-Zubayr ibn Bakkār narrated it, as cited in Muntakhab Kitāb Azwāj al-Nabī ﷺ. He said:
Muḥammad ibn Ḥasan related to me, from Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥah, from Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Ḥassān, from his father; and from Isḥāq ibn Ibrāhīm, from ʿAbdullāh ibn Ḥārithah ibn al-Nuʿmān:
When the Messenger of Allah ﷺ returned from al-Ḥudaybiyah in the sixth year, he dispatched six men, travelling in three pairs: Ḥāṭib ibn Abī Baltaʿah to al-Muqawqis, Shujāʿ ibn Wahb to al-Ḥārith ibn Abī Shamir, and Diḥyah al-Kalbī to Caesar. They travelled until they reached Wādī al-Qurā, whereupon Ḥāṭib took the route to al-Muqawqis carrying a letter from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ containing the foregoing text.
Al-Zaylaʿī also mentioned a similar account from al-Wāqidī in Naṣb al-Rāyah (4/421–422), saying:
Ḥāṭib departed with the letter until he reached Alexandria. When he entered the presence of al-Muqawqis, he said:
“Know that before you there was a man who claimed to be the supreme lord. Allah therefore seized him with an exemplary punishment in the Hereafter and in this world. Allah used him to exact retribution from others and then exacted retribution from him. Therefore, take warning from what happened to others, and do not let others be made to take warning from what happens to you.
“Know that we possess a religion that we will never abandon except for one better than it—and that is Islam, through which Allah has rendered everything else unnecessary. This Prophet called the people, and Quraysh were the fiercest in opposing him, the Jews were the most hostile toward him, and the Christians were the closest to him.
“By my life, Moses’s glad tidings of Jesus were no different from Jesus’s glad tidings of Muḥammad. Our invitation to you to follow the Qur’an is no different from your invitation to the people of the Torah to follow the Gospel. Whenever a prophet reaches a people, they become part of his nation and are obligated to obey him. You are among those whom this Prophet has reached. We are not forbidding you from the religion of the Messiah; rather, we are commanding you to adhere to it.”
Al-Muqawqis replied:
“I have considered the affair of this Prophet. I have found that he does not command that which ought to be shunned, nor forbid that which ought to be desired. I have not found him to be a misguided sorcerer or a lying soothsayer. I have found with him the signs of prophethood: disclosing that which is concealed and informing of secret conversations. I shall give the matter further consideration.”
He took the Prophet’s ﷺ letter, placed it in an ivory container, sealed it, and entrusted it to one of his bondwomen. He then summoned a scribe who could write Arabic and dictated the following reply to the Prophet ﷺ:
“In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful.
To Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh, from al-Muqawqis, the great ruler of the Copts:
Peace be upon you.
To proceed: I have read your letter and understood what you mentioned therein and that to which you invite. I knew that a prophet still remained to appear, but I believed that he would emerge in Greater Syria. I have honoured your messenger and am sending you two bondwomen who hold a distinguished position among the Copts, along with a garment and a mule for you to ride.
Peace be upon you.”
He handed the letter to Ḥāṭib and ordered that he be given one hundred dinars and five garments. He then told him:
“Return to your companion, and do not let the Copts hear a single word from you. The Copts would not obey me if I were to follow him, and I am too possessive of my kingdom to relinquish it. Your companion will prevail over the lands, and after him his Companions will descend upon this very territory of ours. Now depart from me.”
Ḥāṭib said:
“I departed from him after remaining with him for only five days. When I returned to the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, I told him what al-Muqawqis had said to me. He replied:
‘The wicked man clung miserly to his kingdom, but his kingdom will not endure.’”
Some parts of this account contain matters that are unusual.

9799 — Yazīd ibn ʿAbdullāh reported:
We were at al-Mirbad when a man with dishevelled hair arrived, holding a piece of red leather in his hand. We said:
“You appear to be from the desert.”
He replied:
“Yes.”
We said:
“Hand us the piece of leather that you are holding.”
He handed it to us, and we read it. It contained:
“From Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah, to Banū Zuhayr ibn Uqaysh:
If you testify that there is no deity worthy of worship except Allah and that Muḥammad is the Messenger of Allah, establish the prayer, give zakāh, deliver one-fifth of the spoils, along with the Prophet’s ﷺ share and the selected share, then you shall be secure under the protection of Allah and His Messenger.”
We asked:
“Who wrote this document for you?”
He replied:
“The Messenger of Allah ﷺ.”
Authentic: Narrated by Abū Dāwūd (2999) and Aḥmad (20740), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6557).

9800 — Anas reported that the Prophet ﷺ wrote to Bakr ibn Wāʾil:
“From Muḥammad, the Messenger of Allah, to Bakr ibn Wāʾil: Embrace Islam and you will be safe.”
We could find no one able to read it except a man from Banū Ḍubayʿah; consequently, they came to be known as Banū al-Kātib—the descendants of the scribe.
Good: Narrated by al-Bazzār in Kashf al-Astār (1670) and Abū Yaʿlā (2947), and authenticated by Ibn Ḥibbān (6558).
